- **Server fix.** `RegistersAccounts::register()` now wraps user creation, store creation and trial creation in one `DB::transaction()`; mail sends stay outside it (best-effort, never worth rolling back a signup for). `tests/Feature/RegistrationTransactionTest.php` (2 cases) pins both the rollback (a forced failure inside the transaction leaves no user or store row) and the normal commit path.
- **Client fix.** New `withNetworkRetry()` (`client/lib/api/retry-on-network-error.ts`): retries only a thrown error with no `.status` (a true network failure — fetch never got a response), never any HTTP status including 5xx. Wired into the cloud setup/registration flow's four network calls (`app/setup/use-onboarding.ts`'s `register()`/`getStores()`/`getProfile()`, `auth-context.tsx`'s `linkCloudAccount()` → `login()`) — the only place in `client/` with this pattern; day-to-day PIN login is local-only and the sync engine's own retry/backoff is a background queue, not a blocking spinner. `handleRegister()`'s brand-new-account branch also now self-heals a 422 "email already taken": rather than dead-ending, it transparently logs in with the same credentials and continues setup against whatever store the cloud account already has — this also rescues today's pre-existing lost-response failure mode, independent of the new retries. Regression coverage: `client/__tests__/with-network-retry.test.ts` (5 cases) and `client/__tests__/onboarding-register-email-taken-recovers.test.ts` (2 cases — self-heal, and a genuine wrong-password case still surfaces its real error).
- **Reverted mid-fix: queueing `SuperAdminAlertMail`.** The investigation initially recommended queueing the admin-alert email, since two synchronous SMTP sends inside `register()` are the likely source of any timeout a retry would need to recover from. Implemented via `implements ShouldQueue`, then reverted: Laravel's `Mail::to()->send()` auto-detects `ShouldQueue` and silently routes to `queue()` regardless of which method is called. Verified with the user afterward that production's actual `QUEUE_CONNECTION` is `sync` (not `database`, as the repo's own `.env.example`/local `.env` default to), so queueing a mailable there executes it immediately in the same request anyway — no silent loss, but also **no actual speed-up**, since there's no real async queue. The revert stands for a cleaner reason than first assumed: queueing wouldn't have fixed anything here. `laravel-server/AGENTS.md` corrected accordingly (it previously called the five pre-existing `ShouldQueue` mailables "harmless... left in place for a future real worker," which was true in effect but wrong about why, and silently relies on production never matching the repo's own `database` default).
- **Real fix for the slow-registration root cause.** `AdminAlertService::send()` looped `Mail::to($email)->send(...)` per configured admin address — N blocking SMTP round-trips, not one. Changed to a single `Mail::to([...$validEmails])->send(...)` call: one round-trip regardless of admin count, no infra dependency, no queue involved. Logged as `A-125`, fixed the same day. `tests/Feature/AdminAlertServiceTest.php` (3 cases — all admins on one send, invalid addresses dropped without dropping valid ones, nothing sent when unconfigured).
- Docs: `client/AGENTS.md` gains "Cloud setup/registration network calls," covering `withNetworkRetry()`'s scope and why no idempotency key was needed for `/register` itself; `laravel-server/AGENTS.md`'s "Outbound third-party API calls" section corrected on the `ShouldQueue`-is-harmless claim.

### A-28 (ops) — production migration confirmed run; `feedback`/`audit_logs` pushes carrying `occurrence_count` now sync
- **Branch:** none — ops action only, no code change (as the finding's own entry required). Carried from the 2026-09-28/29 passes.
- **What it was.** The migration adding `occurrence_count` to the production `feedback` table existed in the repo but had never been run against production, so any push carrying that column was rejected with `Unknown column 'occurrence_count' in 'field list'`.
- **Resolution.** Confirmed by the user that the pending migration has been run on production. No code was ever at fault — the client-side amplification this used to trigger was already closed by `A-27`.

### A-30 (ops) — production `stores` rows' double-encoded `enabled_payment_methods` confirmed normalised
- **Branch:** none — ops action only, no code change (as the finding's own entry required). Carried from the 2026-09-29 pass, residual cleanup for `A-29`.
- **What it was.** `SyncController::push()`'s now-fixed double-`json_encode()` bug (`A-29`) left some production `stores` rows storing `"[\"cash\",\"card\"]"` (a JSON string) instead of `["cash","card"]` (a JSON array). The `A-29` code fix already heals these on read, so nothing was functionally broken, but the raw bytes stayed malformed until explicitly repaired.
- **Resolution.** Confirmed by the user that the repair (`php artisan tinker` round-trip through the model's `JsonList` cast) has been run against the affected rows in production.

### A-124 (P3) — two more UI surfaces still showed a bulk-import stock correction as a plain "Adjustment," the same gap `A-122` fixed in the Stock Movements table alone
- **Branch:** none — fixed directly on `dev`. Logged while independently verifying `A-122`'s fix.
- **Root cause.** `A-122` routed the general Stock Movements table's label through `resolveMovementDisplayType()`, but two other surfaces still read `movement_type` straight off the row: `stock-movement-details-dialog.tsx` passed `movement.movement_type` directly to `getTypeIconBg`/`getTypeIcon`/`getTypeColor`/`getTypeLabel`, and `use-dashboard-overview.ts`'s recent-activity text built its message off `activity.movement_type` the same way.
- **Fix.** `stock-movement-details-dialog.tsx` now computes `const displayType = resolveMovementDisplayType(movement)` once and routes all four `getType*` calls through it. `use-dashboard-overview.ts` now calls `getTypeLabel(resolveMovementDisplayType(activity))`; this required adding `reference_type`/`reason`/`quantity`/`total_cost` to `DashboardActivity` (`client/lib/types/dashboard-activity.ts`) since the underlying query already selects them but the type never declared them. `resolveMovementDisplayType()`'s parameter type was relaxed from `Pick<StockMovementDbRow, ...>` to an inline type with every field optional/nullable, since `DashboardActivity`'s `movement_type` is optional while `StockMovementDbRow`'s is required — `Pick<>` from the latter rejected the former.
- **`product-history.tsx` needed no change.** Read in full: it never renders the raw movement type as visible text. `isAddition` is derived from direction-based substring checks ("in"/"addition"/"purchase") to pick "Stock Added" vs. "Stock Deducted," and `getIconForAction`/`getColorForAction` both fall "adjustment" and a would-be "import" value through to the same generic default branch either way.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/stock-movement-details-dialog-import-type.test.tsx` (2 cases) — a bulk-import correction (`reference_type: "import"`) renders "Bulk Import" and not "adjustment"; a genuine manual adjustment still renders "adjustment".
- Docs: `client/AGENTS.md`'s note on this surface gap is now resolved — see its `resolveMovementDisplayType()` paragraph.

### A-123 (P3) — a FEFO transfer test hardcoded a batch's expiry date as "soonest, not yet expired," and the wall clock reached that exact date
- **Branch:** none — fixed directly on `dev`. Pre-existing (reproduced unchanged on the pre-session commit `12cb185e`), found while verifying `A-122`'s merge.
- **Root cause.** `client/__tests__/stock-transfers.test.ts`'s FEFO test hardcoded `batches['b1'].expiry_date` as `'2026-10-01'`, intending "soonest-expiring but still valid." `transferStock()`'s FEFO selection excludes a batch whose `expiry_date` is not strictly after today, so once the wall clock reached 2026-10-01 itself, that batch read as already-expired and was excluded from the draw — the transfer pulled everything from `b2` instead, and `b1.quantity` stayed at 5 (untouched) where the test expected 0 (fully drained).
- **Fix.** Both batches' `expiry_date`s are now derived relative to `new Date()` via `date-fns`'s `addDays`/`addMonths`/`formatISO` (`soonestExpiry = addDays(new Date(), 1)`, `laterExpiry = addMonths(new Date(), 6)`) instead of hardcoded calendar strings, and the test's later assertion on the destination batch's `expiry_date` was updated to compare against the same `soonestExpiry` variable rather than a second hardcoded `'2026-10-01'` literal that the first fix pass missed.
- **Regression coverage:** the same test, now passing regardless of wall-clock date — `npx vitest run __tests__/stock-transfers.test.ts` (11/11).
- Docs: none beyond this entry — the fix is confined to a test fixture.

### A-122 (P3) — bulk-import stock corrections still showed as "Adjustment" in the general Stock Movements table, after `A-111` was wrongly retracted as a duplicate of `A-52`
- **Branch:** none — fixed directly on `dev`. Reported live by the user ("I still see the bulk import as adjustments in Stock movements list") after the `A-111`/`A-52` retraction, which turned out to be wrong.
- **Root cause.** `A-52` (2026-09-29) fixed two things, both scoped to the **Adjustments Ledger** tab: `groupAdjustmentMovements()` (`client/components/stock-batch/adjustment-derivations.ts`) now excludes a row whose `reference_type` is `"import"` or whose reason parses to the legacy `"Bulk import stock update"` text, and a server migration retags old-shape rows to match. Neither touch reaches the separate, general **Stock Movements** table (`client/components/stock-batch/stock-movements.tsx`), which has its own `mapMovement()` that read the raw `movement_type` column directly (`type: m.movement_type || "adjustment"`) and never looked at `reference_type` or `reason` at all — confirmed by grep, zero references across `stock-movements.tsx`, `stock-movement-utils.tsx`, the desktop row, the mobile group, the detail modal, or the type filter. A bulk-import correction for an existing/matched product is written with `movement_type: "adjustment"` (`applyStockAdjustmentDelta()` in `client/lib/db/queries/inventory.ts`, so the same FEFO/audit machinery a manual cycle count uses applies to it too), so it was indistinguishable from a manual adjustment on this screen regardless of A-52 or its migration.
- **Fix.** New `resolveMovementDisplayType()` in `client/components/stock-batch/stock-movement-utils.tsx` returns `"import"` instead of the raw `"adjustment"` when `reference_type === PRODUCT_IMPORT_REFERENCE_TYPE` or the parsed reason matches `LEGACY_PRODUCT_IMPORT_REASON` (reusing `adjustment-derivations.ts`'s own `parseAdjustmentReason`, so a trailing free-text note still matches), falling through to the raw `movement_type` for every other row. `mapMovement()` now calls it instead of reading `movement_type` directly. A new `"import"` entry was added to `FILTER_TYPES` and to `getTypeColor`/`getTypeLabel`/`getTypeIcon`/`getTypeIconBg`, labelled **"Bulk Import"** (indigo, to stay visually distinct from the amber "Adjustments" badge) — the exact wording was the user's own call after a first pass ("Import correction") was rejected as the wrong label.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/stock-movement-import-type.test.ts` (7 cases) — a current-shape import correction (`reference_type: "import"`) resolves to `"import"`; a legacy-shape one (`reference_type: "stock_audit"`, the pre-`3b7ce8b8` form) resolves to `"import"` via the reason-prefix fallback, including with a trailing note; a genuine manual adjustment is untouched; every non-adjustment movement type passes through unchanged; a missing `movement_type` still defaults to `"adjustment"`, matching the raw-column fallback it replaces; and the new `"import"` filter option is registered.
- **What this does not change.** The underlying `stock_movements.movement_type` column is still literally `"adjustment"` for these rows — this is a display/filter-layer fix, not a data migration. Nothing in `FEFO`, cost accounting, or sync is affected.
- Docs: this correction, including the fact that the `A-111` retraction was itself wrong, is recorded in `docs/KNOWN_BUGS.md`'s executive summary rather than silently amended.

### A-26 (P2) — a stale device's legitimate second partial receipt collapsed into the first one and nothing told the store the remainder was never booked
- **Branch:** `fix/final-carried-2026-10-01` (worktree), commit `ea1fcc41`. Carried from the 2026-09-28 pass, where it was logged as an accepted limitation of the `A-9` fix rather than a regression in it.
- **Root cause (unchanged, deliberately).** `receiptBatchId`/`receiptMovementId` key a receipt's `stock_batches` and `stock_movements` rows on the PO line plus the balance already received against it. A device that has not pulled since another device received against the same line computes a start balance of 0 and so derives the *same* ids for a genuine second partial receipt; `SyncController::push()` turns its INSERTs into UPDATEs of the first receipt's rows (dropping the quantity, contributing no movement delta) and drops its own `quantity_received` UPDATE as a terminal `version_conflict`. **The keying was not changed** — it is what prevents the far worse phantom doubling `A-9` fixed, and the entry's own ruling recorded losing an un-synced second receipt as strictly better. What was wrong was that nothing told anyone: stock and the PO stayed consistent with each other while real delivered goods existed on the shelf and not in the system, and the receiving screen showed the line as having an ordinary outstanding balance.
- **Fix — surface the loss, don't prevent it.** A terminal push conflict previously left nothing behind but a transient `toast.warning`. A new local-only `_sync_conflicts` table (`table_name`, `record_id`, `reason`, the dropped payload's non-`_` field names, `detected_at`, `resolved_at`) plus `lib/db/sync-engine/conflict-log.ts` now record every terminal conflict on an allow-listed table, written inside `push.ts`'s existing per-batch transaction. `getDroppedReceiptSignal(poId)` (`lib/db/queries/procurement.ts`) reports any unresolved drop that touched `quantity_received` against that order's lines, and `ReceivePOPanel` renders a destructive alert above the receiving form — "A receipt against this order may not have been applied", with what to do (count the shelf, receive the remainder) and an "I've checked this" dismissal. A conflict whose recorded field list is empty is treated as possibly a receipt rather than hidden; failing toward the warning is the right direction when the cost of missing it is unbooked stock.
- **Three ways a signal resolves,** so it can't become permanent furniture: the store dismisses it, a *later* change to the same record is accepted by the server (whatever the dropped one carried has been superseded — done from `markSynced()`'s own path), or a `resetDatabase()` wipe (`_sync_conflicts` is in `LOCAL_WIPE_TABLES`).
- **Deliberately bounded.** `CONFLICT_LOGGED_TABLES` is an allow-list of exactly one table, `purchase_order_items`. A general ledger of every terminal conflict would grow without anything reading most of it; the rule recorded in `client/AGENTS.md` is to extend the allow-list only together with a surface that reads the new table's rows. `_sync_conflicts` is local-only — not in `SYNC_CONFIG`, so `npm run test:schema` ignores it and no Laravel migration corresponds to it.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/po-dropped-receipt-signal.test.ts` (10 cases — per-order scoping, distinct-line counting, the `quantity_received` field filter, both resolution paths, the allow-list, the empty-field-list fallback) and `client/__tests__/receive-po-dropped-receipt-banner.test.tsx` (4 cases — absence, wording, pluralisation, dismissal). `client/__tests__/schema-indexes.test.ts` was updated: its legacy-database case drops every A-6 index and asserted only `idx_stock_batches_product_id` survived, which is now a two-entry `SCHEMA_OWNED_INDEXES` list including the new `idx_sync_conflicts_unresolved`. `receive-item-card-price-fields.test.tsx` now mocks the new hook — rendering `ReceivePOPanel` reaches the local database, which under vitest tries to fetch `/sql-wasm.wasm`.
- **What is still a known gap.** The link from a real two-device stale receipt to the `version_conflict` the ledger records is verified by reading `push.ts`, not by a test — the server half can't be exercised locally. The banner itself is unit-tested against a mocked signal, so a browser smoke test (insert a `_sync_conflicts` row by hand and reopen the panel, or run the two-device flow) is still worth doing before relying on it in production. The signal is also per-PO only: there is no global "sync health" surface listing outstanding dropped changes, and no signal at all on the PO list or details screens — someone who never opens the receiving panel for that order will not see it.
- Docs: `client/AGENTS.md` gains "The terminal-conflict ledger" under "Push details", covering why it exists, the allow-list rule, the resolution paths, and exactly what is and isn't tested.

### P3-2 (P3) — a long-open tab could 404 on a lazy chunk after a deploy pruned the old build's assets
- **Branch:** `fix/final-carried-2026-10-01` (worktree), commit `db12c9dc`. Carried from the 2026-09-26 pass, intentionally skipped then as an accepted tradeoff "needing deploy-asset retention to close fully".
- **Root cause.** `public/sw.js`'s `activate()` deletes every cache entry missing from the current build's `precache-manifest.json`. That prune is necessary (`CACHE_VERSION` is deliberately not bumped per deploy, so without it every deploy's newly hashed chunks accumulate until iOS evicts the whole origin) but it is also what broke a tab left open across a deploy: that tab still runs the previous build's JS, and the moment it lazy-loads a route it doesn't already hold, the chunk is gone from the cache *and* from the server. On this static-export host it doesn't even 404 — the fallback returns `index.html` with `200 OK`, which the browser tries to execute as a module (the `Unexpected token '<'` shape `lib/utils/chunk-error.ts` already recognises).
- **Fix — two-generation retention in the service worker, not in the deploy pipeline.** `computeCachePrunePlan(cachedPathnames, currentUrls, previouslyRetained)` holds the immediately-previous build's hashed `/_next/static/**.{js,css}` back for exactly one more deploy cycle, writes the list of what it kept into the cache under `/__sw_retained_chunks.json`, and reads that record on the next activation to delete anything now two generations behind. Storage growth is bounded at roughly two builds' chunks and self-prunes, with no deploy or hosting change. Retention is limited to hashed JS/CSS on purpose: nothing else changes filename per build (so nothing else accumulates), and an HTML document is keyed on its pathname — a new build overwrites it — so retaining an old copy would only risk serving a previous deploy's shell. The bookkeeping record's own key is excluded from both prune and retention.
- **Why not the deploy-asset retention the finding proposed.** Both `deploy-client.yml` and `deploy-dev.yml` publish via `FTP-Deploy-Action`, which mirrors `out/` and deletes server-side files by diffing its own remote state file. It has no supported "keep the last N builds" mode, so retaining assets there would mean hand-rolling an FTP sync and verifying it against the shared host's real behaviour — not reachable or testable from the repo. The service-worker approach closes the same gap for every SW-controlled tab and is verifiable here.
- **What is still a known gap.** A tab that is not yet SW-controlled (the very first visit, before registration completes) still goes to the network and can hit the removed chunk; `chunk-error.ts`'s one-time auto-reload remains the backstop for exactly that case, and was left as-is. Retention is one generation deep, so a tab open across *two* deploys is still exposed. Tauri is unaffected — it loads `out/` off disk and never goes through this worker.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/sw-cache-retention.test.ts` — 7 cases over the policy function (current-manifest entries kept, previous build's chunks retained, a once-retained chunk deleted on the next cycle, non-chunk assets and stale HTML never retained, the bookkeeping key excluded, duplicate pathnames reported once, the record emptied when nothing is left to hold). `public/sw.js` is a standalone script, so the test evaluates it against a stub `self` and reads the policy off `self.__swInternals`. The cache plumbing around it (`cache.keys()`/`delete`/`put`) is not unit-tested and needs a browser check.
- Docs: `client/AGENTS.md`'s PWA section gains "`activate()`'s prune keeps two generations of chunks", including why the deploy-pipeline route was rejected and what residual exposure remains.

### A-121 (P3) — three `finance-reports.test.ts` fixtures dated rows with a UTC instant, flaking once a month at the local-midnight boundary
- **Branch:** `fix/final-carried-2026-10-01` (worktree), commit `b3d84e9e`. Logged on 2026-09-30 while verifying that pass's merge, and confirmed pre-existing on the pre-sweep baseline.
- **Root cause.** The suite runs under `Africa/Lagos` (UTC+1) and the queries under test bucket by the **local** calendar (`strftime(..., 'localtime')`, and `currentMonthWindow()`'s bounds built from local year/month), but `todayISO()` returned a full `new Date().toISOString()` — a UTC instant. For the ~1 hour after local midnight on the 1st of a month, UTC is still on the previous month, so a fixture row dated that way landed in the wrong month and every assertion built on it read `undefined`/`0`. **Reproduced live during this fix** at `2026-09-30 23:32 UTC` / `2026-10-01 00:32 WAT`: exactly the three named tests failed, and passed after the change at the same clock position.
- **Fix.** Three helpers alongside the existing `firstDayOfMonthLocal()`: `todayLocal()` (bare `YYYY-MM-DD`, local), `todayLocalMonth()` (`YYYY-MM`, for an expected month key) and `todayLocalStartISO()` (local midnight today as the UTC instant it really is, for full-timestamp columns). The two expenses fixtures use `todayLocal()` for `expenses.date`, which is a bare-date column anyway. The `rawMonthlyReturns` fixture uses `todayLocalStartISO()` for `sales.transaction_date`/`returns.created_at` and `todayLocalMonth()` for the expected key.
- **One subtlety worth recording.** A noon-UTC anchor (`${todayLocal()}T12:00:00Z`) was tried first and is wrong: `getAdvancedMonthlySalesData()` caps its upper bound at `new Date()`, so just after local midnight that timestamp is in the *future* and the row is excluded by `created_at <= to`. A fixture timestamp has to be both on the right local day and in the past, which local midnight today always is.
- **Regression coverage:** a new `describe("fixture dates stay on the local calendar day (A-121)")` block pins all three helpers with fake timers frozen at the boundary instant `2026-09-30T23:11Z`, plus two query-level cases (a today-dated expense stays inside `currentMonthWindow()`, a today-dated return stays in the local month bucket) at that same clock position — so the regression is now caught at any wall-clock time, not only in the monthly window where it used to surface.
- Docs: `client/AGENTS.md`'s "Tests pin the timezone" section now also covers fixture-value derivation, listing the three helpers and when to use which.

### A-120 (P3) — the low-stock alerts list scoped batches through `products.store_id` only, so a cross-store batch inflated its on-hand figure
- **Branch:** `fix/final-carried-2026-10-01` (worktree), commit `60d935cf`. Logged on 2026-09-30 as the one residual difference the `A-53` fix deliberately did not close.
- **Root cause.** `getLowStockAlerts()`'s `LEFT JOIN stock_batches inv` filtered `inv._deleted`, `inv.is_active` and expiry but never `inv.store_id`; the active store was applied to `products` only. `getStockBatchStats()`'s batch subquery — the source of the card directly above that list — scopes `stock_batches.store_id` itself. So a batch attributed to another store (or a legacy row with no `store_id`) hanging off one of this store's products was summed into the list's on-hand quantity but not the card's.
- **Fix.** `AND inv.store_id = ?` added to the join when a store is active, with the second `storeId` bound. **Strict equality, no `IS NULL` fallback** — a deliberate departure from the finding's suggested `(inv.store_id = ? OR inv.store_id IS NULL)`: the stats subquery is strict, so admitting `store_id`-less batches on one side only would recreate exactly the divergence being closed. A legacy `store_id`-less batch is now excluded from both the card and the list while a store is active.
- **Regression coverage:** three new cases in `client/__tests__/inventory-stock-alerts.test.ts` (its `seedBatch` helper gained a `storeId` option) — another store's batch is ignored, a `store_id`-less batch is ignored and the list agrees with the card, and an agreement case over mixed batch attribution. All three were verified RED before the query change.
- Docs: `client/AGENTS.md`'s "Low-stock card and its list" note, which previously named this as the one open divergence, now records the fix and why the scoping is strict.

### P2-1 (ops) — `FLUTTERWAVE_SECRET_HASH` confirmed set in production
- **Branch:** none — ops action only, no code change (as the finding's own entry required). Carried from the 2026-09-26 pass, where it was intentionally skipped pending confirmation.
- **What it was.** The Flutterwave webhook handler (`app/Http/Controllers/Api/Web/PaymentController.php`) fails closed (500, webhook rejected) if `FLUTTERWAVE_SECRET_HASH` is empty. The variable was documented in `.env.example` but production `.env` state couldn't be verified from the repo, so every Flutterwave subscription payment risked silently failing to activate.
- **Resolution.** Confirmed by the user that `FLUTTERWAVE_SECRET_HASH` is set in production. No code was ever at fault — the handler's fail-closed behaviour was correct throughout.

## 2026-09-30

### A-53 (P3) — `getLowStockAlerts()` had no `is_active` filter, so the dashboard's low-stock drill-down list could disagree with the card above it
- **Branch:** `fix/carried-client-2026-09-30` (worktree), commit `7b07dda8`. Carried from the 2026-09-30 offline-assistant final review pass, where it was logged and deliberately deferred as out of that pass's scope.
- **Root cause.** `getLowStockAlerts()` (`client/lib/db/queries/inventory.ts`) filtered `products` on `_deleted` only, while its sibling `getStockBatchStats()` — the source of the card sitting directly above that list — requires `p.is_active = 1` for both `low_stock_count` and `critical_stock_count`. The two answered "which products are low on stock" over different populations, so a card reading "2 low on stock" could sit above five names, three of them deactivated products nobody intends to reorder.
- **Fix.** `AND (m.is_active = 1 OR m.is_active IS NULL)` added to the query's `WHERE` (`m` is its alias for `products`), matching the stats query's population. The `IS NULL` arm is deliberate: `is_active` is a runtime-migrated column, and a legacy row that never recorded it must keep behaving as active rather than vanishing from the alerts.
- **The assistant's workaround was reviewed and kept, with its justification narrowed.** `lib/assistant/tools/inventory-tools.ts` phrases the reply "Low-stock alerts include: …" rather than "Top low-stock items (of N): …", which `A-53` had cited as its mitigation. That phrasing is still correct and still necessary, but for a different reason: `getLowStockAlerts()` is `LIMIT 5`, so the named list remains a *sample* of the count even now that both queries agree on the population. No code changed there; the doc note in `client/AGENTS.md` that justified it by the `is_active` divergence was rewritten to say so, and the test that pinned the old behaviour (`assistant-inventory-tools.test.ts`, "does not present the named list as a subset of the count when a deactivated product is in it") was inverted — it now asserts the deactivated product is absent from both the count and the list.
- **Regression coverage:** three new cases in `client/__tests__/inventory-stock-alerts.test.ts` — a deactivated product is excluded, a product whose `is_active` was never recorded is still flagged, and an agreement case asserting the list's length equals `low_stock_count + critical_stock_count` over a mixed seed. The first and third were verified RED before the query change.
- **One residual difference was found and left open as `A-120`:** the stats query scopes `stock_batches.store_id` directly while the alerts list scopes batches only through `products.store_id`, so a cross-store or `store_id`-less batch hanging off this store's product still inflates the list's on-hand figure. Display-only and rare; logged in `docs/KNOWN_BUGS.md` rather than folded into this fix, whose scope was the product-level population.
- Docs: `client/AGENTS.md` gains a "Low-stock card and its list" note stating the shared-population contract, and its claim that `products.is_active` is read in exactly one place was corrected to two.

### A-49 (P3) — an adjustment spanning midnight could fall outside a ledger date-range filter that should include it
- **Branch:** `fix/carried-client-2026-09-30` (worktree), commit `d5c807dd`. Carried from the `c384ca47..6218e1ed` review (`A-42`…`A-49`), deferred at the time as display-only.
- **Root cause.** `groupAdjustmentMovements()` (`client/components/stock-batch/adjustment-derivations.ts`) collapsed a `reference_id` group to one representative date — the *latest* movement's — and `filterAdjustmentGroups()` compared only that date's `slice(0, 10)` against `from`/`to`. A group whose movements straddle midnight (a long cycle count, a slow write batch) was therefore filtered as though it happened entirely on its last day, and was invisible to a range ending on the day it started.
- **Fix.** A group now carries its whole interval: `startDate` (earliest movement) and `endDate` (latest) alongside the unchanged display/sort `date`, which is `endDate`. `filterAdjustmentGroups()` keeps a group when `[startDate, endDate]` overlaps `[from, to]` — `endDate >= from` and `startDate <= to` — instead of testing one point. Day comparison stays a raw ISO `slice(0, 10)` on both ends, i.e. UTC days, exactly what it always compared; an undated group behaves as before.
- **Scope note worth keeping.** The ledger UI (`stock-adjustments-ledger.tsx`) does not currently pass `from`/`to` to this helper at all — its date-range picker is applied at the SQL level by `getStockMovements({ from, to })`, which windows individual `stock_movements` rows. So the *visible* symptom of a straddling group in the shipped UI is a group that appears with a partial net quantity, not one that disappears; the helper's `from`/`to` path is reached only by its tests today. The fix makes the helper correct for the moment a caller does use it (and for the ledger's own in-memory filtering, which is where a date filter would naturally move), rather than leaving a trap in it.
- **Regression coverage:** four new cases in `client/__tests__/adjustment-derivations.test.ts` — the group carries both interval ends with `date === endDate`; a midnight-straddling group matches a range ending on its start day and a range starting on its end day; and it still stays out of a range touching neither day. Two were verified RED before the change.
- Docs: `client/AGENTS.md`'s Adjustments-ledger section documents the interval contract and the UTC-day comparison.

### A-48 (P3) — six table/card layouts switched to their desktop layout at 768px, below the project's 1024px nav breakpoint
- **Branch:** `fix/carried-client-2026-09-30` (worktree), commit `ae1b9189`. Carried from the `c384ca47..6218e1ed` review, deferred at the time because fixing only the newly added file would have made it inconsistent with its neighbours — this is the coordinated sweep that entry asked for.
- **Root cause.** `MobileBottomNav` (`client/components/dashboard/mobile-bottom-nav.tsx`) is `lg:hidden`, so the project's nav-chrome breakpoint is `1024px`. Six list components branched between a wide desktop table and a mobile card list at `768px`, so a 768–1023px device showed the mobile bottom nav *and* the desktop table at once.
- **Fix, all six in one pass:** `components/stock-batch/stock-adjustments-ledger.tsx`, `components/stock-batch/stock-movements.tsx`, `components/stock-batch/supplier-table.tsx`, `components/expenses/expense-list.tsx`, `components/customers/activity-tab.tsx` and `components/procurement/purchase-order-table.tsx` now all use `(min-width: 1024px)`. The original finding listed three of these; the other three were found by a later sweep and never folded in, so all six moved together. `1024px` was already the established figure for this kind of switch — `customers/directory-tab.tsx`, `products/product-database.tsx` and `ui/responsive-detail-panel.tsx` were on it before this change.
- **Deliberately not touched.** Dialog- and toast-presentation breakpoints are a separate decision and stay at `768px`: `ui/responsive-modal.tsx`, `ui/sonner.tsx`, `ui/date-range-picker.tsx` and the assistant panel's Sheet-vs-Drawer split. The adjustment/PO item-builder lists stay at `640px`. No paired `md:hidden`/`md:block` utility class was tied to any of the six — both branches are conditionally rendered — so nothing else needed to move with them.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/responsive-table-breakpoint-convention.test.ts` reads the six sources plus the nav's own `lg:hidden` class and fails on any `(min-width: …)` in them that isn't `1024px`. RED first: six of its seven cases failed against the pre-fix tree. A source-scanning test rather than a render test on purpose — the drift is a constant, and pinning it where it is written is what stops a seventh file appearing at `768px`.
- Docs: `client/AGENTS.md`'s UI-conventions section now states the `1024px` rule, names the guard test, and lists the presentation breakpoints that deliberately differ.

### A-41 (P3) — restoring an archived store brought it back still suspended with no signal to the admin
- **Branch:** `fix/carried-admin-2026-09-30` (worktree), commit `598b2061`. Carried from the 2026-09-29 admin store-management review.
- **Root cause.** Archiving never touches `stores.status`, so a store suspended before it was archived comes back suspended — and `CheckAccountStatus` (which resolves the store `withTrashed()`) keeps 403-ing its owner and staff. `restoreStore()` returned nothing about that, the endpoint answered a bare "Store restored successfully", and the fleet page's toast said the store "is active again". The admin's next signal was a support ticket from an owner who still could not sign in.
- **Fix.** `AdminStoreDeletionService::restoreWarnings()` returns `was_suspended` / `suspension_reason` / an admin-facing `warning` string; `AdminStoreDeletionController::restoreStore()` spreads it into the 200 response (documented in the route's OpenAPI attribute), the `STORE_RESTORED` activity row repeats the suspension and its reason in its description, and `web/hooks/use-store-deletion-actions.ts` shows a warning toast naming the reason instead of "is active again" when `was_suspended` is true. Restoring deliberately does **not** unsuspend: that stays a separate, explicit admin action.
- **Gap (2) — the speculative uniqueness collision — is closed as a documented constraint, not code.** `stores.store_slug` and `stores.device_id` are DB-unique across archived rows (the same property `A-93` relies on), so a restore-time collision with a store created while the original was archived cannot happen today and a check for it would be dead code. `docs/ADMIN_STORE_LIFECYCLE.md` and `laravel-server/AGENTS.md` now carry a standing note that making either index soft-delete-aware requires adding that collision check to `restoreStore()` in the same change.
- **Tests:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/Admin/AdminStoreLifecycleAuditTest.php` — a store suspended before archival restores with `was_suspended`, the reason and a non-null `warning`, and its `STORE_RESTORED` description names the suspension; an unsuspended store restores with `was_suspended: false` and no warning. Both RED before the fix.

### A-40 (P3) — the `STORE_PURGED` audit row was written after the purge had already committed, and archive/restore rows had no `store_id`
- **Branch:** `fix/carried-admin-2026-09-30` (worktree), commit `c0a9ca8d`. Carried from the 2026-09-29 admin store-management review.
- **Root cause.** `AdminStoreDeletionService::purgeStore()` wrote its `ActivityLog` row *after* the purge's `DB::transaction()` closure returned, so any failure of that one insert (a constraint, a full disk, a dropped connection) left a store and every record scoped to it irreversibly deleted with no record of who did it or why — the one action in the whole admin surface that cannot be undone. Separately, the `STORE_ARCHIVED`/`STORE_RESTORED` rows never set `store_id`, so `/admin/activity?store_id=` — the store's own history — never showed that the store had been archived or restored; only the global admin feed did.
- **Fix.** The purge's deletions moved into a private `purgeRows()` and the audit-log write became the last statement of the transaction that calls it. The split is what makes the move possible at all: the deletion body has an early `return` for the no-users case, which would have skipped an inline log write. `Schema::withoutForeignKeyConstraints()` still wraps the transaction rather than sitting inside it (SQLite ignores `PRAGMA foreign_keys` mid-transaction). Both archive and restore rows now carry `store_id`.
- **Tests:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/Admin/AdminStoreLifecycleAuditTest.php` (new) — an `ActivityLog::created` listener that throws on `STORE_PURGED` leaves the store and its owner intact (RED before the fix: the store was gone), a successful purge still records its row, and the archive/restore rows are asserted against `store_id`.

### PG-1 — storefront checkout silently fell back to Flutterwave, dropping the store's payout subaccount and forcing NGN onto a charge nothing could then verify or refund
- **Branch:** `fix/pg-a-2026-09-30` (worktree), commit `e0c4731e`. From the 2026-09-27 payment-gateway pass, deferred until now.
- **Root cause.** `PaymentService::initializeTransaction()` was shared by subscriptions and storefront checkout, and with both gateways enabled (the default) any non-2xx from Paystack's initialize silently retried on `initializeFlutterwave()` — which takes no `$subaccount` and hardcodes `'currency' => 'NGN'`. The resulting intent carried `provider = 'flutterwave'` while `StorefrontController::checkout()`'s verify and `refundUnfulfillableCheckout()` both passed a literal `'paystack'`, so that charge could never be verified or refunded. A transient Paystack 5xx therefore took a customer's money in the wrong currency into the platform's own Flutterwave balance, with no payout split and no way back.
- **Fix.** New `PaymentService::initializeStorefrontTransaction()` — Paystack only, no cross-gateway fallback, throwing if an admin has disabled Paystack — is what `initializeCheckout()` now calls. `checkout()`'s verify and both provider calls in `refundUnfulfillableCheckout()` read `$intent->provider`. The **subscription** fallback is deliberately untouched: it is intentional existing behaviour for a flow that carries no subaccount or per-store currency, and a test now pins it so it isn't removed by accident.
- **Regression coverage:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/StorefrontProviderPinningTest.php` — a Paystack 503 at initialize sends nothing to Flutterwave and writes no intent (RED before: it returned 200 with a Flutterwave checkout URL); the subscription path still falls back; and a confirmation against an intent recorded as `flutterwave` verifies at Flutterwave rather than Paystack (RED before: 422).

### PG-2 — storefront payments had no webhook handler and no reconciliation sweep, so confirmation depended entirely on the customer's browser returning
- **Branch:** `fix/pg-a-2026-09-30` (worktree), commit `04fbd924`. From the 2026-09-27 payment-gateway pass.
- **Root cause.** `PaymentController::processSuccessfulPayment()` only ever looked a webhook reference up in `payment_transactions`. A storefront charge lives in `storefront_payment_intents` (deliberately — see that table's migration), so a genuine signed Paystack `charge.success` for one matched nothing and was silently discarded, and no scheduled command swept stale `pending` intents. The only confirmation path was the customer's own browser coming back with intact `sessionStorage`; close the tab, switch device or clear site data and the payment settled with no order, no refund and no record. `A-95`'s "failed confirmation" retry panel and the orphan-reference panel help a customer who *does* come back — they do nothing for one who doesn't, which is the half this fixes.
- **Fix.** Two halves, both in `App\Services\Storefront\StorefrontPaymentReconciler`. (1) The webhook handler now matches `storefront_payment_intents` **before** `payment_transactions` and, on a matching amount/currency, marks the intent `paid` under a row lock; a mismatch is refunded and alerted. (2) `storefront:sweep-payment-intents` (`App\Console\Commands\SweepStorefrontPaymentIntents`, hourly, window from `payment.storefront_intent_stale_minutes`, default 60) re-verifies every `pending` intent past the window — confirmed → `paid` + alert, provider says not-successful → `abandoned`, provider *unreachable* → left strictly alone — and then alerts once per `paid` intent no order ever claimed, stamped `reconciliation_alerted_at` (new nullable column, `2026_09_30_000001`; the table is server-only and not synced). `pending` and `paid` are both claimable (`StorefrontPaymentIntent::CLAIMABLE_STATUSES`), so the genuine return flow still places the order whenever it arrives.
- **Deliberately not done: the webhook does not create the order.** `online_orders.customer_name`/`customer_phone` are NOT NULL and only the return-flow POST carries them. Fabricating them would hand the store an order it cannot act on *and* burn the reference, so the real confirmation would then be rejected as already used. Marking `paid` keeps the money durable and the reference claimable. The order-creation logic was therefore not extracted into a shared service — no second caller needs it. Revisit if contact details ever move onto the intent at initialize time.
- **Also deliberate:** a stale intent is never auto-refunded. The customer may still be mid-return, and choosing between "contact them to finish the order" and "refund" is a human call.
- **Regression coverage:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/StorefrontPaymentReconciliationTest.php` (10 tests) — webhook marks `paid` without touching subscriptions, refunds and alerts an underpayment, never disturbs a `consumed` intent; the return flow still places the order after the webhook; the sweep ignores a fresh intent, promotes and alerts a stale confirmed one, abandons an unpaid one, leaves an unreachable-provider one alone, and alerts exactly once for an unclaimed `paid` intent across two runs.

### PG-3 — an under-paying or wrong-currency subscription payment kept the money with no refund and no operator alert
- **Branch:** `fix/pg-a-2026-09-30` (worktree), commit `4142fb3a`. From the 2026-09-27 payment-gateway pass.
- **Root cause.** Both observers of an amount/currency mismatch — `PaymentController::processSuccessfulPayment()`'s webhook branch and `SubscriptionController::verifyPayment()`'s — did nothing but `Log::warning` and `status = 'failed'`. The provider had confirmed the money moved, so the outcome was a customer charged for a subscription they never got, with no refund attempt and nothing that would put it in front of a human.
- **Fix.** New `App\Services\Payment\PaymentMismatchHandler`, called from both. It re-reads the transaction and bails if a concurrent observer already activated it (never refund a charge that bought a live subscription), attempts a full refund through `PaymentService::refundTransaction()`, records the outcome under `metadata.mismatch_refund`, calls the existing `failTransaction()` so credit/coupon reservations still release, and fires an `AdminAlertService` alert **either way** so a failed refund is still chased by hand. Per A-110 the refund (third-party HTTP) and the alert (synchronous mail) both happen outside `failTransaction()`'s transaction. A verification that merely answers "not successful" is *not* a mismatch and still goes straight to `failTransaction()` unrefunded.
- **Regression coverage:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/PaymentMismatchRefundAlertTest.php` — underpayment and wrong-currency webhooks are refunded and alerted, a failed refund still alerts and is recorded as failed, the verify path behaves identically, and a matching payment is never refunded. `PaymentWebhookTest` gained an `Http::fake()` in `setUp` because its mismatch tests now reach a provider.

### PG-4 — storefront checkout displayed ₦ regardless of the store's actual currency
- **Branch:** `fix/pg-a-2026-09-30` (worktree), commit `317c0885`. From the 2026-09-27 payment-gateway pass.
- **Root cause.** `StorefrontController::show()` omitted `currency` from its payload entirely, and `web/components/storefront/checkout-form.tsx` carried three hardcoded `₦` literals. A Ghana or Kenya store was correctly charged in its own currency (that half was already right — `storeCurrency()`) but told its customer the total in naira.
- **Fix.** `show()` returns `store.currency` from `storeCurrency()`. `useCartRepricing` surfaces it, and the checkout form formats every amount through the new `web/lib/utils/currency.ts` `formatMoney(amount, currency)`, which uses `Intl` `narrowSymbol` and degrades to the raw ISO code rather than guessing (KES renders `KES`, GHS `GH₵`). `currency` is `null` until the catalog call resolves and `formatMoney` falls back to NGN for that window; the submit button is disabled while `pricesLoading`, so this is a brief summary-only flash.
- **Scoped deliberately:** the rest of `web/`'s hardcoded naira (the `referrals-*`/`plan-tier-card`/`subscription-config-tab` per-file `naira()` helpers, `revenue-overview.tsx`, `lib/constants/subscription-plans.ts`) is platform billing and genuinely NGN. `storefront-cart.tsx` and `product-card.tsx` have the same bug as PG-4 and are noted in `web/AGENTS.md` for whenever those views are next touched — not unified here to keep the money-path diff small.
- **Regression coverage:** `web/__tests__/checkout-form-currency.test.tsx` (`formatMoney` unit cases plus the form rendering `KES` with no `₦` anywhere, and falling back to `₦` when the storefront reports no currency) and two `StorefrontControllerTest` cases for `show()`'s `currency` and its platform fallback.

### PG-5 — the bank-account resolve endpoint was an unbounded name-lookup oracle
- **Branch:** `fix/pg-b-2026-09-30` (worktree), commit `fbd0a7ef`. From the 2026-09-27 payment-gateway pass.
- **Root cause.** `StorePaymentAccountController::resolvePaymentAccount()` checks ownership of the *store*, never of the account being looked up: `account_number` and `bank_code` are free-form strings passed straight to Paystack's `GET /bank/resolve` under the platform's own credentials. On the authenticated group's shared `throttle:60,1`, any store owner had 60 free account-number-to-holder-name lookups a minute — a bank-account name lookup service running on DumosRx's Paystack account.
- **Fix.** A dedicated `throttle:bank-account-resolve` limiter on that route alone: **8/min, keyed on the user id rather than the IP** (an IP key lets one account rotate through proxies, and would make NATed owners share a budget). Eight is comfortably above real onboarding — resolve, fix a typo, resolve again — and far below anything usable for enumeration. The general `throttle:60,1` on the group still applies on top.
- **Regression coverage:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/PaymentRouteThrottleTest.php` (new file, deliberately does **not** disable `ThrottleRequests`, same as `StorefrontThrottleTest`) — asserts the route carries the named limiter and that it actually 429s well under 60 attempts. RED before the fix (no such limiter existed; the loop ran to 40 without a 429).

### PG-6 — `checkout()` accepted and permanently burned a `paystack_reference` on a non-Paystack order
- **Branch:** `fix/pg-b-2026-09-30` (worktree), commit `57e0ccf2`. From the 2026-09-27 payment-gateway pass.
- **Root cause.** Two correct decisions combined into a hole. `online_orders.paystack_reference` is unique across *every* payment method, and `checkout()`'s replay guard deliberately checks it regardless of `payment_method` (so a cash order can't steal a reference). But the field itself was only `required_if:payment_method,paystack` — never *prohibited* otherwise. So a request with `payment_method: in_store` and a reference minted for a real Paystack cart was accepted: it created a cash order carrying that reference, permanently consuming it, and the genuine paid confirmation that followed was refused as "already used" — a customer charged by Paystack with no order.
- **Fix.** `paystack_reference` now carries `prohibited_unless:payment_method,paystack`, so the request 422s during validation, before anything is created or consumed, and the reference stays spendable. The prohibition applies to a non-empty value only, so a client that always sends the key as `null` is unaffected — and the `web/` storefront only ever sends it on the explicitly-`paystack` path, so no client change was needed.
- **Regression coverage:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/StorefrontControllerTest.php` — `test_checkout_rejects_a_paystack_reference_on_a_non_paystack_order` (both `in_store` and `transfer`, 422 with a validation error on the field, no order created) and `test_a_reference_refused_on_a_cash_order_is_still_usable_for_the_real_paystack_checkout` (the refused request burns nothing: the same reference then completes the real initialize→confirm round trip to a `paid` order). The former replaces `test_checkout_rejects_a_duplicate_reference_even_when_the_first_order_was_not_paystack`, whose premise (a cash order succeeding *with* a reference) is now itself the bug.

### PG-7 — a Paystack subaccount could be created and then orphaned from its store row
- **Branch:** `fix/pg-b-2026-09-30` (worktree), commit `f8a131e8`. From the 2026-09-27 payment-gateway pass.
- **Root cause.** `createPaymentAccount()` creates a real, permanent remote object at Paystack and *then* writes the subaccount code to the store row, with no handling for a failure in between. The 409 "already connected" idempotency check reads only `stores.paystack_subaccount_code`, so after such a failure a live remote subaccount existed with nothing pointing at it, the local check quietly lied, and the owner's obvious next move — submit again — created a **second** subaccount.
- **Fix.** `linkSubaccountToStore()` retries the local write 3 times with a short backoff (the realistic failure is transient: a deadlock or a dropped connection). If it still fails, it logs `Log::critical` with the subaccount code, store id, bank code, last 4 and country, fires an `AdminAlertService` alert telling the operator to set the column by hand, and returns a **500 whose message says to contact support and explicitly not to resubmit**. That wording is load-bearing — "please try again" is the duplicate.
- **Deliberately not done, and flagged.** Making the idempotency check ask Paystack whether a subaccount already exists for this bank/account pair. Paystack does expose `GET /subaccount`, but its list/filter semantics for matching on `settlement_bank` + `account_number` were **not verified against the live API**, and a wrong matcher risks *reusing* another store's subaccount — strictly worse than the orphan. Recorded in `laravel-server/AGENTS.md` with the conditions for building it later (verify the response shape and paging first; match on the account pair, never `business_name`, since store names aren't unique).
- **Regression coverage:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/StorePaymentAccountControllerTest.php` — a transient first-attempt failure (via a `Store::updating` hook that throws once) still lands the code; a permanently failing save produces the `critical` log with the subaccount code in context, a `SuperAdminAlertMail`, a 500, and no half-written store row; and the 500's message mentions support and never "try again".

### PG-8 — the payment webhook routes had no rate limit at all
- **Branch:** `fix/pg-b-2026-09-30` (worktree), commit `316e4143`. From the 2026-09-27 payment-gateway pass.
- **Root cause.** `POST /webhooks/paystack` and `POST /webhooks/flutterwave` sat outside every `throttle:` group. Laravel 11 dropped `throttle:api` from the default `api` group and `bootstrap/app.php` deliberately never calls `throttleApi()`, so those two routes were completely unmetered — each call runs an HMAC-SHA512 over the raw request body plus a `provider_reference` lookup, all reachable unauthenticated.
- **Fix.** Both routes moved into a `throttle:webhooks` group, defined in `AppServiceProvider` as **300/min/IP — deliberately loose**. Providers legitimately burst (a settlement batch, a replayed backlog after an outage) and a 429 just makes them redeliver forever, so this is sized to stop a flood, not to police normal traffic.
- **Regression coverage:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/PaymentRouteThrottleTest.php` — asserts both routes carry `throttle:webhooks` **and** that the registered limiter stays at ≥200/min, so a future "tidy-up" can't quietly tighten it into dropping real webhooks.

### PG-9 — only `charge.success`/`status: successful` webhook events were handled; refunds and disputes were silently dropped
- **Branch:** `fix/pg-b-2026-09-30` (worktree), commit `30ec36e1`. From the 2026-09-27 payment-gateway pass.
- **Root cause.** Both handlers had a single `if` and no `else`. Every other event type — refunds, disputes, chargebacks, reversals — was acknowledged with a 200 and dropped with no log and no alert. A refund or dispute raised in the provider's own dashboard therefore left the subscription active and nobody in DumosRx aware it had happened.
- **Fix (visibility, not automation).** `PaymentController::recordUnhandledEvent()` is the shared `else` branch of both handlers: `Log::info` for ordinary noise (`transfer.success`, a plain failed charge, `subscription.*`) and, for anything reading as a refund/dispute/chargeback, `Log::warning` **plus** an `AdminAlertService` alert that states plainly that nothing was refunded or cancelled automatically and must be reconciled by hand. It still returns 200 — a 4xx/5xx only makes the provider redeliver forever. It runs before any transactional work in either handler, so the A-110 rule (never `AdminAlertService::send()` under a DB transaction or row lock) holds.
- **Matched on wording rather than an event-name list, deliberately, and flagged as such.** The needles are `refund`, `dispute`, `chargeback`, `charge_back`, `reversal`, `reversed`, tested against Paystack's `event` and against Flutterwave's `event` + `data.status` concatenated. Paystack's names are known (`refund.processed`, `refund.failed`, `charge.dispute.create|remind|resolve`); **Flutterwave's were not verifiable anywhere in this codebase** — its handler never read `event` at all before this, only `data.status` — so substring matching covers whichever spelling actually arrives instead of a guess that would silently miss. `laravel-server/AGENTS.md` records that, and says to write the real names down before narrowing the match.
- **Regression coverage:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/PaymentWebhookTest.php` — a PG-9 block covering six refund/dispute shapes across both providers (each alerts), a refund event never activating the subscription, an ordinary unhandled event logging at `info` without alerting, a plain failed Flutterwave charge not alerting, and a successful charge still activating and not being misrouted as unhandled.

### A-60 (P3) — a failed hard-delete during a held-sale recall left the sale recallable again while telling the cashier the recall failed
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-client-2026-09-30` (worktree), commit `d1ec3682` (with `A-59`). From the 2026-09-30 `client/` sweep.
- **Root cause.** `handleRecallTransaction()` committed `clearCart()`/`restoreCart(...)` to Zustand *before* `await remove("held_transactions", held.id)`, both inside one `try` whose `catch` reports "Failed to recall transaction". `remove()` fails for reasons unrelated to the recall — `assertWritable()` on a read-only (second) browser tab, `assertStoreOwnership()` on a device whose active store has moved — leaving the cashier with the recalled cart on screen, a failure toast, and the held row still listed as recallable a second time.
- **Fix.** The delete now runs after the parse/lookup work but **before** any cart mutation, so a failure leaves the pre-recall cart exactly as it was and the held sale genuinely still held. The parse-before-mutate ordering from the earlier malformed-`items_json` fix is unchanged; only the delete moved ahead of `clearCart()`.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/use-pos-held-transactions-reseller-recall.test.ts` — a `remove()` that throws leaves the original cart, its original price and `isResellerSale = false` intact while still showing the error toast. RED before the fix (the cart came back as the recalled sale).

### A-59 (P3) — recalling a held reseller sale dropped the markup and the commission, and blamed the loss on a price change
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-client-2026-09-30` (worktree), commit `d1ec3682` (with `A-60`). From the 2026-09-30 `client/` sweep.
- **Root cause.** A reseller sale is three pieces of state: `isResellerSale`, `markupType`, and the per-line `unit_price` marked up above `original_unit_price`. `held_transactions` persisted none of the first two, and recall rebuilt every line from the current catalog, discarding the marked-up price `items_json` actually carried — while `clearCart()` had already reset the two flags and `restoreCart()` did not restore them. A ₦1,200 agreed price on a ₦1,000 shelf item came back at ₦1,000 with the toggle off, so checkout recorded `is_reseller_sale = 0` and zero commission. The only signal was the generic "prices have changed" toast, which pointed the cashier at a catalog change that had not happened.
- **Fix (full, not the fallback mitigation).** `held_transactions` gains `is_reseller_sale INTEGER DEFAULT 0` and `markup_type TEXT` in `client/lib/db/schema.ts` **and** in `SYNC_COLUMN_MIGRATIONS` (so existing databases get them on the next launch), with the server counterpart in `laravel-server/database/migrations/2026_09_30_000000_add_reseller_columns_to_held_transactions_table.php` plus `HeldTransaction`'s `$fillable`/`$casts` — per `.agents/AGENTS.md` §5's cross-repo rule. `handleHoldTransaction()` writes both; `restoreCart()` takes a fourth `restoredMarkup` argument and re-applies them through the **raw** store setters, never `setIsResellerSale()` (whose off-branch reverts every line to `original_unit_price` and would undo the very markup being restored). Recall keeps the held marked-up `unit_price`, clamped to the current catalog price, which remains `original_unit_price` (the markup floor).
- **The misleading toast is fixed at the root, not suppressed.** "Prices have changed" now compares the current catalog price against the held line's `original_unit_price` (falling back to `unit_price` for a pre-feature hold) instead of its possibly marked-up `unit_price`, so a restored markup is no longer reported as a catalog price change while a genuine change still is.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/use-pos-held-transactions-reseller-recall.test.ts` (hold persists both columns; recall restores flag, markup type and marked-up price; no price-change toast for a restored markup; a real catalog change on an ordinary hold still warns) and a `held_transactions` case in `client/__tests__/reseller-commission-schema.test.ts`. `npm run test:schema` re-run against a locally migrated MySQL: `held_transactions` reports no client column missing server-side.

### A-58 (P2) — `npm run test:schema` compared client `audit_logs` against a vestigial MySQL table and silently dropped any column after an inline `--` comment
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-client-2026-09-30` (worktree), commit `d1cae072`. From the 2026-09-30 `client/` sweep.
- **Root cause, two independent defects in one script.** (1) `verify-schema-sync.ts` looked each client table name up directly in `information_schema`, but the sync engine writes client `audit_logs` to the server's **`activity_logs`** (`SyncController::getModelForTable()`); MySQL's own `audit_logs` table is vestigial and unused. So the script compared against the wrong table — three phantom warnings, and the one drift class that has already caused a production incident (`A-28`) could never reach its output. (2) `getSQLiteSchema()` split the raw column block on commas, so `type TEXT NOT NULL, -- 'earned', 'redeemed'` in `loyalty_transactions` produced two phantom columns and **lost `transaction_id`** entirely.
- **Fix.** An explicit `CLIENT_TO_SERVER_TABLE` map (`audit_logs` → `activity_logs`, a `Map` per `.agents/AGENTS.md` §8, mirroring `getModelForTable()`) resolves the server-side table for every comparison and every warning line, which now name both sides. The parser strips `--` to end-of-line from each line before splitting on commas. The pure parts (`parseSQLiteSchema`, `serverTableFor`) are exported and the script only self-executes when invoked directly, so they are unit-testable. The "add to CI" half remains deliberately out of scope per `A-25`'s ruling.
- **What the now-accurate check reports.** Re-run against a locally migrated MySQL: no client column is missing server-side in any synced table (the three `audit_logs` phantoms are gone — `activity_logs` has them all, and `loyalty_transactions.transaction_id` is present now that it is actually compared). Every remaining warning is a *server-only* column — `deleted_at` on the soft-deleting tables, plus `activity_logs`' Laravel-side `description`/`ip_address`/`properties`/`user_agent` — which is expected and harmless; pre-existing, unrelated to this change, and left as-is.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/verify-schema-sync-parsing.test.ts` — the alias map, a synthetic block whose column follows an inline comment, and the real `SCHEMA_SQL` keeping `loyalty_transactions.transaction_id`.

### A-57 (P2) — fulfilling an online order for a product with no unexpired active batch recorded revenue with zero stock-ledger trace and zero COGS
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-client-2026-09-30` (worktree), commit `2726a733`. From the 2026-09-30 `client/` sweep. Not a regression of SF-P2-5, whose routing of this hook through `recordSaleItemStock()` still holds for every case where an active, unexpired batch exists.
- **Root cause.** `recordSaleItemStock()`'s two-step fallback (FEFO over positive-stock batches, then `getAnyActiveBatchForProduct()`) both exclude expired batches, and that function's `includeExpired` escape hatch was used by `submitStockAudit()` only. For a product whose only batches are expired — or which has none — both returned `[]`: no `sale_item_batches` row, no `stock_movements` row, `sale_items.stock_batch_id` null, `cost_price` 0. The POS can't reach it (the catalog query hides such products from `addToCart`), but online-order fulfilment had no equivalent guard, so gross profit was overstated by the whole line, the expired units stayed counted as on hand, and a later return *added* stock that was never deducted.
- **Fix, part 1 — every sale line leaves exactly one `stock_movements` row.** The fallback chain now escalates: FEFO → most-recently-touched active batch → the same lookup with `includeExpired: true` → `getOrCreateTargetBatchForProduct()` opening a `SALE-`prefixed batch valued at the line's cost price and scoped to the product's own store. The partial-shortfall fallback later in the same function escalates to `includeExpired` too, so a leftover quantity can't be left undeducted when only expired batches remain. The existing floor-at-0 and oversold-tagging behaviour is unchanged, so such a line is recorded as an oversell rather than silently.
- **Fix, part 2 — the fulfilment path asks first.** `findOnlineOrderStockGaps()` (`use-fulfill-online-order-mutation.ts`) returns every line whose sellable (active, unexpired, positive) stock doesn't cover the ordered quantity, and `components/pos/online-orders-modal.tsx` shows an `AlertDialog` naming each item with ordered-vs-sellable counts and requires "Fulfill anyway" before proceeding — no `window.confirm`, per `.agents/AGENTS.md` §9.
- **Deliberate behaviour change.** `record-sale-item-stock-depleted-batch.test.ts` previously *asserted* the no-batch case wrote a null `stock_batch_id` and no movement row; that expectation was the bug's contract and is now inverted (a batch is opened, one movement row is written). Called out here because the test rewrite, not just new coverage, is part of this fix.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/record-sale-item-stock-depleted-batch.test.ts` (no batch at all → batch opened, one `-1` movement, cost carried; expired-only → attributed to the expired batch, no new batch created) and `client/__tests__/use-fulfill-online-order-mutation.test.ts` (an expired-only product still writes a movement and deducts the batch; `findOnlineOrderStockGaps` reports zero-stock, partial-shortfall, and nothing when covered). Both new `recordSaleItemStock` cases verified RED first.
- **Not verified in a browser.** The modal change is unit-covered at the hook level only; exercising the dialog end to end needs a real pending online order from the server, which this local pass had no way to produce.

### A-56 (P2) — a mixed-payment sale accepted an over-allocated credit split and permanently overstated the customer's debt by the excess
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-client-2026-09-30` (worktree), commit `e89e6893`. From the 2026-09-30 `client/` sweep. The change-due half of this family was closed earlier (credit excluded from `calculateMixedAmountPaid`/`calculateMixedChangeDue`); that fix still holds and the `outstanding_balance` half was never in its scope.
- **Root cause.** `validatePaymentReadiness()`'s mixed branch rejected only a *negative* split and *under*-coverage. Nothing capped an individual split, so cash ₦400 + credit ₦800 on a ₦1,000 total passed (splits sum to ₦1,200 ≥ ₦1,000) and `use-pos-payment.ts` added the ₦800 credit split **verbatim** to `customers.outstanding_balance`, while `applyCreditPaymentFIFO()` computes this sale's owed amount as `total_amount - amount_paid` = ₦600 and can never apply more than that against it. ₦200 of debt with no sale behind it, invisible in the store's own books.
- **Fix.** `calculateMaxAllowedCreditSplit()` / `calculateCreditSplitTotal()` / `isCreditSplitOverAllocated()` in `lib/utils/pos-calculations.ts` express the rule once (credit ≤ `total - sum(non-credit splits)`, with the module's existing `MONEY_EPSILON` tolerance, negative splits floored so they can't create headroom). `validatePaymentReadiness()` rejects the checkout with the remaining-unpaid amount in the message, and the `outstanding_balance` write in `use-pos-payment.ts` clamps to the same ceiling as a write-side guard in case any other caller reaches it.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/mixed-payment-credit-over-allocation.test.ts` (the three helpers, including the epsilon boundary and the over-tendered-cash case, plus `validatePaymentReadiness` rejecting the exact trace and accepting the correct split) and a case in `client/__tests__/pos-payment-stale-customer-balance.test.ts` asserting the over-allocated checkout writes no sale and leaves the balance at 0.

### A-55 (P2) — the outgoing user's permission group stayed in effect for the incoming user after a lock-screen account switch
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-client-2026-09-30` (worktree), commit `24aa6cf4`. From the 2026-09-30 `client/` sweep.
- **Root cause.** AuthContext's loader effect reset `permissionGroup` to `null` only when `user` became null. The lock screen's "switch account" tile is a user-to-user transition — it calls `login()` without ever going through `logout()` — so `setUser(userProfile)` committed synchronously while `getUserPermissionGroup()` was an awaited sql.js read queued behind the connection-wide FIFO lock. `hasPermission(user, group, key)` took the two as independent arguments and never checked that the group belonged to the user, so for that window every gate was evaluated as `hasPermission(incomingCashier, outgoingAdminsGroup, …)`: `isAdmin`, `canManageStockBatch`, `canViewAllActivity` and every `useHasPermission()` call site read `true`, and a locally-gated write performed in that window succeeded (the local DB has no second permission check).
- **Fix — the group's identity is now explicit.** `getUserPermissionGroup()` returns `{ userId, id, permissions }`, and `hasPermission()`, `useHasPermission()`, `useOwnPermissionGroupId()` and `useOwnGrantScope()` all ignore a group whose `userId` is not the acting user's, falling through to `fallbackPermissions(role)` (fail-safe, role-tier only) instead of another user's grants. A group with no `userId` — the offline assistant's tool context builds one — is still honoured as-is, so nothing else changes. AuthContext additionally drops a group whose `userId` doesn't match on every `user?.id` change, which the report correctly called necessary but not sufficient on its own; the call-site check is the half that actually closes the window.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/has-permission-group-user-mismatch.test.ts` (a mismatched group grants nothing beyond the acting role's tier, an own group still applies, an untagged group still applies) plus a `userId` assertion in `client/__tests__/get-user-permission-group-malformed-row.test.ts`. The mismatch case was verified RED against the pre-fix module.
- Docs: `client/AGENTS.md`'s "Enforced permissions" section now states the group-identity contract and why the `userId` field must not be dropped.

### A-112 (P3, feature addition) — adjustment reasons for damage and loss fed no loss/shrinkage metric anywhere
- **Branch:** `fix/p3-loss-metric-2026-09-30` (worktree). Logged by the 2026-09-30 user-directed follow-up spot-check. Not a correctness bug — nothing computed a wrong number; nothing computed the number at all — so this entry records what was missing and what was added rather than a root cause.
- **What was missing.** `ADJUSTMENT_REASONS` (`client/components/stock-batch/adjustment-derivations.ts`) has let a cashier or manager tag a stock adjustment `Damage` or `Loss` since the Stock Adjustments feature shipped, and `submitStockAdjustment` persists that tag on every `stock_movements` row along with the `unit_cost` needed to value it. No consumer existed: no query in `finance.ts`/`reports.ts`, no dashboard figure, and no `laravel-server` admin metric read `stock_movements.reason` for a shrinkage figure. The categorisation was write-only — a pharmacy's expired/damaged/stolen stock was captured at the point of adjustment and then never reported as the cost centre it is.
- **What was added.** `getStockLossTotal({ from, to })` in `client/lib/db/queries/finance.ts` — `SUM(ABS(quantity) * IFNULL(unit_cost, 0))` over `movement_type = 'adjustment'` rows whose reason is one of the new `STOCK_LOSS_REASON_LABELS`, windowed on `COALESCE(movement_date, created_at)` and scoped by `getActiveStoreId()` like every sibling query in the file. Called from `getBIMetrics` in the same `Promise.all` as `getSmoothedExpensesTotal`, surfaced by `useBIData` as `stockLoss`, and rendered as a "Stock Loss (Damage/Write-offs)" line in the Financial Performance Statement card of Analytics → Profit & Loss (`client/components/analytics/profit-loss-tab.tsx`).
- **Matched on reason labels, not `value` keys.** `buildAdjustmentReason` persists the human label ("Damage"), optionally followed by `ADJUSTMENT_REASON_NOTE_SEPARATOR` and a free-text note, because `stock_movements` has no note column — so the query matches each label exactly *or* as that note's prefix. `STOCK_LOSS_REASON_LABELS` and the separator moved into `client/lib/constants/stock-adjustments.ts` so `lib/db/queries` need not import from `components/`, and `adjustment-derivations.test.ts` now pins the label list to every `direction: "decrease"` entry of `ADJUSTMENT_REASONS` so the two cannot drift.
- **Ruling — reported beside the P&L, not subtracted into Net Profit.** COGS derives from `sale_items.cost_price`, so written-off stock never entered it and the loss is genuinely unreported rather than double counted. Folding it into Net Profit would nonetheless silently redefine Net Profit, Net Margin and the Burn Distribution chart while disagreeing with every other surface reporting the same figures (the P&L CSV export, Daily Close, `fetchProfitLossReportData`). The line therefore carries its own caption saying it is not deducted; promoting it into the P&L proper is a product decision that must change all those surfaces at once. Rationale in `docs/STOCK_LOSS_METRIC.md`.
- **Deliberately not done:** no platform-wide admin equivalent (`AdminStoreMetricsService`, `AdminPlatformService`, `DashboardService` still compute no shrinkage figure) — out of scope for the minimum viable version, and noted as such in `docs/STOCK_LOSS_METRIC.md`.
- **Tests:** five new cases in `client/__tests__/finance-reports.test.ts` (damage and loss both counted and summed at unit cost; a reason carrying a free-text note still counted; `Receive items`/`Inventory count` not counted; out-of-window and soft-deleted movements excluded; 0 rather than null when nothing was written off) plus two drift guards in `client/__tests__/adjustment-derivations.test.ts`. All seven verified RED before the implementation.
- **Suite:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npx vitest run` in `client/` — 347 files / 2266 tests passing (2259 baseline + 7 new), exit 0.

### A-76 (P2) — `CheckAccountStatus` inspected one arbitrary store, so suspending or archiving one store of a multi-store account enforced nothing
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-laravel-a-2026-09-30` (worktree). From the 2026-09-30 `laravel-server/` sweep; the P2 that used to compound the already-fixed `A-74`/`A-75`.
- **Root cause.** For an owner the middleware ran `Store::withTrashed()->where('user_id', $user->id)->first()` — no `orderBy` — and checked only that row. Which store got enforced was whatever the storage engine returned first, so for a two-store owner an admin suspension bit only half the time, and archiving one store 403'd the owner out of the untouched sibling under a `STORE_ARCHIVED` message naming a store the admin never touched.
- **Fix.** The store is resolved **per request**, the same way `SyncController::resolvePushStoreId()` does it (`X-Store-Id` header or a `store_id` input when the caller's tenant owns it, else the caller's own `users.store_id`), and only that store's state blocks. Every lookup stays `withTrashed()` so an archived store can still answer "blocked". A request that names **no** store — an owner calling `/user`, `/dashboard/summary`, … — is blocked only when **every** owned store is suspended or archived; if anything is still in good standing the request proceeds and per-store scoping rejects the rest, so an account-wide 403 is never inferred from one store's state. A named store the caller does not own is ignored rather than honoured.
- **Tests:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/MultiStoreAccountStatusTest.php` — 8 cases (suspended store named, sibling still usable, archived store named, archived sibling not locking the owner out, the all-suspended and all-archived unnamed cases, a foreign `store_id` ignored, staff of a suspended store still blocked). Three were RED before the fix. `ArchivedStoreAccessTest` and `StoreSuspensionEnforcementTest` were green throughout.
- Docs: `laravel-server/AGENTS.md` gains a "`CheckAccountStatus` resolves the store per *request*" section, including the standing requirement that enforcement and push scoping keep using the same resolution rule.

### A-80 (P2) — coupon usage limits were check-then-act with no lock and only counted at activation, so a single-use coupon could be redeemed without limit
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-laravel-a-2026-09-30` (worktree). From the 2026-09-30 `laravel-server/` sweep.
- **Root cause.** `max_uses`/`max_uses_per_user` are counted from `coupon_usages`, and the only writer was `recordCouponUsage()` at **activation**. Validity was therefore evaluated against a count that only moved after the money was committed, with no lock across the check: twenty concurrent `POST /subscription/pay` calls on a `max_uses: 1` launch coupon all passed `isGloballyValid()` against zero usages and all got the discount, and a double-submitted 100%-off coupon took the `$finalAmount <= 0` self-activation branch twice, minting two overlapping active subscriptions for one coupon.
- **Fix — reservation under a lock, mirroring the `A-79` credit-reservation contract.** New `SubscriptionService::reserveCoupon()` opens a transaction, takes `lockForUpdate()` on the coupon row, re-validates inside it and writes the `CouponUsage` row before returning, so a second caller blocks and then sees the real count. `initiatePayment()` uses it for both branches and stamps `metadata.coupon_usage_id` on the `PaymentTransaction`. The reservation is then either **linked** — `attachCouponUsageToSubscription()`, at self-activation (now inside its own transaction with the `Subscription::create()`) and at `activateSubscriptionFromTransaction()` — or **released**: `releaseCouponUsage()` on an `initializeTransaction()` failure, and in `failTransaction()` stamped `coupon_usage_released` so the release is idempotent across whichever of the verify and webhook paths arrives first. Activation no longer creates a usage row for a transaction carrying a `coupon_usage_id`; the create branch survives only for transactions started before reservations existed.
- **Ruling — no `UNIQUE (coupon_id, user_id)` index.** The report offered one as extra enforcement for the common `max_uses_per_user: 1` case. `max_uses_per_user` is a real column and legitimately greater than 1, so the index would enforce a cap the product does not have. The lock is the enforcement point; `validateCoupon()` is now a read-only check and anything granting a discount must go through `reserveCoupon()`.
- **Tests:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/CouponUsageReservationTest.php` — 8 cases (usage recorded at initiate with the id on the transaction, a second user getting no discount on a `max_uses: 1` coupon before either checkout completes, a 100%-off coupon self-activating exactly once, the usage row linked to the subscription it paid for, release on a provider failure, release on `failTransaction()`, activation not double-counting, and a legacy no-reservation transaction still recording at activation). Three were RED before the fix.
- Docs: `laravel-server/AGENTS.md` gains a "Coupon usage is reserved under a lock" section next to the referral-credit one it mirrors.
