- **Ruling — the input stays `min={0}`; only the delta goes negative.** A physical count cannot be negative, so widening the input to accept one would make the field mean two different things depending on the reason. The signed value is an internal consequence of the count, not something the user types. `applyStockAdjustmentDelta()` already handles a negative delta through the same FEFO deduction and shortfall fallback the "Damage"/"Loss" reasons use, so nothing downstream needed to change.
- Copy follows the maths: the reason hint reads "Sets stock to the counted quantity" instead of "Adds or removes", and the quantity column/label reads "Counted" under this reason (`adjustmentQuantityLabel()`), so the field's meaning is visible at the point of entry rather than inferable only from the preview.
- Tests: `adjustment-derivations.test.ts` asserts a count below/above/equal to current yields a negative/positive/zero delta and that the preview equals the count; `adjustment-items-step.test.tsx` asserts the on-screen "Stock After" matches the entered count exactly, in both the table and card layouts; `stock-adjustment-submit.test.ts` drives a real FEFO deduction end to end and asserts on-hand lands on the counted figure, soonest-expiry batch first.

### A-42 — the `JsonList` cast turned a NULL `enabled_payment_methods` into `[]`, disabling every payment method on a fresh device
- **Branch:** `worktree-agent-afe9a1d0a65aef348`. Found by an independent review of `c384ca47..6218e1ed`; a regression introduced by `A-29`'s own fix.
- **The mechanism.** Laravel's `HasAttributes` short-circuits NULL for *primitive* casts but **not** for class casts, so `JsonList::get()` ran against a NULL column and its `toList(null)` returned `[]`. The old `'array'` cast returned `null` there. The client's fallback is `if (!storeProfile?.enabled_payment_methods) return [all five methods]` — `null` is falsy and falls back, but the string `"[]"` is truthy and parses to an empty list, which `PaymentMethodSelector` renders as **zero payment buttons**. No sale can be completed from that screen.
- **Blast radius.** Every server-side store-creation path — `RegistersAccounts.php`, `Web/StoreController.php`, `AdminStoreService.php` — leaves the column unset, so it is NULL by default. Any store created through them was one sync pull away from a till with no payment methods.
- Fix: `JsonList::get()` and `::set()` pass a true SQL NULL straight through (`get()`'s return type widens to `?array`), restoring the pre-`A-29` "unset" sentinel the client's fallback is written against. The repeated-decode heal for the double-encoded case — the whole point of `A-29` — is untouched, as is `[]` for a genuine non-list (bare word, JSON object, scalar).
- **Why `set()` too, not just `get()`.** The review only called out `get()`, but leaving `set()` encoding NULL as `"[]"` would let the very next write of an unset column re-create the bad state, and passing NULL through on write is what the old `'array'` cast did (Laravel skips JSON casting for a null value on write).
- Tests: `laravel-server/tests/Feature/StoreEnabledPaymentMethodsCastTest.php` gains a NULL-column case and a write-NULL case — the exact gap that let this ship, since that suite covered double-encoded, bare-string, object and real-array values but never NULL. On the client, `client/__tests__/pos-enabled-payment-methods-null-fallback.test.tsx` asserts a NULL/absent value renders all five buttons in the real `PaymentMethodSelector`, and that an explicit list is still honoured.
- Housekeeping in the same change: the fallback parser existed twice (`hooks/use-settings-form.ts`, and an inline `useMemo` in `lib/hooks/use-pos-system.ts`, with the default list written out in both). Both now import `parseEnabledPaymentMethods`/`DEFAULT_ENABLED_PAYMENT_METHODS` from the new `client/lib/payment-methods.ts`, so the settings screen and the till can no longer disagree about what "unset" means. Behaviour is unchanged for both.

### A-51 — reloading any `/admin/*` page eventually logged the super-admin out
- **Branch:** `worktree-agent-aac92e45af653f63f`. Found in production, not from an audit pass. Reproduced entirely against local tests and mocked HTTP — an earlier investigation triggered it live and burned the product owner's real session, so this pass deliberately touched no deployed host.
- **Symptom.** Reloading an admin page sometimes, and after a while always, dropped the admin back at `/admin/login` with a session the server still considered valid. Once it happened, every subsequent reload failed the same way until a fresh login.
- **The mechanism — two independent faults that fed each other.** *Client:* `web/app/admin/layout.tsx` set its `checking` flag to `false` and never back to `true`, so once the layout had rendered a bypass page (`/admin/login`, `/admin/handoff`) its redirect effect read "not verified yet" as "definitely logged out" and fired synchronously, before the in-flight `initSession()` could resolve. `web/app/admin/login/page.tsx` meanwhile skipped verification entirely when the **persisted** `user` from `admin-auth-storage` was non-null, and pushed to `/admin` on that value alone. The two bounced off each other in a loop, and each bounce fired another `POST /admin/session/refresh`. *Server:* `refreshAdminSession()` rotates the refresh cookie single-use, so among concurrent requests carrying the same cookie only the first won; every loser hit the already-consumed branch and answered `401` **plus `forgetAdminSessionCookie()`**. Responses arrive out of order, so a loser's cookie deletion routinely landed after the winner's fresh cookie and destroyed the one valid session that had just been established — which is why the failure was permanent rather than transient. Two ordinary admin tabs reloading together are enough on their own; the redirect loop just made it constant.
- **Fix, part 1 — the layout never redirects during an in-flight check.** The verification effect now sets `checking` back to `true` whenever it is about to run, and additionally writes a `verifyingRef` **synchronously**. The ref is the load-bearing half: React runs both effects in the same commit in declaration order, so the redirect effect would otherwise still read the previous render's `checking`. The state half is kept because it drives the spinner and guarantees a re-render once the check settles.
- **Fix, part 2 — the login page requires real verification.** It now keys on `sessionVerified`, never on the persisted `user`, both for deciding whether to call `initSession()` and for deciding whether to redirect away. This restores the principle the store's own comments already state: `user` is persisted to `localStorage` and is therefore editable by whoever sits at the browser, so it can never stand in for server verification.
- **Fix, part 3 — one in-flight refresh per tab.** `initSession()` now holds a module-level shared promise, mirroring the `refreshPromise` slot `web/lib/api/base-client.ts` already used for its own 401 burst, and cleared once settled so a later genuine re-check still runs. `base-client.ts`'s admin 401 path was additionally re-pointed at `initSession()` instead of posting to `/admin/session/refresh` itself, so the layout, the login page and the 401 interceptor all share **one** slot rather than two.
- **Fix, part 4 — concurrent rotation is survivable server-side.** Rotation marks the outgoing token (`name = 'admin-refresh-rotated'`, `expires_at = now + 30s`) instead of deleting it. A request that finds an already-rotated token inside that window is a lost race, not a revoked session: it is served a fresh access token and **no `Set-Cookie` at all**, leaving the winner's cookie intact. A token that is simply unknown, or whose grace window has lapsed, now gets a bare `401` with no cookie deletion. The cookie is still actively cleared for the two cases that genuinely warrant it — a credential that is not `refresh`-scoped, and a deactivated or missing account.
- **Ruling — a grace window, not merely a non-destructive 401.** Dropping the cookie deletion alone would have stopped the permanent logout, but every losing tab would still have been told "session expired" and shown a login form for a session that was fine. Resolving the loser against the same session is what makes a legitimate multi-tab reload actually work. The window is bounded at 30 seconds and does **not** weaken single-use rotation in the way it might appear to: before this change a live refresh token was valid indefinitely until consumed, whereas now consuming it gives it a hard 30-second `expires_at` that Sanctum's guard enforces everywhere else in the app. The used token's usable life is shortened, not extended. Nothing about who may refresh changed: the ability check, the `HttpOnly`/`SameSite=Strict`/`Secure` attributes and the `throttle:session-refresh` limiter are all untouched.
- **Ruling — the loser gets no cookie rather than a second rotation.** Rotating again for the loser would also have worked, but it orphans the winner's freshly minted refresh token (nobody holds it, nothing expires it) and makes the browser's final cookie depend on response ordering. Issuing an access token only keeps exactly one live refresh token per session and makes the outcome order-independent. Rotation also purges that user's grace-expired rotated tokens, so the marked rows do not accumulate.
- Regression coverage, RED before the fix: `laravel-server/tests/Feature/AdminSessionCookieTest.php` — `concurrent refreshes with the same cookie leave a usable session` (RED: the loser emitted a cookie-clearing `Set-Cookie`) and `a grace expired rotated refresh token is rejected` (pins the window's upper bound). `web/__tests__/admin-session-refresh-race.test.tsx` — 7 tests, 5 RED: single-request collapsing and shared-failure handling for `initSession()`, the login page not redirecting on a persisted-but-unverified user and redirecting once verification succeeds, and the layout not bouncing to login mid-check. The two GREEN ones pin deliberately-unchanged behaviour (a later caller still gets a fresh request; a genuinely failed verification still redirects).
- **Suite:** `web/` 7 files / 21 tests passing, `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `laravel-server` 545 passed / 1 skipped.

### A-29 — a double-encoded `stores.enabled_payment_methods` blanked the entire admin Store Details page
- **Branch:** none — committed directly on `dev`. Found in production by reproducing it live in the browser console on `/admin/stores/details/?id=edc5b0f0-6f59-4ce7-8015-5f96fa311895` (device `DRX-JIMA7TL8S`), not from an audit pass. Unrelated to the recurring `Unexpected token '<'` chunk-load noise seen on the same store.
- **Symptom.** The whole page rendered as the browser's native "This page couldn't load / Reload / Back" screen. The uncaught exception was `TypeError: t.enabled_payment_methods.join is not a function`, thrown by `StorePaymentsCard` (`web/components/admin/stores/details/store-detail-sections.tsx`), which did `payments.enabled_payment_methods.length > 0 ? …join(", ") : null` against a value its type declared as `string[]`.
- **The mechanism, traced end to end.** The column is **not** an array at rest anywhere in this pipeline. On the client it is a SQLite **TEXT** column holding JSON (`client/lib/db/schema.ts`, default `'["cash","card","transfer","credit","mixed"]'`), written with `JSON.stringify(next)` (`client/components/settings/store/payment-settings-card.tsx`, `client/hooks/use-settings.ts`). That TEXT value is pushed verbatim by the sync engine, and `SyncController::push()` applies it with **`$model->forceFill($payload)`** — `forceFill` bypasses mass-assignment guarding but **not casting**. `Store::$casts` declared `'enabled_payment_methods' => 'array'`, and Laravel's `array` cast unconditionally `json_encode()`s whatever it is handed on write. Handed the string `["cash","card"]`, it stored `"[\"cash\",\"card\"]"` — **double-encoded**. Every subsequent read `json_decode()`s the outer JSON string literal and yields a PHP **string**, not an array. `AdminStoreDetailService` then serialised that string into the payload and the frontend called `.join()` on it.
- **Why `?: []` did not catch it.** `AdminStoreDetailService` had `'enabled_payment_methods' => $store->enabled_payment_methods ?: []`. `?:` only replaces **falsy** values — `null`, `[]`, `""`, `0`, `false`. A non-empty string is truthy, so the malformed value passed straight through untouched. The guard was testing for absence when the actual failure mode was wrong *type*, which is exactly the case it could never see.
- **Fix, part 1 — the write path, so it cannot recur.** New `laravel-server/app/Casts/JsonList.php`, applied to both `enabled_payment_methods` and `custom_units` (identical shape: client TEXT JSON, synced, previously `'array'`). On write it normalises to a real list before encoding, so a JSON string arriving from the sync push is decoded once rather than encoded twice. On read it decodes repeatedly (bounded at 4 levels), so **rows already double-encoded in production heal on read without a data migration**. Anything that is not a JSON list — a bare word like `cash,card`, a JSON object, a scalar, `null` — yields `[]` rather than throwing or leaking a non-list. `custom_units` was carrying the same latent bug and is fixed in the same change; nothing rendered it yet, so it had not surfaced.
- **Fix, part 2 — backend defence in depth.** `AdminStoreDetailService` now coerces with `is_array($x) ? array_values($x) : []` instead of `?: []`. Checked for other server-side readers that hand this column to a frontend: there are none beyond this service and the generic pull payload (`mapPullRowForClient()` -> `$item->toArray()`), which goes through the same cast and so is fixed by part 1. No storefront/checkout serialiser reads it.
- **Fix, part 3 — frontend defence in depth.** `StorePaymentsCard` narrows through a local `normalizeEnabledPaymentMethods()` (array -> string members only; JSON string -> parsed and re-narrowed; anything else -> `[]`), and the field is now typed `unknown` in `web/lib/types/admin-store-detail.ts`. The type was widened **and** the render site guarded — widening alone would have silenced `tsc` while leaving the identical crash.
- **Fix, part 4 — the structural gap that turned one bad row into a dead page.** `web/app/` had **no `error.tsx` or `global-error.tsx` anywhere**, so *any* uncaught render exception in the admin panel blanked the whole document with no recovery and no report. Added `web/app/admin/error.tsx` (recovery card wired to `reset()`, plus a "Back to dashboard" escape) and `web/app/global-error.tsx` (its own `<html>`/`<body>`, inline styles, no shared components — a root boundary must not depend on the tree it is catching for). Both report through the **existing** `reportClientError()` in `web/lib/api/logger.ts`, which already backs `POST /logs/client-error`; no new telemetry channel was invented. There is no Sentry SDK on this origin — the Sentry references in `app/admin/system/page.tsx` read the *client* app's issues through the admin API and do not instrument `web/` — so `/logs/client-error` is the whole of the available channel and that is deliberate.
- **Ruling — heal on read rather than ship a data migration.** A one-off `UPDATE` would fix the one known row and nothing about the next device that pushes the same shape. The cast's repeated decode makes every existing malformed row correct from the next read onward, and its write normalisation makes new ones impossible, so correctness does not depend on an ops action landing. The row's stored bytes stay malformed until something writes the store again; that residual cleanup is logged as **ops-only** `A-30` in `docs/KNOWN_BUGS.md` and is explicitly **not** required for the page to work.
- Regression coverage, all RED before the fix: `laravel-server/tests/Feature/StoreEnabledPaymentMethodsCastTest.php` — `a json string written by the sync push is not double encoded`, `an already double encoded row still reads back as an array`, `a bare non json string reads back as an empty array instead of throwing`, `a json object is not passed off as a list of methods` (4 of 5 RED; `a real array round trips unchanged` pins the deliberately-unchanged happy path). `web/__tests__/store-payments-card-non-array-methods.test.tsx` — reproduces the exact production `TypeError` by feeding the component a JSON string, plus object/null cases (2 of 3 RED). `web/__tests__/admin-error-boundary.test.tsx` — 4 tests, all RED (the module did not exist), covering the recovery card, `reset()` wiring, reporting through the existing channel, and report-once-per-error.
- **Test infrastructure:** `web/` already had vitest + `@testing-library/react` (`web/vitest.config.ts`, `web/vitest.setup.ts`, `npm test`), with four existing specs under `web/__tests__/`. Nothing new was needed; the new specs follow those conventions.
- **Noted, not fixed — `client/hooks/use-settings-form.ts:40`.** The `useState` initialiser does a bare `JSON.parse(storeProfile.enabled_payment_methods)` with no `try`/`catch`, while the `useEffect` doing the identical parse 40 lines below (line 82) is guarded. A malformed local value would throw during the settings screen's first render. Same bug family, different app and test suite; logged as `A-31` in `docs/KNOWN_BUGS.md` rather than patched without coverage here.

### A-31 — the settings form's first render parsed `enabled_payment_methods` without a `try`/`catch`
- **Branch:** none — committed directly on `dev`. Follow-up fix for the gap `A-29` deliberately left open (different app, different test suite).
- **The mechanism.** `useSettingsForm`'s `useState` initialiser (`client/hooks/use-settings-form.ts:40`) did a bare `JSON.parse(storeProfile.enabled_payment_methods)`. The `useEffect` doing the identical parse 40 lines below was already wrapped in `try`/`catch` with a fallback to the default method list — only the initialiser was unguarded, so a malformed or double-encoded value (the exact shape `A-29` fixed server-side) would throw during the settings screen's very first render, before the guarded path ever ran. The Tauri client has no error boundary on that route, so the throw would have taken the whole settings screen down.
- **Fix.** Extracted a single `parseEnabledPaymentMethods()` helper (parses, falls back to the default list on a JSON error *or* on a non-array result, e.g. a double-encoded string) and call it from both the initializer and the sync effect, so the two sites cannot drift out of guard again.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/use-settings-form-payment-methods-parse.test.tsx` — malformed JSON, double-encoded JSON string, a well-formed value, and no value at all.
- **Suite:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npx vitest run` 319 files / 1743 tests passing.

### A-27 — the stuck-sync crash reporter fed itself, producing unbounded, self-nesting crash reports in production
- **Branch:** none — committed directly on `dev`. Found in production from Sentry (`DUMOSRX-CLIENT-1B`, `17`, `19`, `1A`, `1G`, `1M`, 50+ events each and still climbing at the time of the fix), not from an audit pass, so it is logged here as found-and-fixed in one pass rather than carried in `KNOWN_BUGS.md`.
- **The mechanism, confirmed line by line against the real code.** `recordSyncFailure()` (`client/lib/db/base-helpers.ts`) reports a queue item that has failed `SYNC_FAILURE_REPORT_THRESHOLD` (5) times by calling `logCrash(new Error(\`Sync item stuck … : ${errorMessage}\`))`. `errorMessage` is whatever the push threw or the server returned (`f.reason` in `sync-engine/push.ts`), and for a database-level rejection that is the driver's full error string — which includes the **entire attempted SQL statement**, i.e. a verbatim copy of the row being pushed. `logCrash()` (`client/lib/utils/error-logger.ts`) stores its report as a row in the local `feedback` table, which `insert()` queues for push like any other row. So when the stuck item **is itself a `feedback` row**, the report describing its failure embeds that row's entire content, including the previous report it already embedded. Generation *n+1* is strictly larger than generation *n*, and the real captured events show 5–6 levels of nesting. Growth ends only when MySQL's column length is exceeded (`SQLSTATE[22001] … Data too long for column 'content'` / `'fingerprint'`), at which point *that* insert fails too and generates yet another report — permanently.
- **Why the existing defences did not stop it.** `logCrash()`'s fingerprint dedup coalesces repeats of the *same* crash, but the fingerprint embeds the stuck record's id (`sync|Sync item stuck … on feedback/<uuid>: …`), which is a different uuid every generation, so every generation looked like a brand-new crash. The `[REPORTED]` marker correctly fires `logCrash` once per queue item — the loop does not need a second report for the same item, it creates a *new* item each time. And the direct `reportClientError` HTTP path's 5-minute per-fingerprint throttle is keyed on the same ever-changing fingerprint.
- **Fix, part 1 — nothing unbounded is ever embedded in a new log message.** New `client/lib/utils/error-truncation.ts` (`truncateForLog`, `MAX_EMBEDDED_ERROR_LENGTH` = 300, `MAX_CRASH_MESSAGE_LENGTH` = 2000). `recordSyncFailure()` truncates the push error before it goes into either the new crash description **or** `_sync_queue.last_error`, and `logCrash()` truncates the final message again as a backstop, so no caller can hand it an unbounded string. This alone makes exponential growth impossible: no generation can exceed a fixed size. `MAX_CRASH_MESSAGE_LENGTH` deliberately matches `ActivityLogController::MAX_MESSAGE_LENGTH` — an oversized crash message used to be **silently 422-rejected** by `/logs/client-error`, so the reports that mattered most were the ones the server dropped.
- **Fix, part 2 — a `feedback` sync failure is never reported back into `feedback`.** Truncation caps each generation's *size* but not the *count*: a bounded report about a bounded report still writes one new row per generation, forever. `recordSyncFailure()` now routes a stuck item whose `table_name` is `feedback` through `reportStuckCrashLog()`, which reports it by `console.error` plus a direct `Sentry.captureException` — the already-wired client-side channel that `logCrash()` itself uses — and writes no `feedback` row at all. This is the part that makes the loop structurally impossible rather than merely slower.
- **Ruling — a stuck crash-log row is dropped from the queue, not retried forever.** A crash report is already delivered to Sentry and to `/logs/client-error` the moment it is captured; the `feedback` row is a redundant third copy. Once it has failed five times it is failing for a permanent reason (an oversized column, a missing server column), and retrying at the 1-hour backoff cap forever is pure noise. `reportStuckCrashLog()` therefore deletes the queue entry and calls `markConflictSettled('feedback', id)` — the existing mechanism for exactly this shape. The `markConflictSettled` half is **not optional**: leaving the row `_synced = 0` with no queue entry is precisely what `requeueOrphanedRows()` (`reconcile-identity.ts`) re-queues on every app boot, which would restore the loop one generation per boot.
- **Ruling — only crash rows are dropped; genuine user feedback keeps retrying.** The `feedback` table carries real user-submitted bug reports and feature requests as well as machine-written crash logs. The drop is gated on the row actually being a crash report (`type = 'bug'` **and** a non-null `fingerprint`, which only `logCrash()` sets), so a user's own submission stuck behind a transient server problem keeps its normal backoff and is never silently discarded. It still stops generating crash reports about itself, because that guard is on the table, not on the row.
- **Server side: checked, no equivalent risk.** Crash reports reach the server by two independent routes and neither can amplify. The local `feedback` row goes through the ordinary sync-engine push (`SyncController::push`), which stores it as data and never generates a client-bound error report from it. The direct `/logs/client-error` route (`ActivityLogController::logClientError`) is unauthenticated but hard-capped — `url` 2048, `message` 2000, `details` 8192 bytes via `EncodedSizeAtMost`, plus the `client-error-log` rate-limiter — and it only writes to `laravel.log` and `ActivityLog`; nothing there loops back to the client. `reportClientError()` also refuses to report a failure of `/logs/client-error` itself. This was purely a client-local `feedback`-table + sync-push phenomenon.
- Regression coverage: `client/__tests__/stuck-sync-crash-log-amplification.test.ts` — `does not create another feedback row when the stuck item is itself a crash-log feedback row`, `caps the crash message at a fixed length no matter how large the underlying push error is`, `still reports a normal stuck non-feedback item with its full, informative message`, and `leaves genuine user-submitted feedback in the queue to keep retrying`. They run against a real sql.js database built from the app's own `SCHEMA_SQL` and drive `recordSyncFailure()` with a reproduction of the real captured `SQLSTATE[22001]` payload. Three of the four were RED before the fix (the truncation and both self-reference assertions); the fourth pins the deliberately-unchanged behaviour for real user feedback.
- **Superseded in part by `A-126` (2026-10-01).** Two gaps in this fix stayed live afterwards: the `[REPORTED]` marker gated the `reportStuckCrashLog()` cleanup as well as the once-only `logCrash()`, so no device already past the threshold under the old code was ever cleaned up; and the `'fingerprint'` half of the `SQLSTATE[22001]` failure named above was not actually closed, because `buildCrashFingerprint()` capped at 500 characters against a `VARCHAR(255)` column. See `A-126` at the top of this file.
- Related, still open and **ops-only**: `A-28` in `docs/KNOWN_BUGS.md` — production's `feedback`/`audit_logs` tables are missing the `occurrence_count` column its migration adds (Sentry `DUMOSRX-CLIENT-11`, 567 events), which is what made those items stick in the first place. No code fix applies; this entry closes the amplification the sticking used to trigger, not the sticking.

## 2026-09-28

### A-25 — `npm run test:schema` was broken (`Could not find syncColumns in core.ts`)
- **Branch:** none — committed directly on `dev`, as a follow-up to batch 9 (found while fixing A-15, logged separately per `.agents/AGENTS.md` §2, fixed on request immediately after).
- **Problem confirmed exactly as logged.** `client/scripts/verify-schema-sync.ts` regex-extracted a `const syncColumns = [...]` block from `client/lib/db/core.ts` to get the list of synced, store-scoped tables to cross-check against the MySQL and SQLite schemas. That symbol moved to `client/lib/db/schema-migrations.ts` during an earlier sync-engine extraction and was never renamed back, so the script exited 1 with `Could not find syncColumns in core.ts` before doing any real comparison — a no-op that failed for the wrong reason.
- **Fix:** re-pointed the extraction at `STORE_SCOPED_TABLES`, the canonical exported list of store-scoped/synced tables in `schema-migrations.ts` (the same list `requeueOrphanedRows()` and the A-8 `stores`-prune fix already use elsewhere) — a plain string array, simpler to parse than the old object-literal `syncColumns` shape. Re-ran `npm run test:schema` against the real dev Laravel MySQL schema: it now completes with exit code 0, reporting only pre-existing, expected column-parity warnings (e.g. server-side `deleted_at` columns the local schema intentionally omits in favour of `_deleted`) rather than aborting.
- **Ruling — the "add it to `checks.yml`'s client job" half of A-15/A-25's recommendation is deliberately NOT done.** The script calls `php artisan tinker` against `information_schema.columns WHERE TABLE_SCHEMA = database()`, which requires a real MySQL connection; the CI test suite (and `phpunit.xml`) deliberately runs the server suite against in-memory SQLite for speed, which has no `information_schema`. Wiring this into CI would mean adding a MySQL service container and running migrations against it in the workflow — real infrastructure work, not the "contained tooling fix" A-25 was scoped as. Left as a manually-run check (`.agents/AGENTS.md` §5 already tells contributors to run it after a schema change); worth its own follow-up if the team wants it enforced in CI.
- No regression tests added — this is a standalone Node script with no existing test harness of its own, and the fix's correctness is verified by actually running it against the real server schema (above), not by a unit test asserting regex behaviour.

### A-23 — the cross-module `CustomEvent`/`localStorage` bus had no single source of truth
- **Branch:** none — committed directly on `dev` as batch 9 of the 2026-09-28 audit remediation series, deliberately in its own commit: it is by far the widest diff of the batch (44 files) and the one most likely to want isolated review or revert.
- **Pure refactor. No behaviour change is intended anywhere in it**, which is why it carries no new tests of its own — the existing suite passing unchanged is the regression test. Client suite before: 255 files / 1392 tests. After: 255 files / 1392 tests, all passing, plus the same two pre-existing unrelated `sql-wasm.wasm` unhandled rejections in `sync-skips-when-offline` / `sync-blocked-during-impersonation` that were there before (verified by re-running both files against a stashed tree). `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- Confirmed exactly as described. Six `CustomEvent` names were dispatched from three modules (`core.ts`, `sync-engine/index.ts`, `token-manager.ts`) and listened for in five more (`auth-context.tsx`, `store-context.tsx`, `sync-indicator.tsx`, `DatabaseProvider.tsx`, `license-guard.tsx`), every one of them as a bare string literal, with the payload shape written out inline at the dispatch site and re-guessed at each listener. Around 70 `localStorage` call sites used raw string keys across ~30 files.
- Fix: two new modules. `client/lib/events.ts` — `APP_EVENTS` (the six names), `AppEventDetails` (a payload type per event), and `emitAppEvent()`/`onAppEvent()`; `onAppEvent` returns an unsubscribe closure so a React effect can `return onAppEvent(...)` instead of hand-pairing add/remove. `client/lib/storage-keys.ts` — `STORAGE_KEYS` listing all 31 keys the app writes, typed accessors for the shared ones (`getAuthToken`, `getStoredUser`, `getRecentUsers`, `getStoredActiveStoreId`, `getLastSyncTime`, …), and `readJsonItem`/`writeJsonItem` for the rest. Every dispatch, listener, read and write site now goes through them; module-private keys that already had a local constant now alias the registry rather than repeating the string.
- **Ruling — `getStoredActiveStoreId()`, not `getActiveStoreId()`.** The finding's own example of the bug class is the active store id having two sources of truth: `localStorage["dumos_active_store_id"]` for the `X-Store-Id` header, and `core.ts`'s resolver for local queries. Collapsing them into one name was tempting and is wrong — they are genuinely two different values with two different lifetimes, and unifying them is a behaviour change this refactor explicitly must not make. The accessor is named so the two can never be reached for by the same identifier, and both `storage-keys.ts` and `client/AGENTS.md` say why.
- **Ruling — `RecentUser` moved to `lib/types/user.ts`.** It was declared inside `auth-context.tsx`; `storage-keys.ts` needs it to type `getRecentUsers()`, and importing it from a React context into a plain module is a cycle waiting to happen. `auth-context.tsx` re-exports it, so every existing import path still resolves.
- **The one deliberate normalisation:** the typed accessors never throw. Several old call sites did a bare `JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem("dumos_user") || "{}")` that would throw on a corrupt value; `getStoredUser()` logs and returns `null` instead. `auth-context.tsx`'s session-restore path, which *used* that throw to detect a corrupt write and clear the key, keeps that behaviour explicitly — it now checks for "key is set but parsed to null" and clears it there.
- Docs: `client/AGENTS.md` gains a "Cross-module events and `localStorage`" section stating both modules as the required pattern, including the no-bare-string-key rule and the `getStoredActiveStoreId` vs `getActiveStoreId` distinction, so the next subsystem does not add a seventh ad-hoc event name.

### A-20 — `audit_logs` had no local retention, so it grew forever on every device
- **Branch:** none — committed directly on `dev` as batch 9 of the 2026-09-28 audit remediation series.
- Confirmed for `audit_logs`: nothing anywhere pruned it, and `resetDatabase()` (a full device wipe) was the only path that ever cleared it. At the audit's own figure of 10–15 rows per sale, and with every write on the web build re-serialising the whole database through `db.export()`, the table is the dominant driver of the IndexedDB blob's unbounded growth.
- **Correction to the finding's `_sync_queue` half:** `_sync_queue` does not in fact grow without bound. `push.ts` deletes each row on a successful push (`DELETE FROM _sync_queue WHERE id IN (…)`), so the queue drains. What the audit describes there is real but is a *size-per-row* problem (a full-row JSON snapshot per write), not a retention one, and it is load-bearing — the snapshot is what makes a queued change replayable after the local row has moved on. No change made.
- Fix: a new `client/lib/db/retention.ts` with `pruneSyncedAuditLogs()`, called from `sync()` on the writer tab after `last_sync_time` is stamped. It deletes `audit_logs` rows that are `_synced = 1`, have no pending `_sync_queue` row, and are older than `AUDIT_LOG_LOCAL_RETENTION_DAYS`; it self-throttles to once per 24h via `localStorage`, and deletes in chunks of 400 ids so a first-run backlog cannot exceed SQLite's bound-parameter limit.
- **Ruling — raw `execute()`, never `softDelete()`.** These rows must not enter `_sync_queue`. This is local storage reclamation; a synced delete would propagate to the server and destroy the authoritative trail for every device, which is the exact opposite of the finding's own premise ("the server keeps the full trail").
- **Ruling — the window is 730 days, not 90.** 90 was the obvious choice and is wrong here, because it silently breaks a feature the UI actually offers: the activity log's date-range picker (`components/ui/date-range-picker.tsx`) exposes "Year to date" and "Last year" presets and an unrestricted custom range, and the client has no way to fetch the server's copy — there is no activity-log read endpoint, and A-19 has just removed the last of the legacy cloud reads. Anything pruned locally is therefore unreachable from the app, permanently. 730 days is the smallest window under which every preset the picker can produce (worst case: "Last year" selected on 31 December, reaching 730 days back) is still fully answerable from local data. It still converts unbounded growth into a bounded two-year window, which is the actual finding. Shortening it is a one-constant change if the product decides the activity log may go thin past some shorter horizon — that is a product call, not an engineering one, and it is deliberately not being made here.
- **Punted, with the reason — the "stop pulling `audit_logs` to devices that never render it" half is not done.** It is not cleanly determinable from the code which devices those are. `audit_logs` is read by three surfaces, not one: the activity log page (`components/activity-log/`), the staff-activities tab, and `getProductHistory()`/`getProductCreator()` behind the product Details tab — and that last one is reachable by any role that can open a product, i.e. ordinary cashier devices, not just the owner's. There is no per-device or per-role flag that means "this device will never render an audit row", and inventing one by reading the current session's role would break the moment a cashier hands the terminal to a manager, or a permission group is edited, leaving a device with a silent hole in its local history and no way to backfill it (the pull is watermarked). Making that call needs a product decision about whether the product Details history is guaranteed on every device; it is not a code read. Logged here rather than guessed at.
- Regression coverage: `client/__tests__/audit-log-local-retention.test.ts` — `drops synced audit rows older than the retention window`, `never drops a row this device has not pushed yet, however old it is`, `never drops a row that still has a pending _sync_queue entry`, `does not enqueue a sync delete — the server keeps the full trail`, `keeps everything the activity log's widest date-range preset can ask for`, `runs at most once a day, so it is not a per-sync full-table scan`, `honours an explicit retention window`, and `deletes in chunks so a large backlog does not blow the SQL parameter limit`. All eight run against a real sql.js database built from the app's own `SCHEMA_SQL`, and all eight were RED before (the module did not exist).

### A-22 — `saveDatabase()` calls were not serialised, and nothing flushed the last write when a tab closed
- **Branch:** none — committed directly on `dev` as batch 9 of the 2026-09-28 audit remediation series. Carried from `docs/DATABASE_CONCURRENCY.md` §2.4, whose other three short-term items were already done; this closes the last two.
- Confirmed by reading, as the audit said. `execute()` outside a transaction ended with `void saveDatabase()`, and `saveDatabase()` was `db.export()` followed by an un-serialised `set("dumosrx_db", …)`. Two back-to-back writes therefore had two independent in-flight IndexedDB writes with no ordering guarantee between them and no version stamp on the blob, so an older image landing on top of a newer one would have been completely silent. No `pagehide`/`visibilitychange` handler existed anywhere under `lib/db/`.
- Fix: `saveDatabase()` takes its export synchronously at call time and appends the `set()` to a module-level `saveChain` promise; the actual `set()` moved into a private `persistDatabaseExport()`, so the failure handling and the `dumos_db_save_failed` rate limit are unchanged. A new `installExitFlush()`, called from `initDatabase()`'s web branch, registers `pagehide` and `visibilitychange`(`hidden`) handlers.
- **Ruling — queued snapshots coalesce instead of queueing one write each.** A plain chain fixes the ordering but makes a burst of N writes cost N full-database writes to IndexedDB, and holds N exported copies in memory at once — on the blob sizes §5 of the audit is about, that is a worse problem than the one being fixed. A save issued while another is in flight now replaces the pending snapshot, so the chain does one write of the newest image. Every caller's promise still resolves only once its data, or something strictly newer, is persisted, which is what `transaction()`'s awaited save in its `finally` actually needs.
- **Ruling — the exit flush is gated on the writer lock and on a `writeEpoch` change.** A read-only tab flushing on `pagehide` would write its stale rehydrated image over the real writer's blob — the C1 clobber the whole tab-lock design exists to prevent — so `installExitFlush()` no-ops unless `isWriterTab()`. The `hasUnpersistedWrites()` check (in-memory `writeEpoch` vs. the last persisted one) keeps a tab-switch from paying a full `db.export()` when nothing has changed, which on this codebase's persistence model is the single most expensive thing it could do on a hot path.
- **Not claimed as closed:** the flush shortens the unpersisted window, it does not eliminate it. The browser may kill the document before an async IndexedDB write completes, and `pagehide` gives no way to block on one. `docs/DATABASE_CONCURRENCY.md` §2.4 items 2 and 4 are marked fixed; items 1, 3 and 5 stay open as described there.
- Regression coverage: `client/__tests__/save-database-serialization.test.ts` — `never persists an older export on top of a newer one when two saves overlap` (a gated fake `set()` reproduces the exact interleaving), `collapses a burst of saves into the newest snapshot rather than writing each one`, `flushes the last write when the document goes away`, `does not flush from a read-only tab, which would clobber the writer's blob`, and `does not re-export on exit when everything is already persisted`. All five RED before the fix.

### A-19 — the pre-offline-first cloud CRUD endpoints were live, unused, and wrote stock outside the movement-delta model
- **Branch:** none — committed directly on `dev` as batch 9 of the 2026-09-28 audit remediation series.
- Confirmed exactly as described. `SaleController::store` did `$batch->quantity -= $deduct; $batch->save()` *and* inserted a `stock_movements` row; every device pulling that movement applies its delta locally (`pull.ts`), so the two only agreed because the server happened to skip its own delta path on this route. `index`/`show` were scoped by cashier ids rather than `store_id`, so a multi-store owner would have seen every one of their stores' sales through it. And every one of these routes wrote tenant data with no audit-log row, no correlation id and no `_version` — the three things the sync engine's conflict model depends on.
- **Deadness verified before deleting, not assumed.** Each of the 22 `ApiClient` methods in that block was grepped across `client/` and `web/`: zero call sites. `web/` issues no request to any `/app/*` path. The Android home-screen widget's `RefreshWorker.kt` — the one non-TypeScript API consumer in the repo — calls only `/dashboard/widget-snapshot`. `getPrescriptions()`/`getRecentActivity()` in that block were pointed at `/prescriptions` and `/activity`, routes that do not exist on the server at all and were being swallowed by their own `.catch(() => ({ data: [] }))`. No doc anywhere describes a separate mobile client or partner integration against these routes.
- Fix: the routes (`/app/products` + `/products/search`, the `/app/stock-batches` group, `/app/sales` + `/daily` + `/top-products`, `/app/customers`, `/app/suppliers`, `/app/categories`) are removed from `routes/api.php`, their six `Api\App` controllers are deleted, and the dead `ApiClient` block goes with them (along with `CloudListResponse` and three payload type imports that nothing else used). `/app/sync/*` and `/app/online-orders` are untouched.
- **Ruling — deleted, not feature-flagged.** The audit allowed either. A flag defaulting off still leaves the controllers, their `$batch->quantity -=` write and their cashier-scoped `index` in the tree, still maintained, still one config value away from live — which is the opposite of what "reduce attack surface" means. The external-dependency risk that would have justified a flag was checked directly (above) and is nil, and every one of these operations already has a live, invariant-respecting equivalent through the sync engine. Deletion is also reversible from git in a way a half-maintained flag is not.
- **Ruling — the read-only `/stock-movements`, `/stock-adjustments` and `/purchase-orders` routes are kept.** Their client methods were dead too and went with the rest, but the routes themselves sit outside the `/app` prefix, are GET-only, are properly `ScopesToTenant`-scoped, and are not what this finding is about (unscoped writes outside the delta model). Removing them is a separate, lower-value change.
- Tests: `laravel-server/tests/Feature/LegacyCloudCrudEndpointsRemovedTest.php` — `a_legacy_cloud_write_endpoint_no_longer_exists` (5 data sets), `a_legacy_cloud_read_endpoint_no_longer_exists` (12 data sets), `no_controller_remains_that_writes_stock_batch_quantity_outside_the_sync_engine` — 18 of its 19 cases RED before the deletion. The 19th, `the_sync_and_online_order_routes_under_the_same_prefix_still_exist`, is a guard: it was GREEN before and after, and pins that the route removal stopped where it was supposed to.
- Test suite arithmetic: `SaleControllerTest.php` and `StockBatchControllerTest.php` tested only deleted controllers and were deleted with them; `CrudFixesTest`'s four Sale cases and `TenantIsolationTest`'s Category/Supplier/Product/Customer sections went the same way (their headers now say so). `TenantIsolationTest` keeps its staff cases, and the `ScopesToTenant` trait those deleted cases exercised is still pinned by `TenantScopingArchitectureTest` and by the sync engine's own tenant-scope tests. Server suite: 507 → 480 tests (46 removed with the endpoints, 19 added).

### A-15 — nothing in CI enforced `tsc`/tests, production builds ignored TypeScript errors, and the deploy deleted the lockfile
- **Branch:** none — committed directly on `dev` as batch 9 of the 2026-09-28 audit remediation series.
- Confirmed, and the repo had no CI workflow at all: `.github/workflows/` held five workflows, every one of them a deploy or release. `client/next.config.mjs` set `typescript: { ignoreBuildErrors: true }`, and both `deploy-client.yml` and `deploy-dev.yml`'s client job ran `rm package-lock.json && npm install --legacy-peer-deps` before `npm run build` — so a production build resolved dependencies fresh from the registry every time (two deploys of the same commit could ship different transitive versions) *and* would happily ship a type error.
- Fix: a new reusable `.github/workflows/checks.yml` (`on: workflow_call` + `pull_request`) with two jobs — `client` (`npm ci --legacy-peer-deps`, `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx vitest run`) and `server` (`composer install`, `.env` + `key:generate`, `php artisan test`). `deploy-client.yml`, `deploy-backend.yml`, all three jobs of `deploy-dev.yml`, and both build jobs of `release.yml` now declare a `checks` job that `uses:` it and `needs: checks`, so a red suite stops the deploy instead of annotating it. The two `rm package-lock.json && npm install` steps became `npm ci --legacy-peer-deps`.
- **Ruling — `typescript.ignoreBuildErrors` is removed, not kept for build speed.** The audit's framing allows either. Removed, on evidence: `npm run build` was run locally with the flag off and completes clean, so it costs nothing today, and it makes "production never builds with a type error" a property of the build itself rather than of every workflow remembering to call `checks.yml`. `next build` also type-checks the generated route types that `tsc --noEmit -p .` does not, so the two are not fully redundant.
- **Ruling — a reusable workflow gated with `needs:`, not check steps pasted into each deploy.** `needs:` only works within one workflow file, so the alternative was four copies of the same six steps. `workflow_call` keeps one definition and still hard-gates, and the `pull_request` trigger gives the same signal before merge.
- **Ruling — `checks.yml` has no `push:` trigger.** `deploy-dev.yml` fires on every push to `dev` with no path filter and the `main` deploys are path-filtered per package, so a `push:` trigger here would only duplicate runs that the deploy workflows already gate. PRs are covered by `pull_request:`.
- **`deploy-web.yml` is deliberately not gated.** It deploys `web/`, which `checks.yml` does not cover, and it is also fired by the server's `repository_dispatch` storefront rebuild — gating a storefront publish on the POS client's test suite would let an unrelated red test take live storefronts offline (the exact failure mode `SF-P0-1` was about).
- **Excluded, and logged separately as `A-25`:** `npm run test:schema` (`client/scripts/verify-schema-sync.ts`) is currently broken — it exits 1 with `Could not find syncColumns in core.ts` against today's tree — so it could not be added to the gate. Fixing the script is its own change; see `docs/KNOWN_BUGS.md`.
- Verification limitation: workflow changes cannot be executed from here. All six workflow files were parsed with a YAML loader and their job graphs checked (`checks` present and depended on by every build/deploy job), `npm ci --legacy-peer-deps` was verified to resolve against the committed `client/package-lock.json` (`--dry-run`, no lockfile mismatch), and `npm run build` was run locally with `ignoreBuildErrors` off. A real CI run has not been observed.

### A-14 — "Reset App Data" never reset the database, and asked with `window.confirm`
- **Branch:** none — committed directly on `dev` as batch 9 of the 2026-09-28 audit remediation series.
- Confirmed exactly as described. The recovery button on `DatabaseProvider`'s init-failure screen ran `localStorage.clear()` and reloaded. The web/PWA database is an IndexedDB blob under `dumosrx_db` (`core.ts`, via `idb-keyval`), which `localStorage.clear()` cannot touch — so a corrupt or un-openable database survived the "reset", the same Database Error screen came straight back, and the user had lost their auth token, recent-users list, POS cart and tab-lock state for nothing. The confirmation was the native `window.confirm`, which `.agents/AGENTS.md` §9 forbids outright.
- Fix: a new `discardLocalDatabaseBlob()` in `client/lib/db/core.ts` copies the stored blob to `dumosrx_db_pre_reset_backup` and then `del()`s `dumosrx_db`; the button opens an `AlertDialog` (`components/ui/alert-dialog.tsx`, the component the rest of the app already uses) and only clears `localStorage` and reloads after that delete resolves.
- **Ruling — the blob is kept, not destroyed.** This mirrors `restoreDatabase()`'s existing one-generation `dumosrx_db_pre_restore_backup` safety net. The database being unreadable by sql.js does not mean it is unrecoverable by anything else, and the user pressing this button is by definition already in a bad state; keeping one copy costs one extra IndexedDB entry and is the difference between "support can extract the unsynced sales" and "they're gone".
- **Ruling — a failed delete aborts the whole reset instead of proceeding.** The finding's real complaint is the session being wiped for no benefit. If the `del()` rejects (another tab holding the connection, quota/permission failure), clearing `localStorage` anyway would reproduce exactly that, so the handler now toasts and returns with the session intact.
- **Ruling — `discardLocalDatabaseBlob()` is a no-op on Tauri.** There the database is a file managed by the SQL plugin, not this IndexedDB key, so there is nothing here to delete and deleting a file out from under an open pool from this screen would be a different and riskier change. Desktop/Android recovery stays what it already was (`localStorage` reset plus the existing backup/restore path). Noted rather than silently skipped: this finding is web/PWA-scoped.
- Regression coverage: `client/__tests__/db-reset-clears-indexeddb.test.ts` — `deletes the IndexedDB database key so the next boot starts fresh`, `keeps a copy of the unreadable blob under the pre-reset backup key`, `still clears the key, and reports no backup, when there was nothing stored`, `leaves the pre-restore snapshot alone — the two safety nets are separate keys`, `does nothing on the Tauri build…`; and `client/__tests__/db-error-screen-reset-dialog.test.tsx` — `does not use the native window.confirm`, `does nothing at all when the confirmation dialog is cancelled`, `deletes the IndexedDB database, not just localStorage, once confirmed`, `keeps the session rather than wiping it for nothing when the database could not be cleared`. All nine RED before the fix.

### A-24 — stray `.bak2` copy of a test file in the server test suite
- **Branch:** none — committed directly on `dev` as batch 9 of the 2026-09-28 audit remediation series (the final batch: A-14, A-15, A-19, A-20, A-22, A-23, A-24, one commit each).
- Confirmed and deleted. `laravel-server/tests/Feature/SyncPushRowLockTest.php.bak2` was byte-for-byte identical to the committed `SyncPushRowLockTest.php` it sat next to, so there was nothing in it to recover. PHPUnit never ran it: `phpunit.xml` points its Feature suite at `tests/Feature` and PHPUnit's directory scan only matches the default `*Test.php` suffix, which `.bak2` does not. Nothing in `app/`, `routes/`, `tests/`, `composer.json` or the workflows referenced the path.
- No test added — the fix is a deletion of a file that was never executed, and the server suite's count is unchanged by it (507 before and after).

### A-9 — receiving the same purchase order from two devices booked the delivery twice
- **Branch:** none — committed directly on `dev` as batch 8 of the 2026-09-28 audit remediation series. The last P2 open against the 2026-09-28 pass.
- Confirmed exactly as described, on both sides. `receivePurchaseOrder()` read the PO — including each line's `quantity_received` — *outside* its `transaction()`, so two receipts racing each other both computed the full outstanding balance from the same snapshot. Each then created a `stock_batches` row and a `stock_movements` row with fresh random ids, which the server has no way to recognise as the same delivery: both INSERTs were accepted and both movement deltas applied (on-hand stock doubled), while one of the two `quantity_received` UPDATEs lost the version check and was dropped, so the PO showed a single receipt. Nothing looks wrong until a cycle count.
- Fix, client: the line's `quantity_received` is re-read *inside* the transaction (the shape `submitStockAudit` already uses for system quantity), and the receipt's batch and movement now carry deterministic ids — `receiptBatchId(poItemId, alreadyReceived)` / `receiptMovementId(...)` in the new `client/lib/db/deterministic-id.ts`.
- Fix, server: an UPDATE setting `purchase_order_items.quantity_received` above that line's `quantity_ordered` is rejected as `quantity_received_exceeds_ordered` (both are in base units by that point — `SyncPayloadMapper` scales them together — so they are directly comparable). The reason is added to `push.ts`'s `NON_RETRYABLE_CONFLICT_REASONS`: the payload is frozen, so a receipt judged impossible stays impossible on every resend, and dropping it lets the next pull bring the server's real balance down instead of burning five retries and reporting a stuck queue item.
- **Ruling — the deterministic id is the mechanism, the server clamp is the backstop.** The audit offered "reject the `quantity_received` UPDATE *and* the accompanying batch/movement" or "key the receipt batch on a deterministic id so the second collides naturally". The first was rejected: nothing in the push loop relates one change to another — each is its own savepoint, and the batch/movement INSERTs are frequently in a *different* batch from the PO-line UPDATE — so implementing it means inventing a cross-change grouping concept the sync protocol does not have. The second needs no new server machinery at all: `push()` already converts an INSERT whose id exists into an UPDATE, and that conversion is exactly what is needed here, because an UPDATE on `stock_batches` drops `quantity` outright and an UPDATE on `stock_movements` contributes no delta. So the second device's receipt collapses onto the first's rows and the stock is booked once. The clamp is kept as a backstop for any *other* producer of an over-receipt, since it is four lines and catches what the id scheme cannot (a receipt accumulated against a balance the device never saw).
- **Ruling — the key is the PO line plus its already-received balance, not a separate receipt counter.** "Which receipt of this line is this" is fully determined by the balance it starts from, which both devices read from the same synced column, so no new column and no counter to keep in step is needed. A genuine later partial receipt starts from a different balance and correctly gets its own ids. The cost is deliberate: if device A receives 60 and syncs, and device B — not having pulled — receives 40 more, B still computes a start balance of 0 and its receipt collapses onto A's instead of being added. That is the *same* outcome B's `quantity_received` UPDATE already gets from the version check, so stock and the PO now agree rather than disagreeing by a whole delivery, and B's push reports the conflict rather than silently doubling stock. Losing an un-synced second receipt is strictly better than booking a phantom one.
- **Ruling — the ids are a salted-FNV digest, not `crypto.subtle`.** A v5 UUID wants SHA-1, but `crypto.subtle` is async and unavailable outside a secure context, and this value is needed synchronously inside a transaction. Four independently salted 32-bit FNV-1a lanes give 128 bits, formatted as a v5 UUID so it satisfies every UUID shape check in the codebase. `createAndReceivePurchaseOrder()` (Immediate Purchase) is untouched: it mints its own PO id, so two devices cannot produce the same order in the first place.
- Regression coverage, client: `client/__tests__/po-receive-no-double-booking.test.ts` — `books the delivery once when two receipts for the same PO race each other` (RED before: 2 batches, 2000 units against 1000 ordered), `keys the receipt's batch and movement on the PO line and its already-received balance` and `gives a genuine follow-up receipt against the same line its own ids` (both RED before), plus `re-reads quantity_received inside the transaction…` and a digest property case (stable, distinct, UUID-shaped, no separator-injection collision) that were GREEN before.
- **Follow-up (2026-09-28, post-review) — the separator is written `"\0"`, and the digest is pinned.** `deterministic-id.ts` originally carried the hash-input separator as a literal NUL *byte* in both `lane()`'s template literal and `deterministicId()`'s `parts.join(...)`, which made the whole file binary to git (`git diff` printed "Binary files differ", so no change to it was ever reviewable) and left the digest one editor/formatter normalisation away from silently changing. Because `receiptBatchId()`'s output is part of the cross-device sync protocol — two devices must derive byte-identical ids *forever* for the INSERT→UPDATE collapse to keep working — a changed separator would silently stop the collision and bring the double-booking back. Both are now the `"\0"` escape (identical string value, reviewable source), and `pins the exact ids a fixed PO line and balance derive, across every version of this app` asserts three hardcoded UUID literals rather than comparing the function to itself, so any future change to the separator, the salts, the lane count or the UUID formatting fails loudly.
- Regression coverage, server: `laravel-server/tests/Feature/SyncPushPurchaseOrderReceiptTest.php` — `a_quantity_received_update_beyond_quantity_ordered_is_rejected` (RED before), plus `a_second_devices_copy_of_the_same_receipt_does_not_double_the_stock` and `a_legitimate_partial_receipt_within_the_ordered_quantity_still_applies`, both GREEN before and after. That first one is deliberately a guard rather than a RED→GREEN case: it pins the existing INSERT→UPDATE collapse that the client's deterministic ids now rely on, which was previously incidental behaviour with nothing asserting it.
- Full suites for the whole of batch 8: client 251 files / 1370 tests (up from 245 / 1336), `tsc --noEmit` clean; server 507 tests / 1626 assertions (up from 482).

### A-17 — the POS cart snapshotted selling price and average cost at add-time and kept them indefinitely
- **Branch:** none — committed directly on `dev` as batch 8 of the 2026-09-28 audit remediation series.
- Confirmed exactly as described. `usePOSCartStore` is a zustand `persist` store (`pos-cart-storage` in `localStorage`), and `addToCart` copies `unit_price`/`cost_price` off the product it was given. Nothing ever revisited them, so a cart held overnight — or held across a price change synced in from the owner's device — was charged at the old shelf price and booked COGS at the old average cost through `recordSaleItemStock`.
- Fix: a pure `repriceCartFromCatalog(cart, products)` in `use-pos-cart.ts`, applied from an effect on the catalog the hook is already handed. It refreshes `original_unit_price`, `unit_price`, `cost_price` and the line subtotal, and returns the *same array reference* when nothing moved, so the effect settles in one pass and can safely depend on the cart itself. A price move (not a cost move) warns: "Price updated from the catalog: …".
- **Ruling — re-priced when the catalog arrives, not at checkout.** The audit offered either. Checkout-time is the smaller diff but the wrong place: every figure the cashier and the customer see — line subtotal, cart subtotal, VAT (charged net of discount), total, the amount tendered and the change due — is derived from these lines and is already on screen and already agreed by the time `handlePayment` runs, so correcting a price there charges an amount that was never displayed, and would have to unwind the tendered/change arithmetic to stay coherent. Correcting it the moment the new catalog lands keeps every derived figure consistent *and* gives the cashier a chance to tell the customer before taking money.
- **Ruling — a line whose product is missing from the catalog is left alone, not dropped or zeroed.** `usePOSData` returns `[]` until its query resolves, so "not in the catalog" means "still loading" far more often than it means "deleted"; re-pricing against a catalog that hasn't arrived would be worse than the staleness being fixed. The effect no-ops entirely on an empty catalog.
- **Ruling — a reseller markup is carried across as a markup, not as an absolute price.** The cashier agreed a margin over the shelf price with the reseller, and `updateUnitPrice`'s floor forbids `unit_price < original_unit_price` — which the new catalog price has just moved. So the markup amount is preserved and re-applied over the new floor (₦100 + ₦30 markup, catalog goes to ₦120 → ₦150), rather than silently becoming a discount against the new price.
- Stock is deliberately *not* re-synced onto the line here: it is read live from `products` at every add/increment already, and the oversell ledger path covers the rest. This finding is about money.
- Regression coverage: `client/__tests__/pos-cart-reprices-from-catalog.test.ts` — `re-prices a held line when the catalog's selling price has changed` (asserting the derived cart subtotal and total follow it, not just the line), `refreshes the line's cost price, so COGS is not booked at a stale average cost`, `warns the cashier once when a held line's price moved` (the second half of which pins that a re-render with the same catalog does not nag again), and `keeps a reseller markup as a markup over the new catalog floor` — all four RED before. Two guard cases, `leaves a line alone while the catalog is still loading (empty or missing product)` and `does not touch a line whose catalog price is unchanged` (asserted by object identity, which is what makes the effect terminate), were GREEN before and after.

### A-21 — `returns.user_id`/`stock_audits.user_id` fell back to the literal string `"system"`, which the server's foreign key can only ever reject
- **Branch:** none — committed directly on `dev` as batch 8 of the 2026-09-28 audit remediation series.
- Confirmed exactly as described. Both columns are `foreignUuid('user_id')->constrained('users')` server-side, so a `"system"` row wrote fine locally, pushed, failed the FK, was retried through exponential backoff and finally reported to superadmins as a permanently stuck sync item after `SYNC_FAILURE_REPORT_THRESHOLD` (5) attempts — the same failure shape already fixed once for `feedback`'s `"anonymous"`.
- Fix: both paths now throw before writing anything. `useProcessReturnMutation` rejects a return with no `userId` (the mutation's `onError` already toasts "Failed to process return"), and `submitStockAudit()` rejects a performer-less audit.
- **Ruling — throw, and no import path legitimately has "no performer".** Every call site was traced: the return dialog (`components/pos/return-dialog.tsx:143`), the cycle-count screen (`stock-audits.tsx:286`), the catalog quick edit (`catalog-list.tsx:146`) and the CSV/XLSX import dialog (`import-mapping-dialog.tsx:139`) all source the id from `useAuth().user?.id` on a route-guarded screen. The `?? null` in each is defensive, not a real anonymous mode, so there is no bulk-import case to preserve and nothing to relax.
- **Ruling — the import's check is hoisted to the top of `importProductRows()`, not left to `submitStockAudit()`.** The matched-stock reconciliation runs *after* the import's main transaction has committed (deliberately, to avoid nesting `transaction()`), so letting the throw happen there would commit every product row and only then abort on the stock phase — a worse outcome than the sentinel. `importProductRows()` now rejects up front when `updateStockForMatched` is set without a `performedBy`, before a single row is written. The existing `performed_by` on the opening-stock `stock_movements` rows is untouched: that column is genuinely nullable on both sides.
- Regression coverage: `client/__tests__/no-system-sentinel-user-id.test.ts` — `rejects a return with no user id rather than writing user_id = 'system'` and `rejects a stock audit with no performer rather than writing user_id = 'system'` (both RED before; each also asserts nothing partial survived — the sale's `payment_status` and the batch quantity are untouched), plus the two happy-path cases and a sweep asserting neither column ever holds the literal `'system'`. `client/__tests__/product-import-upsert.test.ts` gains `refuses an updateStockForMatched import with no performing user, before writing anything`, which pins the hoist rather than just the throw. One existing expectation in that file was updated: the `noopRun` re-import now passes `performedBy: "u1"`, matching what the only real caller has always sent.

### A-16 — `createPrescription` and `createProduct` were multi-row writes with no transaction, so an interruption could leave a prescription with no medications
- **Branch:** none — committed directly on `dev` as batch 8 of the 2026-09-28 audit remediation series.
- Confirmed exactly as described. `createPrescription()` inserted the header and then looped its items through separate `insert()` calls; `createProduct()` created a missing category as its own committed write before inserting the product. Every `insert()` opens and commits its own transaction (and, on the web build, its own `db.export()` to IndexedDB), so a process death between two of them — the same iOS-backgrounds-a-PWA case `base-helpers.ts`'s own `insert()` comment cites as the reason *it* wraps its three writes — committed a clinically empty prescription that was already in `_sync_queue`, so the gap propagated to every other device and could not be reconstructed from what survived. Same shape for an orphan category with no product.
- Fix: both are now a single `transaction()`, the pattern `createSale`/`receivePurchaseOrder` already use. `createPrescription`'s post-loop query invalidation stays outside the transaction (it is a cache concern, not a write).
- **Ruling — the whole of `createProduct`, including its category lookup, is inside the transaction.** Wrapping only the two writes would leave the `SELECT id FROM categories` outside, so two concurrent creates of the same new category could both miss and both create one. Neither caller (`useCreateProductMutation`, the demo seeder) runs inside a transaction already, so there is no nesting hazard — `transaction()` calls in this codebase cannot nest.
- Regression coverage: `client/__tests__/create-prescription-product-atomicity.test.ts` — `writes a prescription and all of its items, or neither`, `leaves no prescription queued for sync when its items could not be written` (this is the one that matters: an empty prescription that never enters `_sync_queue` stays a local problem), and `does not leave an orphan category behind when the product it was created for cannot be written`, all three RED before. Two happy-path cases either side of them were GREEN before and after, pinning that the wrap changed nothing else. A failing `insert()` stands in for the interruption; the observable requirement is identical.

### A-12 — every pull wrote the server's NULL `stores.last_monotonic_time` over the local one, disarming the offline clock-tamper guard after each sync
- **Branch:** none — committed directly on `dev` as batch 8 of the 2026-09-28 audit remediation series (batch 8 covers A-9, A-12, A-16, A-17 and A-21, one commit each — the five are unrelated to each other, so each is independently revertable).
- Confirmed exactly as described. `last_monotonic_time` is written only by `updateStoreMonotonicTime()` (`client/lib/db/queries/setup.ts`), a raw local `execute` that never reaches `_sync_queue`, so the server's column — which exists, added by `2026_07_21_194736_align_schema_with_client_db.php` — is permanently NULL for every store. `pull.ts` builds its UPDATE from *every* column the server returns, so each pull round wrote that NULL back and `checkLicenseStatus()` (`lib/licensing/licensing-manager.ts:59`) then found nothing to compare the local clock against and re-armed from whatever it currently said. The anti-backdating rule `.agents/AGENTS.md` §8 says must not be weakened was therefore live only between two syncs.
- Fix: a `DEVICE_LOCAL_PULL_COLUMNS` map in `pull.ts`, applied next to (and exactly like) the existing `stock_batches.quantity` exclusion — the column is dropped from the pulled row's data, the rest of the row still applies. `stores.last_monotonic_time` is its only entry today.
- **Ruling — dropped at the pull, not pushed from the client.** Making the column sync properly would be strictly worse: the audit itself notes that a device whose clock runs ahead would then propagate a future timestamp to every other device of the store and lock them all out with "Clock Discrepancy". The value is per-device state by design, so the fix is to stop the server's copy from having any say, not to give it a better one. The server column is left in place rather than dropped — a migration to remove it buys nothing now that nothing reads it into the client.
- Regression coverage: `client/__tests__/pull-preserves-device-local-columns.test.ts` — `keeps the local stores.last_monotonic_time when the server's copy is NULL` (which also asserts the rest of the pulled `stores` row still applies, so the exclusion stays column-scoped), `does not let another device's clock propagate a future monotonic time`, and `does not write a device-local column when the pull INSERTs a store row this device has never seen`. All three RED before the fix.

### A-10 + A-11 + A-13 — any platform config key was world-readable, two unauthenticated POSTs were unmetered, PIN-only staff had a 4-digit `/login` password, and the Tauri webview ran with no CSP
- **Branch:** none — committed directly on `dev` as batch 7 of the 2026-09-28 audit remediation series. Fixed together because they are the audit's own grouped remediation item ("server public-surface hardening and the Tauri CSP"); the three are independent of each other and of every other open finding.

**A-10 — the public read endpoint, and the two unmetered public writes.**
- Confirmed exactly as described, and slightly worse than the audit's list. `SystemConfigController::show()` was `SystemConfig::getVal($key)` with no filtering at all, and its sibling `PUT /admin/system-configs/{key}` stores arbitrary JSON under arbitrary keys — so the public GET was a read primitive over the whole `system_configs` table, not just the four keys the audit named. Beyond `referral_program`, `default_account_manager_id` and `storefront_rebuild_requested_at`, `SyncSubaccountFeeRates` also writes per-store `…failure_count` keys through the same model, and `storefront_platform_fee_percentage` (the platform's commission rate) was readable by anyone.
- **Ruling — an allow-list plus a `super_admin` bypass, not an allow-list alone.** The audit's three "legitimately needed" keys were incomplete: the marketing site also reads `smartsupp_key` (chat widget) and `social_links` (footer) from logged-out pages, so `PUBLIC_KEYS` is those five. But `web/`'s admin panel reads `default_account_manager_id` and `storefront_platform_fee_percentage` through this *same* public route (`web/app/admin/system/default-account-manager-card.tsx`, `subscription-config-tab.tsx`), and a bare allow-list would have broken both config tabs. Rather than widen the public list to keep them working — which is the bug — `show()` now resolves a bearer token explicitly (`$request->user('sanctum')`; the route sits outside `auth:sanctum` because it must answer before login) and lets `super_admin`, the same role `update()` is already gated on, read anything. The admin panel attaches its token on `/admin` paths already, so no client change was needed. One deliberate narrowing: a non-`super_admin` platform admin (`permission:manage_platform` but not the role) could previously *read* those two values and will now get a 404 on them. That matches reality — `PUT /admin/system-configs/{key}` has always been `role:super_admin`, so they could never save the form they were being shown.
- **Ruling — 404, not 403.** A 403 would confirm that a key exists but is privileged, turning the endpoint back into an enumeration oracle for admin-only config names; 404 makes "not allow-listed" and "never stored" the same answer. It also matches how the rest of this codebase hides things a caller has no business seeing — `TenantIsolationTest` pins 404 (not 403) on every cross-tenant show/update/destroy.
- **Limiters.** Three new named limiters in `AppServiceProvider`, matching the existing `storefront-*` pattern: `public-read` (120/min/IP, the config GET — generous, because a whole shop behind one NATed IP launches together), `public-write` (5/min/IP, `POST /support`, which persists a `Feedback` row *and* emails every platform admin), `client-error-log` (30/min/IP — above what a genuinely broken device bursts at, since an offline sync fails per batch, far below what it takes to fill a shared host's disk with `laravel.log` lines).
- **Payload cap.** `details` was `nullable|array` with no ceiling. Laravel's `max:` on an array counts *elements*, which is no defence against one key holding a megabyte, so the cap is on encoded size: a new `App\Rules\EncodedSizeAtMost` (8 KiB for `details`), plus plain `max:` on `method` (16), `url` (2048), `message` (2000) and `deviceId` (128) — every one of which also reaches `laravel.log`. Rejected as a 422 rather than truncated: a report too large to be a real one is not a report worth half-keeping, and truncation would silently hide that a client is misbehaving.

**A-11 — the PIN-derived web password.**
- Confirmed on both paths: `StaffController::store()` (`$pin = $request->pin ?: '1234'; Hash::make($pin)`) and the sync-push `users` INSERT in `SyncController` (which the git history shows had already been *patched once* to fall back to `'1234'` when the pushed PIN turned out to be pre-hashed — preserving the hole rather than closing it). `AuthenticatesSessions::login()` authenticates any `is_active` user, so both produced a live Sanctum credential with a 10,000-value keyspace on the `<username>@local.dumosrx.com` address the same method generates two lines earlier.
- **Ruling — `password = null`, not "require a real password".** Requiring one would be a behaviour change for the product: the staff form documents the field as optional and the API docs say to treat staff as PIN-first, so rejecting creation without one turns a security fix into a workflow break for every owner adding a cashier. Null keeps "PIN-only staff" as the default state and simply removes the credential. `users.password` is now nullable (`2026_09_28_100000_make_users_password_nullable.php`), and `login()` rejects a null/empty-password account *before* `Hash::check()` — both because that is the actual rule and because `password_verify()` against null is a PHP deprecation rather than a clean false.
- **Verified the POS never uses `/login` for staff, before changing anything.** The client's only call to it is `linkCloudAccount(email, password)` in `auth-context.tsx` — the store owner's cloud-account link, reached from onboarding and the Settings cloud-link dialog, with a real email and a real password. Staff PIN unlock is entirely client-side: `verifyPin()` checks the typed PIN against the bcrypt hash in the local `users` table (`lib/utils/pin-hash.ts`), which the sync pull ships down. So a staff account with no password loses nothing the POS uses. `StaffPinDerivedPasswordTest::the_pos_still_receives_a_password_less_staff_members_pin_hash` drives that pull and asserts the hash arrives intact, and `…the_store_owners_cloud_login_is_unaffected` pins the one `/login` the POS does make.
- **Not done: nulling existing derived passwords.** The column cannot distinguish a PIN-derived password from a real one an owner typed, so a blanket backfill would lock real web users out. Existing staff rows keep whatever they have; the fix stops new ones being minted. Anyone wanting the historical ones cleaned up needs a targeted pass (staff rows with a `@local.dumosrx.com` email whose password verifies against their own PIN, or against `1234`), which is an ops action, not a migration.
- The one pre-existing assertion that pinned the old behaviour — `PinHashingTest::creating_staff_stores_the_pin_hashed`'s `assertTrue(Hash::check('4321', $staff->password))` — now asserts `assertNull($staff->password)` and points at the new file.

**A-13 — the Tauri CSP.**
- **The policy:** `default-src 'self'; script-src 'self' 'wasm-unsafe-eval'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self' data: blob: https:; font-src 'self' data:; media-src 'self' blob:; worker-src 'self' blob:; connect-src 'self' ipc: http://ipc.localhost https: http://localhost:* http://127.0.0.1:* http://10.0.2.2:* ws://localhost:* http://dumosrx.test; frame-src 'none'; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'self'; form-action 'self'`. Every source was derived from what the client actually loads, not pasted from a template — the per-directive rationale is in `client/AGENTS.md` under "Tauri webview content-security policy".
- **`script-src` needs no `'unsafe-inline'`, and that was checked against Tauri's source rather than assumed.** `tauri-codegen`'s `inject_script_hashes()` sha256-hashes every non-empty inline `<script>` in each exported HTML file at build time and `set_csp()` appends those hashes to the directive per page — which covers Next's static `self.__next_f.push(...)` payload scripts (94 of them across the 47 pages in `out/`). Tauri's nonce injection only targets `script[src^='http']`, and the static export has none, so no nonce is added either.
- **`style-src 'unsafe-inline'` is load-bearing and fragile in a non-obvious way.** `components/ui/chart.tsx` writes a `<style>` via `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`, React 19 hoists `<style precedence>`, and Radix's scroll-lock injects its own. Under CSP 3, a nonce *or* hash in a directive makes `'unsafe-inline'` ignored — and Tauri adds a style nonce whenever the built HTML contains a `<style>` element. It contains zero today (verified across all 47 exported files), which is the only reason this works; a future build-time inline `<style>` would silently disable `'unsafe-inline'` and break every runtime-injected stylesheet. That trap is written down in `client/AGENTS.md` rather than left to be rediscovered.
- **Ruling — `withGlobalTauri` dropped, not kept.** Both readers of `window.__TAURI__` (`isTauri()` in `lib/db/core.ts` and the platform tag in `lib/utils/error-logger.ts`) are already written `window.__TAURI__ !== undefined || window.__TAURI_INTERNALS__ !== undefined`, and Tauri v2 injects `__TAURI_INTERNALS__` regardless of the flag.
