Nothing else in the client, the Android widget code or the build scripts touches the global, so removing it costs nothing and takes the whole plugin API out of an injected script's reach.
- **`connect-src` is deliberately the loose one.** `components/ui/server-selector.tsx` repoints the API host at runtime (production, staging, two local-dev hosts, the Android emulator's `10.0.2.2`, plus a dynamically-discovered host IP for wireless debugging), so pinning it to a fixed list would break development. `https:` plus the localhost/emulator entries is what that supports. The directive that matters for this finding is `script-src`: the threat model is an injected script pulling remote code, and that is closed.
- **Scope of the CSP, which is narrower than it looks.** Tauri applies it to assets served over its own protocol, so it covers the bundled desktop/Android build only. `npm run tauri dev` loads `devUrl` from the Next dev server and is unaffected (Next's HMR `eval` keeps working), and the web/PWA build at `app.dumosrx.com` is served by a plain host and is not covered at all.
- Regression coverage: `client/__tests__/tauri-csp-config.test.ts` (new, 9 cases) reads `tauri.conf.json` and asserts the CSP is a real string, that `script-src` contains no `'unsafe-inline'`/`'unsafe-eval'`/`http*` source but does contain `'wasm-unsafe-eval'`, that `style-src`/`worker-src`/`img-src`/`connect-src` carry the sources named above, that `object-src`/`frame-src` are `'none'`, and that `withGlobalTauri` is gone. All 9 RED before the change.

**Coverage, verification and caveats.**
- New server tests: `laravel-server/tests/Feature/PublicSurfaceHardeningTest.php` (11 cases) — `a_public_config_key_is_still_readable_without_auth`, `a_non_public_config_key_is_not_readable_without_auth`, `an_unknown_config_key_is_not_readable_without_auth`, `a_store_owner_token_does_not_unlock_a_non_public_key`, `a_super_admin_can_still_read_any_config_key`, `the_public_write_routes_carry_a_named_limiter`, `support_submission_returns_429_once_the_limiter_is_exhausted`, `client_error_logging_returns_429_once_the_limiter_is_exhausted`, `an_oversized_client_error_details_payload_is_rejected`, `an_oversized_client_error_message_is_rejected`, `a_normal_client_error_report_is_still_accepted` — 8 RED before. Like `StorefrontThrottleTest`, it deliberately does **not** disable `ThrottleRequests`.
- `laravel-server/tests/Feature/StaffPinDerivedPasswordTest.php` (11 cases) — the two creation paths (`staff_created_with_a_pin_gets_no_login_password`, `…with_no_pin_and_no_password…`, `a_synced_offline_created_staff_row_gets_no_login_password`, `…with_an_already_hashed_pin…`), the `/login` refusals (`a_pin_only_staff_account_cannot_log_in_with_its_pin`, `a_pin_less_staff_account_cannot_log_in_with_the_literal_1234`, `an_empty_password_never_authenticates_a_null_password_account`), and the four things that must keep working (`staff_created_with_a_real_password_can_still_log_in`, `setting_a_password_later_restores_web_login`, `the_pos_still_receives_a_password_less_staff_members_pin_hash`, `the_store_owners_cloud_login_is_unaffected`) — 9 RED before.
- Full suites: server 504 passed / 1612 assertions (482 / 1548 before, +22); client 246 files / 1345 tests passing (245 / 1336 before, +1 file, +9 tests), `tsc --noEmit` clean. `cargo check` in `client/src-tauri` passes, which is what validates the new `tauri.conf.json` against the config schema.
- **Manual smoke test still owed on the desktop/Android build.** `cargo check` proves the config parses, not that the policy lets the app run: nothing here booted a bundled webview. Before trusting this in production, install a real build and exercise (a) POS load and a sale, (b) generating and **printing** a PDF report — `openBlobForPrint()` does `window.open(blobURL)`, and a `blob:` document inherits the opener's CSP, which is the single most likely thing to misbehave, (c) the barcode-scanning UI, (d) a chart screen, which is the `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` `<style>` path `style-src 'unsafe-inline'` exists for, (e) opening and closing a dialog, for Radix's runtime scroll-lock `<style>`, and (f) sync against a server picked in the server selector. If anything is blocked, the browser console names the exact directive.

### A-7 + A-8 + A-18 — launch was gated behind a full network sync, every pull paid a 26-subquery `stores` prune, every boot scanned all 26 tables, and a `COUNT(*)` of the sync queue ran every 5 seconds
- **Branch:** none — committed directly on `dev` as batch 6 of the 2026-09-28 audit remediation series. Fixed together because they are the audit's own grouped remediation item: the three costs a low-end tablet pays at launch and at idle, none of which does any work in its steady state.
- **A-7 (launch gate).** `LicenseGuard.performCheck()` awaited `Promise.race([sync(true), 5 s timeout])` — a full push+pull round — while rendering `<SplashScreen/>`, so every online launch was up to five seconds slower than the same launch offline, on a product whose stated value is that every screen works offline. Its effect depends on `storeProfile?.status/suspension_reason/subscription_tier`, all `undefined` on the first render, so the profile resolving fired `performCheck()` a second time and with it a second `sync(true)`. `StoreProvider` fired a third `sync()` on mount. Confirmed exactly as the audit described it, and measured in the new test: three `sync()` calls per launch before the fix.
- Fix: the license is evaluated from the local `stores` table alone, so `performCheck()` no longer touches the network — it reads local state and paints. The launch sync has exactly one owner, `StoreProvider`'s existing mount/online trigger, which already refetches the profile and runs the 24 h-gated `checkSyncHealth()`; `LicenseGuard` reacts to its result instead of blocking on it, via `storeProfile` (already an effect dependency) plus a new `dumos_sync_completed` listener for a renewal that only moved the expiry date. The 5 s race survives on one path only: `performCheck({ refreshFromCloud: true })`, which the lock screen's own "Check Again" / "Refresh Account Status" buttons call, where waiting *is* the interaction and the splash is the expected feedback.
- **Ruling — `StoreProvider`, not the `SyncIndicator` daemon, owns the launch sync.** The audit offered either. `SyncIndicator` is a dashboard chrome component mounted in several places at once (sidebar, mobile header, drawer) and is unmounted entirely on `/login` and `/setup`; its daemon is a *steady-state* scheduler (instant-on-change or every N minutes, per `auto_sync_interval`) and is disabled outright on a throttled tier, an impersonated session or a read-only tab. A launch sync that only happens when a particular piece of chrome is on screen is not a launch sync. `StoreProvider` wraps the whole app, already owns the `online` re-trigger and the profile refetch, and is where the mount-time sync already lived.
- **Ruling — the launch sync is no longer `isManual`.** Dropping `sync(true)` in favour of `StoreProvider`'s `sync()` puts the launch round back under the plan-tier `sync_interval` throttle and per-item backoff that batch 5 (A-5) restored. That is the point of A-5: only a real "Sync Now" click bypasses them.
- **Ruling — `syncSubscriptionStatus()` on mount was left in place.** It is a single lightweight status call, not a push+pull round, and it exists precisely to work when full cloud sync is gated off by tier — deduplicating it into the launch sync would reintroduce the gap it was written to close.
- **A-8 (per-pull prune).** `stores` is always pulled as a full snapshot, so the reconciliation prune ran on **every** pull round: a `PRAGMA table_info` probe per store-scoped table, then one `UPDATE` carrying a `NOT IN (SELECT DISTINCT store_id FROM <table> WHERE store_id IS NOT NULL)` clause per table — 26 full scans of `sales`, `sale_items`, `stock_movements`, `audit_logs` and the rest — to decide something that in the steady state (the server returning exactly the stores this device already has) could not possibly match a row.
- Fix: resolve the prune's candidates first — `SELECT id FROM stores WHERE _deleted = 0 AND id NOT IN (…)`, a primary-key lookup on a table with a handful of rows — and only build and run the expensive statement when that set is non-empty. The statement itself now targets `id IN (candidates)` rather than re-deriving them, so its "never prune a store with real local data attached" clauses are unchanged and still checked against every store-scoped table.
- **A-8 (boot orphan scan).** `DatabaseProvider` ran `requeueOrphanedRows(STORE_SCOPED_TABLES)` on every boot: `SELECT * FROM <table> WHERE (_synced = 0 …) AND id NOT IN (SELECT record_id FROM _sync_queue WHERE table_name = ?)` per table. Batch 3 (A-6) indexed the `_sync_queue(table_name, record_id)` side, which was the audit's other recommendation, but `_synced` is not indexed and cannot usefully be — so this stayed a full scan of all 26 tables at every launch.
- Fix: a new `requeueOrphanedRowsOnce()` runs the scan once per install (marker `dumos_orphan_requeue_v1` in `localStorage`), plus whenever this launch found crashes queued by a previous session (`dumosrx_pending_crashes`, read synchronously in `DatabaseProvider` *before* `flushPendingCrashes()` clears it). `requeueOrphanedRows()` itself is untouched and still runs unconditionally from `remapForeignKey()`, where it repairs a state that call just created.
- **Ruling — not `_synced` indexing, and not "every boot but cheaper".** The state this scan repairs is a row INSERT that never got its `_sync_queue` INSERT, which `base-helpers.ts` has done atomically since the fix that added this call. So there are exactly two moments it can find anything: the first boot after upgrading into that fix, and the first boot after a process death mid-write. Both are covered; an index would have made a scan that finds nothing cheaper without making it necessary.
- **A-18 (5 s poll).** `useActionCenterAlerts` polled `queryKeys.sync.queueCount()` at `refetchInterval: 5000`. `SyncIndicator` uses the same query key at 30 s, and React Query applies the *shortest* interval registered for a key — so while the dashboard was mounted, the whole app ran a `COUNT(*)` against main-thread sql.js every 5 seconds, each tick taking the connection-wide lock (`reserveDbSlot`) and queueing behind any in-flight import or sync transaction. This is the exact poll `SyncIndicator`'s own comment says was removed there.
- Fix: the hook now subscribes to `addSyncQueueChangeListener` — the event path `SyncIndicator` already uses — and keeps `refetchInterval: 30000`, matching the other consumer of the key so neither can silently speed up the other. The 30 s poll is retained rather than removed: it is the safety net for a queue drain that completes without emitting a change event (a push clearing rows from another tab), which is what makes the count correct rather than merely fresh.
- Regression coverage: `client/__tests__/launch-not-gated-on-sync.test.tsx` — `paints the app without waiting for sync() to resolve` (RED before: with a `sync()` that never settles, the splash never cleared) and `fires exactly one mount-time sync for the whole launch` (RED before: 3 calls), plus `still re-evaluates the license after the background sync lands`, which pins that dropping the gate did not drop the refresh. `client/__tests__/store-prune-skipped-when-unchanged.test.ts` — `does not run the 26-subquery prune when the server's store set matches the local one` and `does not run the prune when the only local store missing from the snapshot is already soft-deleted` (both RED before), plus `still runs the prune when the server's snapshot drops a store this device has`. `client/__tests__/orphan-requeue-runs-once-per-install.test.ts` — `does not scan again on a later boot of the same install`, with the first-boot and crash-forced cases either side of it (all three RED before: the API did not exist). The four existing `store-prune-safety.test.ts` cases, which are the reason the prune's data-attached guard exists at all, were GREEN before and after.
- One existing expectation was updated, for the prune's changed parameterisation only: `sync-engine.test.ts`'s `prunes a local store not present in a stores pull response` now supplies a candidate row to the mocked `query()` and asserts the `UPDATE` is bound to the resolved candidate ids instead of to every id the server returned. No test expectation about prune *behaviour* changed.
- Full suites: client 245 files / 1336 tests (242 / 1328 before this batch, +3 files, +8 tests), `tsc --noEmit` clean; server 482 passed / 1548 assertions, unaffected as expected (client-only change).

### A-5 — every background sync claimed to be a user-clicked "Sync Now", so the plan-tier sync-interval throttle was decorative and per-item backoff never applied
- **Branch:** none — committed directly on `dev` as batch 5 of the 2026-09-28 audit remediation series.
- `SyncIndicator`'s auto-sync daemon — both modes, the instant sync-queue listener and the N-minute poll — called `handleManualSync()`, the button's own handler, which calls `sync(true)`. `isManual` is load-bearing in two places: `getPendingSyncItems(isManual)` (`base-helpers.ts:425`) returns the *whole* queue instead of only rows whose `next_retry_at` has elapsed, and `apiClient.pushChanges/pullChanges` append `?manual=1`, which `SyncController::validateSync` treats as "skip this plan tier's `sync_interval`". So every request any device ever sent carried `manual=1`: the 180 min / 30 min / 0 per-tier limit in `SystemConfigSeeder` applied to nobody, and a permanently-failing queue item (unknown column, FK violation) was re-sent on every cycle — every 2 s of activity in instant mode — instead of backing off. Confirmed exactly as the audit described it.
- **Ruling — the interval is treated as a real plan rule, and is now actually enforced.** Nothing in the seeder, the API docs or either `AGENTS.md` describes it as advisory; it sits in `limits` beside `stores` and `staff` (both hard-enforced in this same method), it is per tier and monotonic with price, `SYNC_THROTTLED` has a user-facing "upgrade your plan for faster sync" message, and `client/lib/hooks/use-feature-gate.ts` surfaces it to the UI as a plan limit. Silently deleting a monetization gate is the bigger judgment call, so it was fixed rather than removed. What was *not* changed: an explicit "Sync Now" click still sends `manual=1` and still bypasses both the interval and per-item backoff — that is the audit's own recommendation ("keep `manual=1` for the button only"), it is human-rate-limited and still subject to the API's 60 req/min limiter, and `SyncValidationTest::the_manual_flag_bypasses_the_sync_interval_throttle` continues to assert it.
- **The latent bug the audit flagged was real and was fixed first.** With the throttle actually live, one `sync()` call is many requests — pushes go out in 50-change batches paced ~1.1 s apart, pulls page — and the first push stamps `stores.last_sync_at` via `touchStoreLastSyncAt()`. Batch 2 then arrived with `minutesSinceLastSync === 0` and was rejected `SYNC_THROTTLED`; the pull side's same-minute exemption (`!$isPush && $minutesSinceLastSync === 0`) never covered the push side. Verified red before the fix: `later batches of the same push run are not throttled by their own first batch` failed with 429 against the pre-fix controller.
- **Per-run exemption mechanism: a client-generated run token.** The existing payload had nothing reusable — batches are sliced by index with no sequence number in the request, `X-Device-Id` is per install (not per run) and pull's `page_offset`/`page_cursor` are per table — so `sync()` now mints one id per call (`crypto.randomUUID`, with a timestamp+random fallback for insecure origins/old webviews) and every push batch and pull page of that run sends it as `X-Sync-Run-Id`. `validateSync()` measures the interval **per run, not per request**: the run's first request is checked normally, and later requests carrying the same id skip the check. The token is persisted on the store row (`stores.last_sync_run_id` + `last_sync_run_started_at`, new migration `2026_09_28_000000_add_sync_run_columns_to_stores_table.php`) rather than in the cache, so it does not depend on the cache driver surviving between requests, and it is honoured for at most `SYNC_RUN_MAX_MINUTES` (10) — comfortably longer than any real run, far shorter than the smallest configured interval (30 min) — so a client that pinned one id forever buys one run's exemption, not a standing bypass. The header value is length- and charset-capped before it is stored, both new columns are out of `$fillable`, and both are in `STORE_SYNC_FORBIDDEN_FIELDS` so no sync payload can write them.
- Client fix: `SyncIndicator`'s handler became `runSync(isUserInitiated)`; the daemon's two call sites pass `false`, the button and card keep `handleManualSync` (`runSync(true)`). A background run is also quiet — no "Sync completed successfully" toast for a sync nobody asked for, and a plan-restriction rejection (`isExpectedSyncRestriction`) leaves the indicator on "online" instead of showing "Sync Error", since on a throttled tier that rejection is the expected steady state. `pushChanges` additionally re-throws a plan restriction instead of routing it through `recordSyncFailure`: now that a background push can genuinely be throttled, running a 429 through the per-item backoff path would burn every queued item's 5-attempt budget and then report a perfectly healthy queue as "stuck".
- The backoff half needed no code change — `getPendingSyncItems(false)` and `withheldRecordsWithBackedOffSiblingsRemoved()` were always correct — only a caller that stopped lying about being manual.
- Regression coverage. Server, `laravel-server/tests/Feature/SyncRunThrottleTest.php` (new): `a background push with no manual flag is throttled inside the interval`, `later batches of the same push run are not throttled by their own first batch` (RED before: 429 on batch 2), `a new run immediately after a completed one is still throttled`, `a run id held open past the run window stops exempting anything`, `a push with no run id at all still behaves exactly as before`. Client: `sync-indicator-background-sync-is-not-manual.test.ts` (new) — `the instant-mode (interval 0) daemon syncs with manual = false` and `the polling daemon syncs with manual = false` (both RED before: `true`), plus `the Sync Now button still syncs with manual = true`; `push-background-backs-off-failing-item.test.ts` (new) — `re-sends the item once, then holds it back on the next background cycle` and `an explicit manual sync still bypasses that backoff`, driving a real sql.js queue across repeated cycles.
- Four existing tests asserted `apiClient.pushChanges`/`pullChanges` call arguments exactly and were updated for the new trailing `runId` parameter (`sync-engine.test.ts`, `pull-paginates-past-500-rows.test.ts`, `pull-resumes-across-rounds.test.ts`, `pull-full-fetches-categories-suppliers.test.ts`); no behaviour in them changed.
- Full suites: client 241 files / 1325 tests passing (+5), `tsc --noEmit` clean; server 482 passed / 1548 assertions (+5).

### A-4 — a fulfilled online order was recorded under `sales.cashier_id`, so it had no cashier at all on the device that fulfilled it
- **Branch:** none — committed directly on `dev` as batch 4 of the 2026-09-28 audit remediation series.
- The local `sales` table carries both `user_id` — the column every cashier-attributed read uses (`getRecentSales` filters and joins on `s.user_id`, `getTopStaffByDate`, the dashboard activity feed and `cashierPerformance` in `reports.ts`, and `useMyTodaySales` through `getRecentSales`) — and a migration-added `cashier_id` (`schema-migrations.ts:209`), which exists only so a pulled row does not fail on an unknown column. `use-fulfill-online-order-mutation.ts` wrote the cashier into `cashier_id` and left `user_id` NULL, so on the fulfilling device the sale belonged to nobody: missing from "My Sales Today"/"My Transactions Today", missing from Recent Sales when scoped to a cashier, and uncounted in the staff-performance report. After the sale round-tripped through the server the pull mapping (`SyncController::mapPullRowForClient`, `cashier_id → user_id`) restored it, so *other* devices showed the correct cashier — the same sale attributed differently per terminal. Confirmed exactly as the audit described it.
- The same insert also wrote `payment_status: "paid"`, which is not in the client's vocabulary (`completed | pending | partial | refunded | partially_refunded`), leaving the row unmatched by any status filter keyed on those values.
- Fix: the insert now writes `user_id: cashierId` and `payment_status: "completed"`, matching what `use-pos-payment.ts` writes for an ordinary POS sale. Two lines; no schema change and no data migration (the column was already there and already the one everything reads).
- **Ruling — the pull-side `cashier_id → user_id` mapping is left in place, and `cashier_id` is not dropped from the local schema.** `cashier_id` is the *server's* real column name on `sales` (`app/Models/Sale.php`, the `2024_01_20_000005` migration, and every server-side report: `SaleController`, `DashboardService`, `AdminStoreService`), and the sync engine translates in both directions — `mapPullRowForClient()` maps `cashier_id → user_id` on the way down, and push maps `user_id → cashier_id` on the way up (`SyncController.php:1478`). So the mapping is load-bearing for every pulled sale, not a workaround for this bug, and the local column still receives pulled values. Only the local write path was wrong.
- **No server-side counterpart.** The server's own fulfilment endpoint (`Api/OnlineOrderController`) only transitions `online_orders.payment_status` — a different table with its own vocabulary in which `paid` is correct — and never writes a `sales` row. Nothing on the server needed changing; its suite is unchanged and was run as a sanity check.
- Regression coverage: `client/__tests__/use-fulfill-online-order-mutation.test.ts` — `attributes the sale to the cashier via sales.user_id, the column every report reads` (RED before: `user_id` was `null`) and `shows the fulfilled order in the fulfilling device's own cashier-scoped sales query`, which drives the real `getRecentSales("cashier1")` rather than re-asserting the column (RED before: the query returned 0 rows). The existing `inserts a valid sales row …` case's `payment_status` expectation moved `"paid"` → `"completed"` and was RED before the fix. All three run against a real sql.js database with the app's own schema plus the sync-column migrations, as the rest of that file does.
- The two new cases are appended at the end of the file deliberately: inserted before `retrying after a server failure does not record a second local sale or deduct stock twice`, they made that pre-existing case go red on a stale `result.current` after its second, successful `mutateAsync` — an order-sensitivity in that test's render/`mockRejectedValueOnce` interaction that predates this change and is unrelated to it (it reproduces with the fix reverted). Not fixed here; noted so the next person does not rediscover it.
- Full suites: client 239 files / 1320 tests passing (239 / 1318 before, +2 tests), `tsc --noEmit` clean; server 477 passed / 1534 assertions, unchanged and untouched.

### A-6 — the local SQLite database had one index, so every hot read path on a mature store was a full-table or correlated scan
- **Branch:** none — committed directly on `dev` as batch 3 of the 2026-09-28 audit remediation series.
- `SCHEMA_SQL` declared exactly one index, `idx_stock_batches_product_id`. Everything else read without one: `getRecentSales()`'s three correlated subqueries per row over `sale_items`/`returns`, `getCustomers()`/`getCustomerTotalSpent()`, the activity log's `COUNT(*)` + `ORDER BY created_at` over `audit_logs`, `getProductHistory()` by `record_id`/`product_id`, the product import's per-row barcode/name lookups, the dashboard movements feed, `getFastMovers()`'s correlated returns subqueries, `pull.ts`'s per-pulled-record `_sync_queue` probe, and `reconcile-identity.ts`'s boot-time orphan scan. sql.js runs on the main thread, so on the web/PWA build each of those blocks paint for its full duration.
- Fix: fifteen new indexes in a single `READ_PATH_INDEXES` list in `client/lib/db/schema-migrations.ts`, applied by `ensureReadPathIndexes()` as the last step of `runSchemaMigrations()` — `sale_items(sale_id)`, `returns(sale_id)`, `return_items(product_id)`, `sales(customer_id)`, `sales(created_at)`, `sales(store_id, created_at)`, `stock_movements(product_id)`, `stock_movements(reference_id)`, `stock_movements(store_id, created_at)`, `audit_logs(record_id)`, `audit_logs(store_id, created_at)`, `products(barcode)`, `products(name)`, `products(store_id, barcode)`, `_sync_queue(table_name, record_id)`. Per-index rationale, and which query each one serves, is in `docs/LOCAL_DB_INDEXES.md`.
- **Ruling — the indexes live only in the migration step, not in `SCHEMA_SQL`.** The audit (and the obvious reading of the existing `ensureStockBatchesProductIndex` pattern) called for both. Putting them in `SCHEMA_SQL` is actively wrong: `core.ts` hands that whole string to sql.js as one `db.run()`, and a `CREATE INDEX` naming a column an old local table has not been migrated to yet throws and silently skips every statement after it, including `CREATE TABLE`s. `store_id` is migration-added and absent from most of `SCHEMA_SQL`'s own `CREATE TABLE` bodies, so this is not hypothetical — it was caught by `init-database-migrations.test.ts` going red against a legacy `products(id, name)` table the moment `idx_products_barcode` was added to the schema blob. `runSchemaMigrations()` runs on fresh installs too, and after the column migrations, so both populations converge on the same set with no ordering hazard, and `tryRun` keeps one un-buildable index from taking the other fourteen with it. A comment in `schema.ts` points at the list so nobody "fixes" this back.
- **Ruling — nothing is `UNIQUE`, including `products(barcode)`.** Barcodes are not unique today and the app does not assume they are: every lookup is written `… LIMIT 1`, import de-duplication is deliberately app-level (union-find in `product-import.ts`), and a multi-store device holds several catalogs in one table so the same barcode appears once per store by design. Worse, a `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX` that fails on already-violating data would be swallowed by `tryRun`, so the index would silently not exist on exactly the mature databases this work targets.
- **Ruling — `store_id` is never indexed alone.** A single-store device has one `store_id` for every row, so the index matches everything and the planner still picks it: measured, a standalone `idx_products_store_id` made the import's `WHERE barcode = ? AND store_id = ?` plan as `SEARCH products USING INDEX idx_products_store_id (store_id=?)` and still walk the whole catalog. It now only ever appears as a composite's leading column.
- **The `ANALYZE` step is load-bearing — without it the indexes made one query 3× *slower* than having none.** With no `sqlite_stat1`, SQLite plans from built-in row-count guesses, and on the synthetic one-year store it drove `getCustomers()`'s 2,000-customer join off `idx_sales_store_id_created_at` (which matches every sale on a single-store device) instead of `idx_sales_customer_id`: **10.2 s unindexed became 30.5 s indexed.** A full `ANALYZE` makes the same query 55 ms. Sampling (`PRAGMA analysis_limit = 400`) is 14× cheaper — 6.8 ms vs 94 ms — but leaves the activity log's `COUNT(*)` preferring a 200,000-row index scan over the table scan that wins (100 ms vs 22 ms), so the stats are built in full and instead rebuilt rarely: `areTableStatsStale()` compares the row count `sqlite_stat1` recorded for `sales` against the live count and re-runs when the table has grown 4× past it. That gate also covers the fresh install, which necessarily analyses an empty database and would otherwise keep those stats for the life of the device. On the web build the one-off `ANALYZE` is persisted through the same `saveDatabase` callback `clearLegacyTransactionsOnce` already used (its parameter is renamed `onLegacyCleared` → `persist` now that two steps share it).
- **Measured** (sql.js, desktop Node; 5k products, 2k customers, 50k sales, ~100k sale items, 50k movements, 3k returns, 200k audit rows, 15k queued rows — an entry-level Android tablet is 5–20× slower): `getRecentSales()` 772 ms → **0.7 ms**; `getCustomers()` 10,194 ms → **55 ms**; activity-log page 31 ms → **0.2 ms**; `getProductHistory()` 14 ms → **0.2 ms**; the `_sync_queue` probe ×500 20 ms → **7.5 ms**; the import's barcode lookup ×200 5.8 ms → **3.6 ms**; boot-time orphan scan 11 ms → **9.0 ms**. The activity log's `COUNT(*)` (22 ms) and the dashboard movements feed (12 ms → 15 ms, within noise) are unchanged — the latter's `reference_type NOT IN (…)` predicate is not indexable. Full table in `docs/LOCAL_DB_INDEXES.md`.
- Regression coverage: `client/__tests__/schema-indexes.test.ts` — `creates every hot-read-path index on a fresh install` and `adds them to an existing database that predates them` (both RED before: only `idx_stock_batches_product_id` existed in either population), eight `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN` assertions that each hot query reaches its index rather than `SCAN`ing (all RED before), `analyzes so the planner has real stats to choose between the new indexes`, `re-analyzes once the store has outgrown the stats the last pass recorded`, and `does not make products.barcode unique — duplicate barcodes stay insertable`, which pins the ruling above.
- Full suites: client 239 files / 1318 tests passing (238 / 1305 before, +1 file, +13 tests), `tsc --noEmit` clean; server 477 passed / 1534 assertions, unchanged and untouched by this batch (client-only change, run as a sanity check). `npm run test:schema` fails identically before and after this change (`Could not find syncColumns in core.ts` — the script still expects `syncColumns` in `core.ts`, which the `schema-migrations.ts` extraction moved); pre-existing and unrelated, logged here rather than fixed in this batch.

### A-1 + A-3 — a table over ~100,000 changed rows could never finish syncing, and every pull page rebuilt the tenant's whole id list in PHP
- **Branch:** none — committed directly on `dev` as batch 2 of the 2026-09-28 audit remediation series. Fixed together because the audit's own remediation order requires it: the client half alone leaves the server's per-page cost, the server half alone leaves the cap.
- **A-1 (client).** `pullChanges()` paged a table with `pageOffsets`, a local variable discarded when the function returned, and wrote `_sync_state.last_synced_at` only in the `!tableHasMore && !skippedTables.has(table)` branch. So a round that ended without draining a table — `MAX_PULL_PAGES` (200 × 500 = 100,000 rows), a network failure, the app being closed — threw away every page of progress it had made and the next `sync()` restarted from the same window. `audit_logs` (10–15 rows per POS sale) crosses 100,000 rows in 2–3 months at 100 sales/day, after which a new device can never complete its first sync of that table and every existing device re-downloads the same ~100,000 rows every cycle, forever. Confirmed against the code exactly as the audit described it.
- **A-3 (server).** `applyPullTenantScope()` was called once per table per page and re-resolved the owner, the owned-store list and the staff-id list every time. Its six child-table scopes passed a **Collection** to `whereIn` (`Sale::whereIn('store_id', $storeIds)->pluck('id')`), which executes the parent query and inlines every id the tenant owns as bound literals; `sale_item_batches` nested three such plucks. `fetchPullPage()` used `skip($offset)`, making a full walk of N changed rows cost O(N²/500), and `applyPullCursor()`'s `Schema::hasColumn()` probe ran per table per page uncached.
- Fix, server: the six child scopes now pass a **Builder** (`Sale::query()->select('id')->whereIn('store_id', $storeIds)`), which Laravel compiles into a real SQL subquery, with `sale_item_batches` nested two levels deep via a shared `tenantSaleIds()` helper; a new `resolvePullTenantScope()` resolves owner/`$storeIds`/`$ownedStoreIds`/`$userIds` once per request in `pull()` and hands the result to `applyPullTenantScope()`; the `_synced_at` column probe is memoized in a per-request `$hasSyncedAtColumnCache`; and `fetchPullPage()` seeks by a keyset cursor on `(updated_at, id)` instead of `OFFSET`.
- Fix, client: `_sync_state.server_cursor` (already declared in `SCHEMA_SQL`, now also added to `SYNC_COLUMN_MIGRATIONS` for pre-existing local databases) holds the keyset position reached inside a still-undrained window, written after **every** committed page via `PULL_PROGRESS.savePosition` and cleared by `PULL_PROGRESS.completeWindow` when the window is finally stamped. `MAX_PULL_PAGES` is raised to 1000 and is now a liveness bound rather than a correctness ceiling.
- **Ruling — no breaking change to the pull contract, so no coordinated deployment is needed.** The client sends `page_offset` *and* `page_cursor`; the server prefers the cursor per table and falls back to the offset. An old client against the new server pages exactly as before, and a new client against an old server has its cursor ignored and its offsets honoured — i.e. today's behaviour. `normalizePageCursor()` degrades a malformed cursor to offset paging rather than throwing.
- **Ruling — the window and the position are kept as two separate values, not merged.** Advancing `last_synced_at` to the last applied row's `updated_at` would have needed no new column, but the server's window filter is `_synced_at > X OR updated_at > X`: an offline device pushing a backdated row relies on the `_synced_at` branch, so collapsing the window into a row-ordered position would silently stop delivering late-arriving rows. Splitting them means the window only ever moves when the table is genuinely drained.
- **Ruling — `forceFullResync()` left as-is.** Clearing `_sync_state` clears both columns, which is exactly right; a full resync of a very large table is now simply expected to span several rounds instead of having to complete in one.
- **The `id` tie-break and the cursor's date format are both load-bearing.** `updated_at` has second precision, so a bulk import puts thousands of rows on one timestamp and an `updated_at`-only cursor would loop or skip. And the cursor is reformatted through `$query->getModel()->getDateFormat()` rather than left as ISO8601, because SQLite compares datetimes as text: `'… 10:00:00'` sorts before `'… 10:00:00.000000'`, which would push every row of that second onto the wrong side of the equality branch. Design notes in `docs/SYNC_PULL_PAGINATION.md`.
- Held-back progress is preserved in both existing cases: a page containing a skipped record (pending local edit / UNIQUE collision) stops advancing the *persisted* position while the in-memory one keeps moving (otherwise the round would re-request the same page forever), and `stock_movements` carries its position into the same final transaction as its deferred quantity deltas, for the same reason its window stamp already did.
- Regression coverage: `laravel-server/tests/Feature/SyncPullPaginationTest.php` — `test_child_table_pull_scope_uses_a_subquery_not_a_materialised_id_list` (RED before: the `sale_items` page query was `where "sale_id" in (?, ?, … ×60)`), `test_pull_walks_a_multi_page_table_with_a_keyset_cursor` (RED before: 600 sales sharing one `updated_at`, the second page returned the same 500 rows instead of the remaining 100), plus `test_subquery_scoping_does_not_leak_another_tenants_child_rows` and `test_legacy_page_offset_clients_still_page_correctly`, which were GREEN before and after and pin that the rewrite neither broadened the tenant scope nor broke the old paging contract. Client: `client/__tests__/pull-resumes-across-rounds.test.ts` — `persists each committed page's keyset position and resumes from it on the next round` (RED before: `server_cursor` was `null` after the interrupted round and the next round re-requested the window from the start) and `does not persist progress past a record skipped for a pending local edit`.
- Full suites: server 477 passed / 1534 assertions (473 before, +4 new); client 238 files / 1305 tests (237 / 1303 before, +1 file, +2 tests); `tsc --noEmit` clean. Two existing pull tests' request-payload assertions were updated for the added `page_cursor` key (`pull-paginates-past-500-rows.test.ts`, `pull-full-fetches-categories-suppliers.test.ts`); no test expectation about cursor *behaviour* changed, including the one that pins that `last_synced_at` must not advance when a round never finishes.

### A-2 — every production migration re-seeded over admin-edited platform config and email templates
- **Branch:** none — committed directly on `dev` as batch 1 of the 2026-09-28 audit remediation series.
- Production has no shell access: migrations are applied by hitting `GET /migrate-db?key=…` (`laravel-server/routes/web.php`), which is hard-wired to `Artisan::call('migrate --seed --force')`. So `DatabaseSeeder::run()` executes in full on **every** deploy, not just first install.
- Two of the four seeders it calls wrote unconditionally. `SystemConfigSeeder` called `SystemConfig::setVal()` — an `updateOrCreate` with `value` in the update set (`app/Models/SystemConfig.php`) — for `subscription_plans`, `global_suggestions`, `referral_program` and `smartsupp_key`; `EmailTemplateSeeder` used `EmailTemplate::updateOrCreate` for all four templates. Anything a super-admin had since changed through `PUT /admin/system-configs/{key}` or the email-template admin UI silently reverted to the checked-in defaults, with no notification: plan pricing/limits/feature flags, the referral reward settings, the Smartsupp widget key, `global_suggestions` reset to `[]` (which blanks every client's autocomplete list on its next sync, `sync-engine/index.ts`), and every edited email body.
- Fix: both are now idempotent-if-present, matching what `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder`/`PaymentAccountSeeder` already did with `firstOrCreate` and what the super-admin block in `DatabaseSeeder` already did with an `exists()` guard. `EmailTemplateSeeder` switched `updateOrCreate` → `firstOrCreate`. `SystemConfigSeeder` routes its four keys through a private `seedIfMissing()` that returns early when the key already exists and otherwise delegates to `setVal()` — keeping `setVal()`'s cache invalidation on the create path rather than reimplementing the write.
- **Ruling — the `/migrate-db` route was left alone.** The audit offered "separate `migrate` from `--seed`" as an alternative, not a requirement. Dropping `--seed` from the only deploy path this host has would mean a newly added config key or email template never lands in production at all, and there is no shell to run a one-off seeder from; keeping the seed call but making it non-destructive gives first-install and new-key coverage *and* survivability, which splitting the route does not. A deleted key is still restored on the next deploy — covered by a test.
- Regression coverage: `laravel-server/tests/Feature/SeederIdempotencyTest.php` — `test_a_re_seed_does_not_revert_admin_edited_system_configs` and `test_a_re_seed_does_not_revert_admin_edited_email_templates` (both RED against the pre-fix code: pro pricing reverted `12000 → 8000`, the welcome-email subject reverted to `'Welcome to DumosRx!'`), plus `test_seeders_still_create_missing_rows_on_a_first_install` and `test_a_re_seed_restores_a_key_that_has_been_deleted`, which pin that the guard did not turn the seeders into no-ops. Full server suite 473 passed / 1371 assertions (469 before, +4 new); `client` `tsc --noEmit` clean.

### Comprehensive-review remediation (C1, I1–I4, I6, M2–M4)
- **Branch:** `fix/comprehensive-review-findings`.
- A fresh-context review over the previous day's 70 commits (Roles & Permissions, three procurement UX/perf passes, a whole-app UX/perf pass, a deep-optimization pass, the `audit_logs` sync fix) raised 1 Critical, 6 Important and 4 Minor findings. I5 (the low-stock total-vs-batch fix) was already closed by merging `feature/test-coverage-gaps` before this branch started.

**C1 (Critical) — the `audit_logs` DELETE path had the same coerced-id bug the UPDATE path was fixed for.**
- `SyncController::push()`'s DELETE branch still did a raw `find($change['record_id'])`. `audit_logs` maps to `ActivityLog`, whose primary key is the server's own auto-increment bigint while the client's UUID lives only in `properties->client_id`, so a client id string is coerced to its leading numeric run and can match a completely unrelated row. `ActivityLog` does **not** use `SoftDeletes` (verified), so `$target->delete()` was a genuine **hard delete** of an unrelated audit record, and `authorizeChangeTarget()` only checks store ownership — a same-store decoy sailed straight through. Reproduced: the decoy row was really destroyed.
- **Ruling — rejected outright rather than routed through `findAuditLogByClientId()`.** `audit_logs` is append-only on both sides: `logAction()` (`client/lib/db/core.ts`) only ever queues `INSERT`, and even its repeat-dedup path rewrites the pending INSERT payload in place rather than queueing an UPDATE or DELETE (verified; nothing else queues this table either). So there is no correct row for that lookup to find, and *making* deletion work correctly would hand the sync endpoint a capability to erase the tamper-evidence trail that nothing should have. The change is now failed with `reason: 'unsupported_operation'` and a `Log::warning`, so a malformed or crafted client becomes visible instead of silently destructive. Cost if wrong: a future legitimate need to delete audit rows via sync would have to add an explicit, client-id-matched path — a deliberate act, which is the point.
- Regression coverage: `SyncPushOwnershipTest::test_audit_log_delete_does_not_hard_delete_an_unrelated_row_with_a_matching_numeric_id` (decoy planted at id `87` in the **attacker's own** store, so store-scoping alone cannot be what saves it). RED before, GREEN after.

**I1 (Important) — Roles & Permissions was unreachable on mobile.** `settings-mobile-menu.tsx` still carried `disabled: true, badge: "Soon"` after the desktop nav (`app/(dashboard)/settings/[tab]/settings-tab-nav.tsx`) shipped it, and `settings-client.tsx` renders that menu as the *only* settings navigation below `md` (the sidebar is `hidden md:flex`). Covered by `client/__tests__/settings-mobile-menu-nav-items.test.tsx`, which also pins that the entry stays admin-only.

**I2 (Important) — the purchase-order PDF lazy-load bought nothing.** `purchase-order-details.tsx` statically imported `PurchaseOrderPdf`, which itself statically imports `@react-pdf/renderer`, so the `await import("@react-pdf/renderer")` in its handler was decorative and the Orders route chunk still paid for the dependency. Now defers both together via `Promise.all`, matching `daily-close-actions.tsx`. Verified at the real import-graph level, not just by pattern match: every remaining path to `@react-pdf/renderer` is now dynamic or a worker entry. `heavy-dependency-laziness.test.ts` gained a fourth guard which, unlike the existing three, also checks the **transitive** wrapper import — the direct-package check alone would have passed vacuously here.

**I3 (Important) — `React.memo(CatalogRow)` never bailed out.** All four save handlers depended on the whole `useMutation` result, and react-query v5 returns `{ ...result, mutate, mutateAsync: result.mutate }` — a new object literal every render (verified in the installed source; only `.mutateAsync` is stable, bound once per observer). `onProductUpdated` also arrived as an inline `() => void refetch()`. Both fixed. Covered by `catalog-list-row-callback-identity.test.tsx`, which asserts handler *identity* across a rerender rather than render counts — `pos-product-list-memoization.test.tsx` already establishes that render-count assertions are treated as brittle here.

**I4 (Important) — the product catalog was the last undebounced search.** `genericFuzzySearch` ran straight off the raw term over the whole catalog, with the Tier-4 Levenshtein fallback firing exactly while a user is mid-word, on the same main thread as synchronous sql.js. Now uses `useDebouncedValue` like the other four search surfaces; inputs stay bound to raw state. `isFiltering` moved to the debounced term too, so it stays in lockstep with the `filteredProducts` it gates. Guarded by `search-surfaces-debounced.test.ts`, which covers all five surfaces.
- **Ruling — `lowercaseIndexFor` was NOT wired in.** It is shaped for the POS's snake_case row (`name`/`generic_name`/`barcode`, plus pre-split word arrays) and does not generalize to `genericFuzzySearch`'s arbitrary `searchKeys`; a generic version would need cache keys covering the key set as well as the array. The value is also much reduced now that the search is debounced, and `preFilteredProducts` is a fresh array on every filter change, so the WeakMap would miss precisely when filters move. Cost if wrong: repeated searches at one filter state still re-lowercase — bounded, and now off the keystroke path.

**I6 (Important) — the permission system duplicated AuthContext's own state 26+ times.** `useHasPermission` kept its own `useState`/`useEffect`, its own `getUserPermissionGroup()` query and its own `dumos_sync_completed` listener, so every call site re-ran the synchronous sql.js read independently on each sync cycle and 26+ copies could transiently disagree with each other and with AuthContext's `isAdmin`/`canManageStockBatch`. `AuthContext` now exposes `permissionGroup` and owns the single copy.
- **The trap, and how it was threaded.** An earlier session reverted this exact fix because collapsing the hook to a bare context read turned the documented Critical C2 rules-of-hooks crash (`pos-layout-header.tsx`'s short-circuited `&&` chain) into a silently swallowed warning. Cause: `useContext` occupies no slot in React's hook list, so a conditionally-called context-only hook no longer changes the hook **count** and the invariant never trips. The computation therefore stays inside a **`useMemo`**, which does occupy a slot and keeps the tripwire armed. Verified both directions: `pos-layout-header-rules-of-hooks.test.tsx` still passes (its first case asserts the crash), and collapsing the `useMemo` away was confirmed to make that case fail exactly as it did for the prior session. **Do not "simplify" that `useMemo` into a plain return.**
- Additional coverage: `use-has-permission-single-group-read.test.tsx` — five call sites plus AuthContext now cause **one** group read (six before the fix) and every call site agrees with AuthContext.

**M2 — null store id in `findAuditLogByClientId()`.** `where('store_id', null)` silently becomes `whereNull('store_id')`, which would match legacy store-less rows sharing a client id rather than matching nothing. A super-admin push has no store to scope to and a client id is only unique per device, so there is no safe unscoped match: the helper now returns "not found", leaving an INSERT as an INSERT and an UPDATE as a no-op.

**M3 — `CatalogRow`'s `commitOnBlur={false}` / aria-labels were only guarded at the cell level.** `catalog-row-single-branch.test.tsx` now asserts all three labels and that a blur on a half-typed stock quantity does not commit while an explicit confirm still does. Verified the guard bites by removing the prop.

**M4 — a corrupt `permissions` column granted role defaults instead of denying.** `getUserPermissionGroup` returned `null` for both "no group assigned" and "malformed JSON", so `hasPermission`'s `group?.permissions ?? fallbackPermissions(role)` restored the full role tier for a corrupt row — a silent privilege reversion to pre-feature behaviour.
- **Ruling — fixed; the two cases are distinguishable, so conflating them was never necessary.** `fallbackPermissions`' own doc comment scopes it to the offline/pre-sync gap, where **no row exists yet**; a malformed row is the opposite situation — it did sync, it is simply corrupt — so denying it cannot strand a brand-new device. `getStorePermissionGroups` already degraded the same corruption to "grants nothing", and its comment claimed to match `getUserPermissionGroup`'s guard while the two in fact diverged; they now agree. A `store_owner`/`super_admin` still cannot be locked out, because `hasPermission` short-circuits for those roles before any group is consulted. Cost if wrong: a non-owner with a corrupt row loses access until it re-syncs, instead of silently operating on broad defaults — the safer failure direction, and visible rather than silent. Covered by `get-user-permission-group-malformed-row.test.ts`.

**Deferred, with reasoning:**
- **M1 — no index on `properties->client_id` for `findAuditLogByClientId()`'s `lockForUpdate()`.** The review's "effectively a table lock per push" framing overstates it: `activity_logs.store_id` **is** already indexed (`2026_09_05_000003_add_store_id_to_activity_logs.php`), and it is the only usable index on this two-condition query, so InnoDB examines and locks one store's audit rows rather than the whole table. What remains is a per-store scan, which is worth fixing but not urgent, and correctness is unaffected either way. Fixing it properly means a generated column plus an index, and the expression is driver-specific (`json_unquote(json_extract(...))` on MySQL vs `json_extract(...)` on SQLite) while the tests run on SQLite `:memory:` — so either the query layer branches and the production path goes untested, or the migration branches on driver (an established pattern in this repo) and must use a **VIRTUAL** column, since MySQL's STORED variant forces a table rebuild on what is typically the largest table in the schema and SQLite forbids adding STORED columns via `ALTER TABLE` at all. That is a deploy-risk and measurement question that cannot be answered from an in-memory SQLite suite. Recommended shape when picked up: driver-branched VIRTUAL generated `client_id` column plus an index on `(store_id, client_id)`, keeping the column name identical across drivers so `SyncController` stays single-path.

### fix: audit_logs conflict resurrection loop, and the cross-record overwrite behind it
- **Branch:** `fix/audit-log-sync-conflict-loop`.
- Reported as a permanently non-zero "N unsynced changes" indicator: the same small set of `audit_logs` rows was re-pushed and re-rejected on every app launch, forever.

**1. Client — a terminal conflict never settled the source row.**
- `pushChanges()` treats `version_conflict`/`stale_timestamp` as terminal: it deletes the `_sync_queue` row and relies on "a later pull will bring down the winning server row". `markSynced()` only runs for changes the server ACCEPTED, so the source row kept `_synced = 0` with no queue entry — exactly what `requeueOrphanedRows()` (every boot, over `STORE_SCOPED_TABLES`, which includes `audit_logs`) re-queues.
- For every other table that self-heals, because the pull matches the server's row by the SAME id. `audit_logs` can never self-heal: the server keeps its own auto-increment `activity_logs.id` and stores the client's UUID only in `properties->client_id`, so no pull will ever match. Conflict → dequeue → re-queue on boot → conflict, indefinitely.
- Fix: `markConflictSettled()` (`client/lib/db/base-helpers.ts`) sets `_synced = 1` on the source row at the same point the queue row is dropped, gated on `TERMINAL_CONFLICT_SETTLES_SOURCE_ROW` (`client/lib/db/sync-engine/push.ts`) — currently `audit_logs` only. Other tables keep the existing "leave it for a future pull" semantics deliberately; settling them would discard a real pending edit.
- **`feedback` does NOT need this.** Its server rows are inserted with the client's own id as the primary key (only `audit_logs` skips `$model->id = $recordId` in `SyncController::push`), so a pull reconciles it by id like any other table. It is also absent from `STORE_SCOPED_TABLES`, so boot never re-queues it either way. Left unchanged.

**2. Server — the UPDATE path looked `audit_logs` up by a coerced numeric id.**
- A resubmitted `audit_logs` INSERT is rewritten to an UPDATE by an existence probe that correctly matches on `properties->client_id` scoped to the store — but the UPDATE branch then re-looked the row up with a raw `find($recordId)`, i.e. a client id string against a `bigint` primary key. MySQL coerces it to its leading numeric run (`"87fe24ac-…"` → `87`), so the lookup could land on a completely unrelated `activity_logs` row, and `forceFill()` then overwrote its action/description/properties/user_id with another device's audit data. (`audit_logs` payloads carry no `_version`, so `resolveUpdateConflict()` falls to the timestamp fallback and lets an older decoy row through.) Reproduced end-to-end, not theoretical.
- Fix: `findAuditLogByClientId()` is now the single lookup for this table, used by both the existence probe and the UPDATE branch (with `lockForUpdate()` on the latter, preserving the row-lock semantics of the path it replaces). Every other table keeps its `find()`/`withTrashed()` lookup untouched.

**3. Ruling — `getSyncQueueCount()` keeps counting `audit_logs`.** With (1) a conflicted row settles inside the same sync cycle and boot no longer re-queues it, so there is no lingering window left to paper over. The only remaining ways an `audit_logs` row stays queued are a transient network failure or a `forbidden` rejection — both genuine "not yet synced" states shared with every other table, and the `forbidden` case is precisely how the 2026-09-05 audit-log sync bug was noticed at all. Excluding the table from the count would have hidden it.

- Regression coverage: `client/__tests__/push-settles-conflicted-audit-logs.test.ts` (conflicted `audit_logs` row is settled and a simulated next boot no longer re-queues it; a conflicted `feedback` row is still deliberately left unsynced) and `laravel-server/tests/Feature/SyncPushOwnershipTest.php::test_resubmitted_audit_log_update_does_not_overwrite_an_unrelated_row_with_a_matching_numeric_id` (the unrelated row is untouched and the right row is updated). Both RED against the pre-fix code.

### Whole-client UX + performance pass (U1–U14, P1–P10)
- **Branch:** `feature/whole-app-ux-perf-fixes` (commits `45c0bef9`…, POS / sync indicator / cycle count / catalog / staff / reports / responsive-branch groups).
- Scope was a fresh-context UX + performance audit of `client/` outside procurement (a concurrent branch owned that area). Every finding is either fixed with a test, fixed with a ruling recorded below, or deferred below.

**Design decisions worth knowing before touching these areas again:**
- **A failed read is never an empty list.** `usePOSData`, `useCustomerData` and `product-database` all expose their query's `isError` rather than coercing with `|| []`, and the POS grid, product catalog and customer directory each render a distinct, retryable "couldn't load … on this device" state. When adding a new list surface, thread `isError` through the same way — `|| []` alone silently turns a dead local database into "no results, try a different search term".
- **`useSortableData` reads `accessors` through a ref**, deliberately not through its memo's dependency array, because every call site builds that object inline. The trade-off: changing an accessor alone does not re-sort. Accessors must stay pure projections of a row.
- **An in-progress cycle count is persisted** (`lib/hooks/use-stock-audit-draft.ts`, zustand `persist`, same pattern as the POS cart) and is cleared at exactly the points that clear the POS cart: submit, logout, a different user signing in, and a store switch. Backing out of an edited count confirms first and *keeps* the draft; it is not discarded.
- **The stock-quantity quick-edit in the catalog commits only on an explicit confirm** (`commitOnBlur={false}`), unlike its sibling cells, because its save posts a real inventory adjustment and an immutable `stock_movements` row. Any future cell with consequences beyond itself should opt out of blur-commit too.
- **`sync()`'s "Sync already in progress" is a no-op, not an error** — it is exported as `SYNC_IN_PROGRESS_ERROR` so callers can't drift from the literal. The sync indicator's pending state is driven by the queue count alone; the 30-minute staleness check only escalates the badge's colour.
- **Responsive list branches are conditionally rendered, not CSS-hidden**, wherever one branch is virtualized and the other isn't (POS-adjacent lists, stock movements, customer directory/activity, expenses, activity log, staff activity, suppliers). `hooks/use-media-query.ts` starts at `false` by design (static export), so the mobile branch renders for one frame on desktop — acceptable here, but don't build anything with side effects on that first frame.
- **Only one `SyncIndicator` is mounted at a time.** The mobile header row is gated on a media query rather than `sm:hidden` because each indicator installs its own auto-sync daemon, and two racing daemons made a successful sync report the loser's refusal as a red "Sync Error".

**Deferred, with reasoning:**
- **Virtualizing the *mobile* branches** of the five lists converted in P2. Now that only one branch renders, mobile no longer pays for a hidden desktop render, and each of those card lists needs its own scroll-container/measurement work. Worth doing per-screen, not as one sweep.
- **`React.memo` on `POSCart` is currently inert.** It is applied, and the cart mutators from `usePOSCart` are now stable (`useCallback`), but five handlers it receives (`onCheckout`, `onHoldSale`, `onRequestClearCart`, `onOpenHeldSales`, `onEditPrescription`) are still per-render closures built in `pos-system.tsx`, and `onCheckout` in particular wraps `useFeatureGate`'s `withRestriction`, which is itself recreated every render. Making the memo actually bail out means stabilising `withRestriction` first. The concrete win that *was* taken here is `handleAddToCart` (`useCallback`, with `searchTerm` read through a ref): it is `POSProductCard`'s only per-card prop, so the existing `memo` on hundreds of product tiles now holds across keystrokes.
- **The remaining ~24 `useQuery` call sites with no `isError` branch.** The three highest-traffic list surfaces were done properly; a shallow pass over the rest would add noise without changing what staff actually hit.

## 2026-09-26

### fix: a batch of many sync conflicts crashed the page ("Maximum update depth exceeded")
- **Commit:** `e5be3b1c`
- Direct follow-on to the mass-requeue-on-boot fix below: draining that backlog meant a single push batch could report up to `SYNC_BATCH_SIZE` (50) version conflicts at once. The conflict-reporting loop called `toast.warning()` once per conflicted record with no upper bound — Sonner's `Toaster` does a `flushSync`-driven state update per `toast()` call, and ~25-30 of those fired synchronously in one tick trips React's own "Maximum update depth exceeded" guard, crashing the whole page (reported live via the crash logger, `pushChanges` at the bottom of the stack).
- Past a small threshold (5), the loop now collapses into a single summary toast ("N changes could not be saved...") instead of one per record; below the threshold, unchanged.
- Regression coverage: `client/__tests__/push-limits-conflict-toasts.test.ts` (a batch of 30 conflicts reproduces 30 synchronous `toast.warning()` calls against the pre-fix code; asserts far fewer after). Full suite (904 tests / 169 files) and `tsc --noEmit` clean.

### fix: markSynced() never flagged the source row synced, mass-requeuing a device's entire history every boot
- **Commit:** `b2cd256c`
- Reported as a recurring "A change to Activity Log could not be saved because the record changed since this edit" toast, plus the sync-status indicator showing up to ~15,000 unsynced changes.
- Root cause: `insert()`/`update()` (`client/lib/db/base-helpers.ts`) set `_synced = 0` at write time, but `markSynced()` only ever `DELETE`d the processed `_sync_queue` row — nothing ever flipped the source row's `_synced` back to 1 (`stores`, via a separate pull-path update, was the sole exception). Every already-synced row across the other ~24 tables therefore looked permanently "unsynced with no queue entry" — exactly what `requeueOrphanedRows()` (`client/lib/db/reconcile-identity.ts`, run every app boot from `DatabaseProvider.tsx`) treats as needing a fresh queue entry, mass-requeuing a device's entire synced history on every launch.
- `audit_logs` specifically has no `_version` field in its queued payload (`logAction()` never sets one), so a resubmitted already-accepted INSERT falls to the server's legacy `stale_timestamp` conflict fallback and gets rejected every time — hence the toast loop landing there rather than some other table.
- Almost certainly a long-standing bug, not something the two DB-lock fixes above introduced — it was most likely masked until those fixes made boot/sync reliable enough to run this path to completion instead of crashing first on "Statement closed".
- `markSynced()` now also takes each successfully-pushed change's `{table_name, record_id}` and sets `_synced = 1` on it. `audit_logs` version conflicts are now silenced the same way `feedback`'s already are (both are push-only telemetry the user never edits — a resubmit "conflict" is sync plumbing, not a real edit collision worth a toast).
- Regression coverage: `client/__tests__/push-marks-source-row-synced.test.ts` (asserts `_synced = 1` after a successful push, and that `requeueOrphanedRows()` no longer re-adds the row on a simulated next boot) and `client/__tests__/push-silences-audit-log-conflicts.test.ts` (reproduces the exact toast text against the pre-fix code, then asserts it's gone). Full suite (903 tests / 168 files) and `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Not addressed:** the one-time backlog of already-accumulated `_synced = 0` rows on devices that hit this bug will still get requeued and pushed once more after this fix ships (self-healing from then on, since `_synced` is now correctly maintained) — no migration was written to pre-clear that backlog, so a device with a large existing backlog will see one more (now-silent, for audit_logs) sync burst before it stabilizes.

### fix: writer-promotion db-swap race, and N one-at-a-time saves in the boot-time orphan-row requeue
- **Commit:** `3b6edc15`
- Follow-up to the connection-lock fix below: after that fix shipped, the same "bad parameter or other API misuse" error still surfaced from `requeueOrphanedRows()` (via `DatabaseProvider`'s boot-time call), and the login PIN page started feeling noticeably slower on first load.
- **Root cause 1 (the error):** `rehydrateFromIndexedDb()` — run when a read-only tab is promoted to writer — closed the old `db` handle and swapped in a freshly-read one with zero coordination with the new connection-wide lock. A promotion landing during boot could still invalidate an in-flight `query()`/`execute()`, exactly the race the lock was meant to close everywhere else. It now reserves `reserveDbSlot()` before swapping.
- **Root cause 2 (the hang):** `requeueOrphanedRows()` issued one `execute()` — one lock-acquire-plus-full-`saveDatabase()`-export round trip — per orphaned row, run unconditionally on every app boot. Once every DB operation shares one FIFO lock, that meant the login screen's own user-lookup query had to wait behind however many of those individual round trips were still in flight. Now batches a table's requeue inserts into a single `transaction()` (skipped when already nested inside a caller's own transaction, e.g. `remapForeignKey()`, since nesting `transaction()` deadlocks) — one lock/export cycle per table instead of one per row.
- Regression coverage: `client/__tests__/reconcile-identity.test.ts` (new test asserting one `idb-keyval.set()` call for three orphaned rows in one table, not three). Full suite (900 tests / 166 files) and `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Not covered by a new automated test:** the writer-promotion race itself (`rehydrateFromIndexedDb()` vs. an in-flight query) — reproducing a real multi-tab Web Lock promotion mid-boot in the test environment wasn't attempted; the fix reuses the already-tested `reserveDbSlot()` lock, but this specific interleaving is unverified beyond that.

### fix: query()/execute()/transaction() now serialize onto one connection-wide lock ("Statement closed" / "bad parameter or other API misuse")
- **Commit:** `7f6663db`
- sql.js has a single shared connection with no reader isolation. `query()`'s row-fetch loop yields a tick every 200 rows so a large result set doesn't block paint; a write landing in that window (a `transaction()`'s `BEGIN`, or a bare `execute()`) could invalidate the in-flight prepared statement, throwing "Statement closed" or sql.js's own SQLITE_MISUSE string. A retry-on-error mitigation already existed (`MAX_MISUSE_RETRIES = 2`) but could exhaust under real collision load.
- Reported as two symptoms that turned out to be the same race: the sync engine crash-logging `area: "sync-run"` with an `audit_logs`/`feedback` queue breakdown, and — separately — a fast login sometimes throwing "bad parameter or other API misuse", traced to `DatabaseProvider`'s unawaited boot-time jobs (`requeueOrphanedRows()`'s per-table scan in particular) racing `login()`'s own fire-and-forget `logAction()`/`migrateLegacyPinToHash()` writes.
- `query()` (when called outside an open `transaction()`) now reserves the same connection-wide `reserveDbSlot()` lock `transaction()` already used, for its whole duration including every yield — not just retrying after the fact. `execute()` reserves it too when called outside a transaction. A call already inside an open `transaction()` skips reserving its own slot (the enclosing transaction holds it; reserving again would deadlock). The prior retry logic is kept as defense-in-depth.
- Regression coverage: `client/__tests__/query-torn-read-retry.test.ts` (updated — a write attempted mid-read now queues behind it instead of tearing it, `prepares === 1`) and the existing `client/__tests__/query-retries-on-statement-closed.test.ts`. Full suite (899 tests / 166 files) and `tsc --noEmit` both clean.
- See `docs/DATABASE_CONCURRENCY.md` §2.3/§2.4 for the updated mechanism writeup.

### Storefront pass (`docs/STOREFRONT_REVIEW.md`) — 15 of its 16 findings closed

All of `SF-P0-1`, `SF-P1-1`, `SF-P1-3`, `SF-P2-1`…`SF-P2-6` and `SF-P3-1`…`SF-P3-6`. **`SF-P1-2` (wiring the built-and-tested Paystack flow into the storefront UI) was deliberately left open** — enabling real online payment collection is a business decision, not a routine bug fix, and was explicitly deferred to its own round. Nothing below makes Paystack reachable from a client; `checkout-form.tsx` still offers only `in_store`/`transfer`. **It was closed the same day**, once the subaccount payment design that deferral was waiting on shipped — see the entry immediately below.

#### fix: storefront online payment is wired up end-to-end via per-store Paystack subaccounts (SF-P1-2)
- **What it was.** `checkout-form.tsx` offered only `in_store`/`transfer`; the entire two-step Paystack payment-intent flow (`initializeCheckout()`, `StorefrontPaymentIntent`, its dedicated limiter, 14 of the 29 storefront tests) was built and tested server-side but unreachable from any client, deliberately deferred earlier the same day because wiring it up as-is would have meant every customer payment landing in DumosRx's own Paystack account with no ledger, no payout mechanism and no way to get it to the store owner — a custodial-money problem, not a UI gap. Its two named prerequisites were a real refund path (`SF-P2-4`'s remaining half) and an answer to "whose money is it, and how does DumosRx get paid?"
- **The money question was answered with direct-to-store Paystack subaccounts**, not platform-collect-then-payout: each store gets its own Paystack subaccount (created programmatically from the owner's bank details — the owner never touches Paystack directly, no "plug and play" burden), and a checkout charge is split automatically at the point of payment, so the store's share settles straight to their own bank account and DumosRx never holds it. Full design and rationale, including the two rejected alternatives and why Cameroon is explicitly out of scope (Paystack doesn't reach it — that's a Flutterwave/separate-project question): `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-storefront-paystack-subaccounts-design.md`.
- **What shipped, in build order** (see `git log` for the full 13-commit sequence): new `stores` columns (`paystack_subaccount_code`, `paystack_subaccount_country`, `paystack_bank_code`, `paystack_account_number_last4`, `paystack_fee_dirty_at`) on both sides per the usual synced-column convention; `PaystackSubaccountService` (`listBanks`/`resolveAccount`/`createSubaccount`/`updateSubaccountFee`/`refundTransaction`, `Http::fake()`-tested against success and Paystack-error responses); `PaymentService` gained an optional subaccount/currency parameter on `initializeTransaction()` and a provider-agnostic `refundTransaction()` delegate; store-owner endpoints (`GET /store/payment-banks`, `POST /store/payment-account/resolve`, `POST /store/payment-account`) that re-resolve the account server-side even after the client already called resolve, are idempotent (409 once connected), and persist only the masked last 4 digits; a superadmin-editable `storefront_platform_fee_percentage` `SystemConfig` value that dirties every connected subaccount on change; a new `SyncSubaccountFeeRates` scheduled command (same cadence as `RebuildStorefrontIfDirty`) that propagates a changed rate to every dirty subaccount, clearing the flag on success and alerting via `AdminAlertService` after repeated failures; `StorefrontController` gating the Paystack checkout option on a connected subaccount and pricing in the store's own currency; a real `PaymentService::refundTransaction()` call replacing `OnlineOrderController::flagRefundRequired()`'s log-and-notify stub (falls back to the old log-and-notify behaviour only if the provider call itself fails); a new "Online Payments" settings panel in `client/` for the owner to connect a subaccount; a third `paystack` radio in `web/`'s `checkout-form.tsx` that calls `/checkout/initialize` and redirects to Paystack; automatic order confirmation on return (`?reference=`/`?trxref=` read via `sessionStorage`, not a URL round-trip through the server); and a superadmin settings card for the platform commission in `web/app/admin`.
- **`percentage_charge` is the platform's cut, not the store's** — a real, easy-to-get-backwards fact about Paystack's subaccount API worth stating plainly, since `createSubaccount()`/`updateSubaccountFee()` pass it straight through.
- **Refund cost, accepted as a v1 tradeoff, not fixed:** Paystack draws a refund on an already-settled split transaction from DumosRx's own balance, not clawed back from the store. Confirmed accepted with the user; no Transfer/Transfer-Recipient claw-back was built. Revisit only if refund volume makes it worth automating.
- **`web/` gained its own vitest test infrastructure** as part of this work (mirroring `client/`'s), since it previously had no test runner at all — new tests for the checkout radio, the return-handling flow, and the admin fee card all run under it.
- **Verified:** `laravel-server` — 435 tests passing (up from 399 before this plan). `client` — 897 tests / 166 files passing (up from 894), clean `npx tsc --noEmit`. `web` — 5 tests / 3 files passing (new; `web/` had none before), clean `npx tsc --noEmit`. **Not verified:** a real sandbox Paystack checkout click-through against a running local stack (`php artisan serve` + `npm run dev` in both `client/` and `web/`, a live Paystack test-mode account, a real browser session) — the repo's local `.env` only has placeholder Paystack keys (`PAYSTACK_SECRET_KEY=sk_test_xxxx`), so this remains an explicit, named gap rather than something claimed as done. Whoever picks this up next should run that click-through — connect a subaccount with one of Paystack's documented test bank numbers, place a real sandbox charge, confirm settlement — before treating the flow as production-verified end to end.

#### fix: final whole-branch review of the Paystack subaccount work — currency, settlement-column sync hole, and four silent gaps
- **Every non-NGN store's checkout was charged and then rejected.** `initializeCheckout()` charged in `stores.currency` but stamped the payment intent with, and verified against, the global `config('payment.currency')` — so a Ghana/Kenya/SA store's customer paid successfully and then got a 422 with no order created and no refund path (the refund hangs off order cancellation, and there was no order). All three places now come from one `storeCurrency()` helper, and verify compares against the intent's own stamped currency, the same per-record shape `PaymentController`'s subscription webhook already used. New `StorefrontPaystackLifecycleTest` fakes Paystack at the HTTP layer (rather than mocking `PaymentService`, as every per-task test did — which is how this shipped unnoticed) and walks a KES store through connect → initialize → confirm → cancel → refund; it fails against the pre-fix code.
- **Any staff device could redirect a store's settlement.** `SyncController::push()` applies a `stores` payload with `forceFill()`, which bypasses `$fillable`, and the ownership check admits staff as well as the owner — so a pushed `paystack_subaccount_code` would silently repoint every future storefront sale, bypassing the onboarding endpoint's resolution, its 409 idempotency guard and its last-4-only rule. All five `paystack_*` columns are now stripped in `normalizePushPayload()` (`STORE_SYNC_FORBIDDEN_FIELDS`, mirroring the existing `users` strip). Coverage: `SyncStoresPaystackFieldGuardTest`.
- **"We can't verify this country" and "this account number is wrong" were the same branch**, so a typo'd Nigerian account could be waved through the escape hatch meant for Rwanda/Kenya. `PaystackSubaccountService` now declares `RESOLVE_COUNTRIES` (Nigeria, Ghana — what Paystack's `/bank/resolve` actually covers) and exposes it; the resolve endpoint reports `verifiable`, the create endpoint refuses `confirmed_unverifiable` for a verifiable country, and the settings panel only offers the override when the server says the country can't be verified at all.
- **A paid order that became unfulfillable kept the money.** Nothing is reserved at initialize time, so stock selling out (or a product being deactivated) during the customer's detour to Paystack's hosted page failed the confirm call with a bare stock error on an already-charged customer. `checkout()` now verifies and refunds that payment, marks the intent `refunded`, and returns a 422 saying so. An unpaid/failed reference is never refunded and stays retryable.
- **Two more silent gaps closed:** a customer returning from Paystack with no `sessionStorage` entry (new tab, new session, cleared storage) got an ordinary empty-cart page with no hint they had just paid — now a dedicated panel quoting the reference; and a store that already had a subaccount was still shown the empty onboarding form, with the bank code and masked last 4 it syncs down for display shown nowhere — now a read-only "Connected" state.
- **A refunded order no longer reads `paid`.** `online_orders.payment_status` gained a `'refunded'` enum value (migration `2026_09_26_000003`; the table is API-only, so there is no client-schema half) and `refundOrFlag()` sets it on a successful refund — a failed refund deliberately stays `paid`, because the money is still owed. Anything summing paid online orders as revenue must treat `'refunded'` as not-revenue.
- **Also in this pass:** the three payment-account endpoints moved out of `Api/Web/StoreController` (394 lines, over the 350-line guideline) into `Api/Web/StorePaymentAccountController`, and the plan's over-length inline comment blocks were trimmed to the 2-line convention with their rationale moved into the relevant `AGENTS.md`.
- **Verified:** `laravel-server` 447 tests passing (up from 435), `client` 899 / 166 files + clean `npx tsc --noEmit`, `web` 6 / 4 files + clean `npx tsc --noEmit`. The manual sandbox Paystack click-through named in the entry above is **still** not done and is still the open verification gap.

#### fix: a transient `/storefront-slugs` failure during any `web/` deploy deleted every live storefront from production (SF-P0-1, SF-P1-1)
- `getStorefrontSlugs()` swallowed every failure (non-200, non-JSON, network throw) and returned the single `demo` placeholder, so `npm run build` **exited 0** having emitted one page and `FTP-Deploy-Action`'s sync — which deletes server files absent locally — removed every real `/store/<slug>/` directory. Nothing re-triggers a rebuild, so the paid `store_url` feature stayed dark indefinitely behind a green checkmark. `getStorefrontData()` had the same shape one scope down: any non-OK response became `notFound()`, publishing a rendered 404 page at that store's path while the build still succeeded.
- `getStorefrontSlugs()` now **throws** on a fetch/parse failure, with `STOREFRONT_ALLOW_EMPTY=1` as the explicit escape hatch for offline/sandbox builds (the only legitimate consumer of the `demo` fallback). A genuinely empty list — 200 with `{"slugs":[]}`, i.e. no store has an online store yet — is still a legitimate answer and still falls back to `demo`: a demo-only sync has nothing real to delete in that state.
- The per-store fetch moved to a new `web/lib/api/storefront-data.ts` shared by both `[store_slug]` routes, which now distinguishes the two cases: **404/403** (store gone, suspended, plan lapsed) returns `null` → `notFound()` as before; **5xx, network failure or non-JSON** throws `StorefrontFetchError`, failing the build for that page so the last good deploy stays live.
- Belt to those braces: `web/scripts/verify-storefront-output.mjs` (`npm run verify:storefront-output`) re-asks the API for the slug list and asserts every slug actually landed in `web/out/store/<slug>/index.html`, wired into `deploy-web.yml` as a step **before** the FTP sync so a lost storefront aborts the deploy instead of propagating. It honours the same `STOREFRONT_ALLOW_EMPTY=1` skip.
- Verified against a local stub API (the `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL` trick documented in `web/AGENTS.md`): a healthy build emits both storefronts with correct metadata and exits 0; a 500 on `/storefront-slugs` fails the build with the new message; a 500 on one store's `/storefront/<slug>` fails the build (exit 1) naming that store; `STOREFRONT_ALLOW_EMPTY=1` with a dead API still builds `demo` only; and the guard script passes, then fails with a non-zero exit when a storefront directory is removed by hand. **Note for future debugging:** Next's persistent `.next/cache` fetch cache can mask a newly-failing storefront fetch across successive local builds — `rm -rf .next` before trying to reproduce one. CI checks out fresh, so this doesn't affect the real pipeline.

#### fix: no rate limit on the public storefront reads or on order placement (SF-P1-3)
- Only `POST /storefront/{slug}/checkout/initialize` was throttled. `GET /storefront-slugs`, `GET /storefront/{slug}` and the unauthenticated `POST /storefront/{slug}/checkout` had **no limiter at all** — Laravel 11 removed `throttle:api` from the default `api` group and `bootstrap/app.php` never calls `throttleApi()`.
